# Security Now! podcast — episode guide

Weekly security podcast by Steve Gibson and Leo Laporte (see Security Now!). This wiki covers 690 ingested episodes, #400–#1104, from 2013-04-17 to 2026-11-10. Each episode has a Source page; recurring storylines are collected as timeline pages under Syntheses.

# Storylines

* [.zip and .mov domain risks](../../syntheses/dot-zip-tld-risks.md) - Phishing risk from Google's new TLDs.
* [1Password metadata design](../../syntheses/1password-metadata-design.md) - Unencrypted vault metadata and the company's response.
* [Abusive browser notification prompts](../../syntheses/browser-notification-abuse.md) - Sites coercing notification permission and browser countermeasures.
* [Ad blocking and anti-adblock](../../syntheses/adblocking-and-anti-adblock.md) - iOS blockers, network blocking and sites fighting back.
* [Adobe breach of 130 million accounts](../../syntheses/adobe-breach-2013.md) - Weak password encryption and fallout.
* [Adobe Flash zero-days and decline (2015)](../../syntheses/flash-zero-days-2015.md) - Series of Flash exploits and platform moves against the plugin.
* [Ads, tracking and blocking debate](../../syntheses/ad-tracking-and-blocking-debate.md) - Ethics and technology of blocking.
* [Adups firmware spyware](../../syntheses/adups-firmware-spyware.md) - Spying firmware on low-cost Android phones.
* [Age verification laws and breaches](../../syntheses/age-verification-laws.md) - Global wave of age-gating laws and Steve's push for privacy-preserving assertions.
* [AI agents and AI supply-chain risk](../../syntheses/ai-agents-and-ai-supply-chain-risks.md) - OpenClaw and model-hub malware.
* [AI and the decline of bug bounties](../../syntheses/bug-bounty-decline.md) - AI slop and volume undermine bounties.
* [AI coding and Claude Code](../../syntheses/ai-coding-claude-code.md) - Growing use of agentic coding tools and the skepticism around them.
* [AI crawlers and the web economy](../../syntheses/ai-crawlers-web-economics.md) - Scrapers, robots.txt, AI summaries and copyright litigation.
* [AI hype, control and limits](../../syntheses/ai-limits-and-control.md) - Episodes weighing AI capability, controllability and what the models actually do.
* [AI in bug hunting and coding](../../syntheses/ai-in-vulnerability-research-and-coding.md) - Where AI helped find bugs and where AI-written code papered over problems.
* [AI in vulnerability research](../../syntheses/ai-vulnerability-research-timeline.md) - AI finding bugs.
* [AI jailbreaking and guardrails](../../syntheses/ai-jailbreaking-and-guardrails.md) - Recurring discovery of ways to bypass LLM safety measures.
* [AI risks and AI policy](../../syntheses/ai-threats-and-ai-policy.md) - GenAI worms, AI regulation and AI-generated code debates.
* [AI vulnerability discovery and Claude Mythos](../../syntheses/ai-vulnerability-finding-and-mythos.md) - Mythos, Fable and rival systems finding and exploiting vulnerabilities at scale.
* [AI-enabled cyberattacks](../../syntheses/ai-enabled-cyberattacks.md) - Real-world offensive use of AI.
* [Albania and Iran cyberattack](../../syntheses/albania-iran-cyberattack.md) - State-backed attack on Albanian government networks.
* [AMD flaws and CTS Labs](../../syntheses/amd-cts-labs-disclosure.md) - Controversial disclosure of Ryzen/EPYC chipset flaws.
* [Android malware and platform hardening](../../syntheses/android-malware-and-platform-hardening.md) - Android patch rules, accessibility abuse and Google Play permission tightening.

# Episodes

* [SN 0400 - VPN Solutions](../../sources/sn-0400.md) (2013-04-17) - Episode 400 covers Oracle's 42-fix Java update with signed-applet prompts, a WordPress brute-force botnet, the PlaneSploit aircraft-hacking claims, and a refresher on VPNs and OpenVPN.
* [SN 0401 - Listener Feedback #166](../../sources/sn-0401.md) (2013-04-24) - Episode 401 covers a new Java sandbox bypass, Google Play apps that turned malicious after an update, standards-based one-time passwords spreading, the 1Password design flaw, and PPTP weaknesses, plus listener questions.
* [SN 0402 - BitTorrent Sync](../../sources/sn-0402.md) (2013-05-01) - Episode 402 covers the Syrian Electronic Army Twitter hacks, the LivingSocial breach, the FBI's wiretap and spyware push, the broken EV indicator in Internet Explorer, and a first look at BitTorrent Sync.
* [SN 0403 - Listener Feedback #167](../../sources/sn-0403.md) (2013-05-08) - Episode 403 covers the overhyped quantum Internet story, an IE8 zero-day used in watering-hole attacks, Carrier Grade NAT at BT, GRC going HTTPS-only with HSTS, and listener questions on stealth ports, Thunderbolt DMA, OpenVPN and authenticators.
* [SN 0404 - EFF: How Facebook Monetizes](../../sources/sn-0404.md) (2013-05-15) - Episode 404 covers May 2013 Patch Tuesday, Microsoft following links shared in Skype chats, The New Yorker's Tor-based StrongBox, DHS seizing Mt. Gox funds, and an EFF explanation of how Facebook uses data brokers for targeted ads.
* [SN 0405 - Listener Feedback #168](../../sources/sn-0405.md) (2013-05-22) - Episode 405 covers Firefox 22's planned third-party cookie block, Microsoft reading Skype links, contactless card mis-charges, SSL interception boxes, carrier-grade NAT logging, whitelisting versus patching, and Twitter's new two-step login.
* [SN 0406 - Chatting Off the Record With OTR](../../sources/sn-0406.md) (2013-05-29) - Episode 406 explains the Off the Record (OTR) messaging protocol behind Cryptocat, after covering the Ars Technica password-cracking article, Amazon's OAuth login, and Google's 2048-bit certificate migration.
* [SN 0407 - Listener Feedback #169](../../sources/sn-0407.md) (2013-06-05) - Episode 407 covers mysterious keyless car-door openings, the Zeus banking trojan spreading on Facebook, the Oracle v. Google API copyright appeal, a health site that rejected duplicate passwords, and listener questions on VPNs, HTTPS, third-party cookies and BitTorrent Sync.
* [SN 0408 - The State of Surveillance](../../sources/sn-0408.md) (2013-06-12) - Episode 408 reacts to the first Snowden/PRISM revelations, covering telephony metadata collection, Clapper's testimony to Congress, and Steve's theory that PRISM is an upstream fiber-optic tap like the AT&T Room 641A splitter.
* [SN 0409 - Listener Feedback #170](../../sources/sn-0409.md) (2013-06-19) - Episode 409 follows up on Steve's PRISM theory, covering the Narus traffic-analysis link, surging demand for encrypted messaging, a Java critical patch, Steve's plan to make SpinRite run on Macs for free, and listener questions on DNS settings, SSL and the NSA, iMessage and TextSecure.
* [SN 0410 - Interesting & Useful Intel History](../../sources/sn-0410.md) (2013-06-26) - Episode 410 covers UK fiber tapping, the NSA treating encryption as suspicious, Firefox 22 with WebRTC and asm.js, Project Chess at Skype, and a technical history of Intel real and protected mode that lets SpinRite 6.1 use huge buffers.
* [SN 0411 - Listener Feedback #171](../../sources/sn-0411.md) (2013-07-03) - Episode 411 covers upstream fiber tapping confirmed by NSA slides, Facebook and Google still on 1024-bit RSA keys, side-channel eavesdropping on EU faxes, Firefox 23 removing the JavaScript switch, Google's QUIC protocol, and listener questions on NSA storage math and certificates.
* [SN 0412 - SSL & Perfect Forward Secrecy](../../sources/sn-0412.md) (2013-07-10) - Episode 412 explains why RSA key exchange lets archived SSL traffic be decrypted later if a server's private key is obtained, and how ephemeral Diffie-Hellman cipher suites give perfect forward secrecy, after covering a TrueType font patch, the Cryptocat group-chat flaw and a Dropbox two-factor bypass.
* [SN 0413 - How Much Tinfoil?](../../sources/sn-0413.md) (2013-07-17) - Episode 413 covers new Guardian documents on Microsoft's cooperation with the NSA and Microsoft's denials, Clapper's retraction, bundled adware installers, Adblock Plus acceptable ads, Lavabit, and a discussion of how much privacy protection is enough using pre-Internet encryption.
* [SN 0414 - Inflection Points](../../sources/sn-0414.md) (2013-07-24) - Episode 414 covers government pressure on web firms for SSL master keys, the SIM card DES vulnerability, UK default porn filtering, Google Drive encryption tests, and the legal fallout of the Snowden revelations.
* [SN 0415 - Listener Feedback #172](../../sources/sn-0415.md) (2013-07-31) - Episode 415 covers the XKeyscore disclosures, the Megamos car-key crypto court injunction, the SkyDrive trademark loss, the HackRF software radio, and listener Q&A on Lavabit, VPN versus Tor, PGP and third-party cookies.
* [SN 0416 - Black Hat 2013, Tor & More](../../sources/sn-0416.md) (2013-08-07) - Episode 416 covers the FBI-style JavaScript exploit against Tor Browser users of Freedom Hosting, the BREACH compression attack from Black Hat, Twitter's new two-factor scheme, Firefox 23 changes, Chrome's visible saved passwords, and RSA factoring worries.
* [SN 0417 - Listener Feedback #173](../../sources/sn-0417.md) (2013-08-14) - Episode 417 covers the shutdown of Lavabit and Silent Mail, the suppressed FISA court opinion, the Android random number flaw that exposed Bitcoin wallets, XP exploit stockpiling, August Patch Tuesday, and Q&A on CryptoLink, SMTP TLS and Mailvelope.
* [SN 0418 - Considering PGP](../../sources/sn-0418.md) (2013-08-21) - Episode 418 opens year nine with Snowden fallout (Lavabit threats, the Guardian hard-drive destruction, the detention of David Miranda, Groklaw closing) and explains how PGP and OpenPGP work, including ASCII armor and the web of trust.
* [SN 0419 - Listener Feedback #174](../../sources/sn-0419.md) (2013-08-28) - Episode 419 surveys the rush of secure-messaging offerings after Lavabit, HashCat's removal of its 15-character limit, the NSS key logging feature, Yahoo's recycling of dormant email addresses, flash drive wear leveling, ETag tracking, and listener Q&A on PGP and traffic analysis.
* [SN 0420 - Bitmessage](../../sources/sn-0420.md) (2013-09-04) - Episode 420 explains the Bitmessage peer-to-peer messaging system and its weaknesses, and covers international reaction to the NSA leaks, the TrueCrypt password found on David Miranda, an Apple font-rendering crash bug, New Zealand's software patent ban, and Java's forgeable warning dialogs.
* [SN 0421 - The Perfect Accusation](../../sources/sn-0421.md) (2013-09-11) - Episode 421 puts the Bullrun/SIGINT Enabling revelations in perspective, explaining the Dual_EC_DRBG random number generator controversy, and also covers Patch Tuesday, Yahoo's FISA court suit, LastPass's statement, the iPhone 5S fingerprint sensor and the end of myOpenID.
* [SN 0422 - Listener Feedback #175](../../sources/sn-0422.md) (2013-09-18) - Episode 422, hosted with Tom Merritt, covers an Internet Explorer zero-day, Glendale schools monitoring student social media, unpatched Java 6 attacks, doubts about IPsec and standards, elliptic curve constants, Touch ID speculation, and mixing entropy sources.
* [SN 0423 - Fingerprint Biometrics](../../sources/sn-0423.md) (2013-09-25) - Episode 423, hosted with Tom Merritt, covers RSA making the suspect Dual_EC_DRBG its default in BSAFE, iOS 7 lock screen bypasses, Google holding Wi-Fi passwords, and a close look at Apple Touch ID being spoofed.
* [SN 0424 - SQRL](../../sources/sn-0424.md) (2013-10-02) - Episode 424, hosted with Tom Merritt, is the first public presentation of SQRL (Secure QR Login), Steve's proposal for replacing website usernames and passwords with per-site keys derived from a master identity, plus short news on fingerprints as usernames and IE6 market share.
* [SN 0425 - SQRL Q&A #176](../../sources/sn-0425.md) (2013-10-09) - Episode 425 follows up SQRL with listener questions on usability, domain binding, phishing and collisions, and covers the Lavabit court battle over SSL keys, a large October Patch Tuesday, and early community adoption of SQRL.
* [SN 0426 - SQRL: Anti-Phishing & Revocation](../../sources/sn-0426.md) (2013-10-16) - Episode 426 covers a 51-flaw Java 7 update, internet governance bodies breaking from US oversight, Lavabit's revoked certificate, NSA contact-list harvesting and FOXACID, the TrueCrypt audit fundraiser, a D-Link router backdoor, and two SQRL upgrades (previous-key revocation and IP-matching anti-phishing).
* [SN 0427 - A Newsy Week!](../../sources/sn-0427.md) (2013-10-23) - Episode 427 is a news potpourri covering CryptoLocker ransomware, Google Project Shield, the CryptoSeal VPN shutdown, BlackBerry Messenger and Threema, Mozilla's Shumway Flash renderer, the Apple touch patent ruling and a planned SQRL identity-lock protocol.
* [SN 0428 - Listener Feedback #177](../../sources/sn-0428.md) (2013-10-30) - Q&A episode centred on CryptoLocker follow-ups, plus LinkedIn Intro, the NSA MUSCULAR tap on Google's internal links, Firefox 25, Threema and SQRL's Identity Lock.
* [SN 0429 - "Monkey" Was 26th!](../../sources/sn-0429.md) (2013-11-06) - News-only episode on the Adobe breach of 130 million accounts and its ECB-encrypted passwords, a TIFF zero-day in Office, the badBIOS claims, CryptoLocker escalation, the TrueCrypt audit and Google's reaction to the NSA tap.
* [SN 0430 - Listener Feedback #178](../../sources/sn-0430.md) (2013-11-13) - Patch Tuesday and the spreading Office TIFF zero-day, bitcoin exchange thefts, Backblaze drive-failure data, why 256-bit identifiers cannot realistically collide, the Knock app's weak implementation and iCloud Keychain.
* [SN 0431 - What Is RADIUS?](../../sources/sn-0431.md) (2013-11-20) - News on GRC's move to perfect forward secrecy, Google's 2048-bit and PFS completion, CryptoLocker's scale, baseband processor insecurity, HTTP/2 and opportunistic encryption, a GCC optimiser flaw and two new payment gadgets, then an explainer on the RADIUS authentication protocol.
* [SN 0432 - Coin, CryptoLocker, Patent Trolls & More](../../sources/sn-0432.md) (2013-11-27) - Corrected analysis of how the Coin card works, BGP traffic-diversion claims, Twitter and Microsoft hardening, the TQP v. Newegg patent-troll verdict, the CryptoLocker file format and GRC's malware samples page, and the Safeplug Tor appliance.
* [SN 0433 - BULLRUN: Breaking SSL](../../sources/sn-0433.md) (2013-12-04) - Steve walks through Matthew Green's brainstorm on how the NSA's BULLRUN programme might undermine SSL, after news on the nuclear launch code story, acoustic air-gap networking, a car-stopping EMP device and NASA warp-drive research.
* [SN 0434 - Listener Feedback #179](../../sources/sn-0434.md) (2013-12-11) - December Patch Tuesday and Adobe zero-days, NSA use of Google tracking cookies, the Pony botnet credential haul, FreeBSD distrusting hardware random generators, ANSSI's abused intermediate certificate, Touch ID finger fade, and SSD error rates in SpinRite.
* [SN 0435 - Listener Feedback #180](../../sources/sn-0435.md) (2013-12-18) - NSA fallout (60 Minutes, court ruling, tech-executive meeting, Snowden's profile), bot traffic statistics, Gmail image proxying, the acoustic RSA key extraction attack, Microsoft joining FIDO versus SQRL, and listener Q&A on SpinRite in VirtualBox, collisions, NAT and pointers.
* [SN 0437 - New Year's News Catch-Up](../../sources/sn-0437.md) (2014-01-07) - First 2014 episode covering the report that the NSA paid RSA 10 million dollars to make Dual_EC_DRBG the BSAFE default, the leaked ANT exploit catalogue, webcam LED bypass, the mysterious router port 32764, CryptoLocker copycats and the Snapchat phone-number leak.
* [SN 0438 - NSA's ANT: What We've Learned](../../sources/sn-0438.md) (2014-01-14) - A walk through the leaked NSA ANT catalogue of hardware and firmware implants, plus news on the Target breach, NTP monlist amplification DDoS attacks, the port 32764 router backdoor command set, the RSA conference boycott and the net-neutrality ruling.
* [SN 0439 - Listener Feedback #181](../../sources/sn-0439.md) (2014-01-21) - Reaction to the President's NSA speech, how the BlackPOS malware hit Target, CryptoLocker's estimated 40 million dollar haul, Steve's Scrypt-based key derivation for SQRL, plus listener questions on VPN anonymity, port 32764, TrueCrypt, XP end of support and SSL Labs.
* [SN 0440 - Listener Feedback #182](../../sources/sn-0440.md) (2014-01-28) - More point-of-sale breaches, Steve's Touch ID over-training trick, new CryptoLocker samples, the government panel's verdict on phone metadata, Facebook's Android permissions, Bluetooth Low Energy pairing weaknesses and Q&A on XP, MailStore and SpinRite versions.
* [SN 0441 - Password Policies](../../sources/sn-0441.md) (2014-02-04) - Analysis of Dashlane's survey of the login password policies of the top 100 online retailers, plus news on a Flash zero-day, malicious ads, the Chewbacca point-of-sale botnet, coin-toss bias, Windows Firewall Control and Firefox 27 with TLS 1.2.
* [SN 0442 - Listener Feedback #183](../../sources/sn-0442.md) (2014-02-11) - Q&A episode following the password-policy show, with Patch Tuesday, Comcast home-router hotspots, the Target breach vendor entry point, and password lockout and echo debates.
* [SN 0443 - Sisyphus](../../sources/sn-0443.md) (2014-02-18) - An all-news episode on record NTP-reflection DDoS, the Linksys Moon worm, Belkin WeMo and ASUS router flaws, Kickstarter's breach, Bitcoin transaction malleability and apps that skip SSL certificate validation.
* [SN 0444 - Goto: Fail](../../sources/sn-0444.md) (2014-02-25) - Apple's goto fail SSL/TLS verification bug in iOS and OS X, the Telegram crypto critique after Facebook's WhatsApp deal, Netflix peering disputes, Mt. Gox's collapse and the value of running without admin rights.
* [SN 0445 - Listener Feedback #184](../../sources/sn-0445.md) (2014-03-04) - Q&A episode covering Bruce Schneier on goto fail, the GnuTLS certificate-verification bug, GCHQ's Optic Nerve webcam collection, Mt. Gox bankruptcy and Flexcoin theft, plus Windows XP end-of-support advice.
* [SN 0446 - iOS Security](../../sources/sn-0446.md) (2014-03-11) - Part one of a deep dive into Apple's revised iOS Security white paper (hardware AES, secure boot, per-device update signing, Secure Enclave and Touch ID), plus Snowden's SXSW appearance, the Foxit installer complaints and Windows XP survival advice.
* [SN 0447 - iOS Security Part 2](../../sources/sn-0447.md) (2014-03-18) - Pwn2Own 2014 felling every browser, a weak early random-number generator found in iOS 7, five steps for running Windows XP past April 2014, cheaper cloud storage, and the second half of the iOS Security white paper on data protection classes and passcodes.
* [SN 0448 - iOS Security Part 3](../../sources/sn-0448.md) (2014-03-25) - A Word and Outlook RTF zero-day with an EMET workaround, debunking the "WPA2 cracked" and "text an ATM for cash" headlines, Gmail forcing HTTPS, the NSA's Huawei intrusion, and the end of the iOS security series on AirDrop, iMessage, Siri and iCloud Keychain.
* [SN 0449 - Listener Feedback #185](../../sources/sn-0449.md) (2014-04-01) - Q&A episode centered on the academic study of the NSA's Dual EC random generator in RSA BSAFE and TLS "Extended Random", with listener questions on certificate authorities, DNS blocking in Turkey, crypto libraries, iMessage versus Threema and Windows XP.
* [SN 0450 - How the Heartbleeds](../../sources/sn-0450.md) (2014-04-08) - The final Windows XP Patch Tuesday and the discovery of the OpenSSL Heartbleed bug in the TLS heartbeat extension, with advice on certificate revocation, server fixes and checking revocation in browsers.
* [SN 0451 - TrueCrypt & Heartbleed Part 2](../../sources/sn-0451.md) (2014-04-15) - The first-phase TrueCrypt audit results (bootloader and Windows driver), the Heartbleed discovery timeline and the missing certificate-revocation tsunami, the CloudFlare challenge, the Bloomberg NSA claim, and an Android launcher-icon hijacking proof of concept.
* [SN 0452 - Listener Feedback #186](../../sources/sn-0452.md) (2014-04-22) - Q&A on Heartbleed follow-ups, the Lavabit appeal loss, a second hidden Ethernet backdoor in consumer routers, Google exploring PGP in Gmail, the LibreSSL fork of OpenSSL, large Apple OS X security updates and why jailbreaks exist.
* [SN 0453 - Certificate Revocation Part 1](../../sources/sn-0453.md) (2014-04-29) - An unpatched Internet Explorer VML zero-day with a workaround, the Core Infrastructure Initiative funding OpenSSL, and a first-principles explainer of PKI, CRLs, OCSP, OCSP stapling and Must-Staple.
* [SN 0454 - Certificate Revocation Part 2](../../sources/sn-0454.md) (2014-05-06) - Microsoft's out-of-cycle IE zero-day fix for Windows XP, OAuth Covert Redirect, an iOS 7.1 email attachment encryption gap, the US NSTIC internet identity pilot, and why certificate revocation checking is hard and inconsistent across browsers and platforms.
* [SN 0455 - Listener Feedback #187](../../sources/sn-0455.md) (2014-05-13) - May 2014 Patch Tuesday, the Certificate Authority Security Council's rebuttal on Chrome's revocation stance, the appeals court ruling for Oracle on Java API copyright, and listener questions on DANE and DNSSEC, the number of trusted CAs, EFT blocking, certificate handling in the OS versus browser, and randomness.
* [SN 0456 - Harvesting Entropy](../../sources/sn-0456.md) (2014-05-20) - Mozilla adds DRM video, a voicemail-based two-factor bypass, a camera as quantum noise source, and how SQRL harvests entropy with a streaming SHA-512 hash.
* [SN 0457 - Listener Feedback #188](../../sources/sn-0457.md) (2014-05-27) - Net Neutrality debate with ISP operator Brett Glass, the Windows XP "PosReady" update hack, the eBay breach, and ten listener questions on entropy, 2FA, SSL load balancers and SpinRite.
* [SN 0458 - TrueCrypt: WTF?](../../sources/sn-0458.md) (2014-06-03) - Steve and Leo dissect the sudden end of TrueCrypt, plus CFAA chilling effects on researchers, the GameOver Zeus takedown, and security features of iOS 8 and Swift.
* [SN 0459 - Listener Feedback #189](../../sources/sn-0459.md) (2014-06-10) - Patch Tuesday and XP, Google's End-to-End browser PGP, an OpenSSL CCS man-in-the-middle flaw, iOS 8 MAC randomisation, LACNIC IPv4 exhaustion, peering congestion and datacenter 95/5 bandwidth billing.
* [SN 0460 - Authenticated Encryption](../../sources/sn-0460.md) (2014-06-17) - Bitcoin pool GHash.io reaches 51 percent, the TweetDeck self-retweeting XSS, Azure's IPv4 shortage, the P.F. Chang's card breach, and an explanation of authenticated encryption (OCB, GCM, CAESAR).
* [SN 0461 - Listener Feedback #190](../../sources/sn-0461.md) (2014-06-24) - EFF's Open Wireless initiative, Google's BoringSSL fork, Google as domain registrar, congressional and Supreme Court moves on the NSA and software patents, and ten listener questions on proxies, HTTPS, timing attacks and CBC.
* [SN 0462 - Cloud Storage Solutions](../../sources/sn-0462.md) (2014-07-01) - Microsoft and Canada's anti-spam law, PayPal freezing ProtonMail's funds, iOS 7.1.2, a careless Android KeyStore buffer comment, and the launch of a series on Trust No One cloud storage.
* [SN 0463 - Listener Feedback #191](../../sources/sn-0463.md) (2014-07-08) - Microsoft's heavy-handed No-IP domain seizure, an Indian CA issuing rogue Google certificates, the end of the XP embedded update trick, LIFX smart-bulb static-key flaw, SSD maintenance with SpinRite and the EFF Open WiFi follow-ups.
* [SN 0464 - Listener Feedback #192](../../sources/sn-0464.md) (2014-07-15) - Indian CA misissuance aftermath, UC Berkeley's web password manager study, new IoT standards (AllSeen, OIC, Thread), Google Project Zero, LibreSSL's early flaws, CryptoLocker disruption, and listener questions.
* [SN 0465 - iOS Surveillance?](../../sources/sn-0465.md) (2014-07-22) - Steve analyses Jonathan Zdziarski's HOPE X claims about undocumented iOS services and pairing records, plus Chrome dropping its revocation checkbox, Level 3 versus Verizon, canvas fingerprinting and Microsoft's password-portfolio paper.
* [SN 0466 - Listener Feedback #193](../../sources/sn-0466.md) (2014-07-29) - iOS and canvas-fingerprinting follow-ups, Signal for iPhone, the Android Fake ID certificate flaw, Verizon versus Level 3 peering, Avast HTTPS interception, resistive RAM, SQRL's password hint design and listener questions on TNO cloud storage and router isolation.
* [SN 0467 - Browser Password Managers](../../sources/sn-0467.md) (2014-08-05) - HP's IoT security survey, a PayPal two-factor bypass, Google's hash-based abuse-image detection, a preview of the BadUSB research, and Steve's summary of the UC Berkeley study of five web-based password managers.
* [SN 0468 - Listener Feedback #194](../../sources/sn-0468.md) (2014-08-12) - CryptoLocker key database found and a free decryption service, Hold Security's 1.2 billion credential claim, Google ranking HTTPS sites, BadUSB follow-up, light-based eavesdropping on chip bags, and listener questions on password meters, VPNs and Netflix, and home networks.
* [SN 0469 - Big Routing Tables](../../sources/sn-0469.md) (2014-08-19) - How a Verizon routing leak overflowed the 512K default table of older Cisco routers, plus the Gyrophone sensor eavesdropping research, Delaware's digital-assets law, HTTP shaming, secure coding in SQRL and the origin of SpinRite.
* [SN 0470 - Listener Feedback #195](../../sources/sn-0470.md) (2014-08-26) - A quiet news week with the first confirmed serious Heartbleed-based breach (Community Health Systems), plus listener Q&A on fiber versus copper between buildings, StartSSL free certificates, password policies and SpinRite.
* [SN 0471 - PGP: Time for an Upgrade?](../../sources/sn-0471.md) (2014-09-02) - The iCloud celebrity photo leak and Find My iPhone brute-forcing, new WPS router flaws from weak random number generators, IMSI-catcher base stations, a fake CryptoLocker, and why email encryption with PGP needs replacing.
* [SN 0472 - Listener Feedback #196](../../sources/sn-0472.md) (2014-09-09) - Calm September 2014 Patch Tuesday, the Home Depot point-of-sale breach, Comcast Wi-Fi script injection and Google's push against SHA-1, plus Q&A on Ethernet isolation, ATA Secure Erase, rogue cell base stations, XP in a VM and Diffie-Hellman.
* [SN 0473 - Google vs. SHA-1](../../sources/sn-0473.md) (2014-09-16) - Google's abrupt plan to flag SHA-1 certificates in Chrome from November 2014 versus the industry's 2017 timeline, plus news on Comcast and Tor, Tim Cook's iMessage claims, a LinkedIn contact-import quirk and the Android browser same-origin bypass.
* [SN 0474 - Listener Feedback #197](../../sources/sn-0474.md) (2014-09-23) - iOS 8 trusted root certificate analysis, CloudFlare Keyless SSL, Simply Secure, the Zedo malvertising incident, plus listener Q&A on encrypting everything, POS patching policies, SSD error rates and third-party iOS keyboards.
* [SN 0475 - Shocked by the Shell](../../sources/sn-0475.md) (2014-10-01) - Shellshock, the two-decade-old Bash environment-variable command-execution flaw, plus iOS 8 MAC randomization claims, FBI complaints about phone encryption, Mozilla's SHA-256 download losses and Kevin Mitnick's zero-day exchange.
* [SN 0476 - Listener Feedback #198](../../sources/sn-0476.md) (2014-10-07) - The JPMorgan Chase breach, Yahoo and Shellshock, the public release of BadUSB firmware tools, a Bugzilla email-domain privilege flaw, plus Q&A on HTTPS and referrer headers, MAC filtering, ECC versus RSA and spotting Trust No One services.
* [SN 0477 - Payment Tokenization](../../sources/sn-0477.md) (2014-10-14) - The Sandworm Windows OLE zero-day fixed on Patch Tuesday, a rumored SSLv3 flaw, Firefox 33 with Cisco's H.264 codec, Dropbox credential dumps, and how EMVCo payment tokenization and Apple Pay work.
* [SN 0478 - Poodle Bites](../../sources/sn-0478.md) (2014-10-21) - A deep explanation and debunking of the POODLE SSLv3 padding-oracle attack, the TLS Fallback SCSV fix, FBI complaints about encryption, and new low-cost Yubico FIDO U2F keys.
* [SN 0479 - Listener Feedback #199](../../sources/sn-0479.md) (2014-10-28) - Retailers' CurrentC payment app versus Apple Pay, Verizon and AT&T injecting persistent UIDH tracking headers, Rivest's Spritz upgrade of RC4, and Q&A on cheap certificates, DNSSEC, POODLE details and FIDO U2F YubiKeys.
* [SN 0480 - Listener Feedback #200](../../sources/sn-0480.md) (2014-11-04) - A POODLE correction, the CurrentC email-provider leak, AirHopper FM data leakage from air-gapped PCs, TextSecure's formal audit and the EFF secure messaging scorecard, a ruling on passcodes versus fingerprints, and Q&A on cert types, SSD erase and long-term storage.
* [SN 0481 - Certificate Transparency](../../sources/sn-0481.md) (2014-11-11) - The huge November 2014 Patch Tuesday headed by MS14-066, a remote code execution flaw in Windows Schannel, plus Obama's Title II Net Neutrality call, Steve's SQRL talk, a Belkin router root flaw and an explanation of Google's Certificate Transparency and Merkle hash trees.
* [SN 0482 - Listener Feedback #201](../../sources/sn-0482.md) (2014-11-18) - A re-issued MS14-066 Schannel patch with broken cipher suites, Firefox's Forget button, airborne cell-site simulators, carrier supercookie updates, the EFF's Let's Encrypt and ACME announcement, TextSecure in WhatsApp, BitTorrent Sync concerns and Q&A on M-DISC, destroyed drives and brute force.
* [SN 0483 - Let's Encrypt](../../sources/sn-0483.md) (2014-11-25) - Symantec's discovery of the Regin state-grade espionage malware and a detailed walkthrough of the EFF-led Let's Encrypt free certificate authority and its ACME protocol.
* [SN 0484 - Listener Feedback #202](../../sources/sn-0484.md) (2014-12-02) - Q&A episode with guest host Mike Elgan covering Firefox 34, corporate SSL interception, Firefox OCSP enforcement, root certificate lifetimes, safe vulnerability reporting, an RDP ransomware story and the origin of the name Regin.
* [SN 0485 - Expensive Lessons](../../sources/sn-0485.md) (2014-12-09) - POODLE still affects TLS appliances from F5 and A10, the Turla APT gets a Linux backdoor, a court lets banks sue Target over its 2013 breach, and Steve walks through the scale of the Sony Pictures hack.
* [SN 0486 - Listener Feedback #203](../../sources/sn-0486.md) (2014-12-16) - Chrome plans to flag plain HTTP as non-secure, an unreported Iranian-linked wiper attack on Sands casinos surfaces, Steve and Leo debate publishing the stolen Sony emails, and a Q&A covers SSL Labs grades, VPNs, AppLocker and Sony exfiltration.
* [SN 0488 - The (In)Security of 2014](../../sources/sn-0488.md) (2014-12-30) - Year-end episode recorded in studio that debates whether North Korea really hacked Sony, covers an NTP remote-code flaw patched by Apple, the NSA's ranking of encryption tools, and a review of 2014's big security stories from Target to POODLE and Regin.
* [SN 0489 - Listener Feedback #204](../../sources/sn-0489.md) (2015-01-06) - Q&A covering Thunderstrike, the HSTS supercookie, Gogo's forged Google certificate, CryptoWall 2.0, AppLocker success stories, HTTPS being faster than HTTP via SPDY and the Sony FireEye alert debate.
* [SN 0490 - The Enigma Machine](../../sources/sn-0490.md) (2015-01-13) - A busy news week (Lizard Squad router botnet, Google's 90-day disclosure of a Windows flaw, unpatched old Android WebView, David Cameron on encryption) followed by a detailed explanation of how the WWII Enigma machine worked and was broken.
* [SN 0491 - Cryptographic Backdoors](../../sources/sn-0491.md) (2015-01-20) - Steve examines how the NSA reportedly tapped North Korean networks before the Sony hack, then explains that government access to encryption can be built with extra recipient keys rather than a secret backdoor, while arguing the policy question is separate.
* [SN 0492 - Listener Feedback #205](../../sources/sn-0492.md) (2015-01-27) - Q&A on SQRL usability, Project Zero's 90-day disclosure policy, Apple fixes, key-escrow follow-ups to episode 491, renewing versus rekeying certificates, CryptoWall via ad networks, how good Enigma was, and free Sophos UTM firewalls.
* [SN 0493 - Tor: Not so Anonymous](../../sources/sn-0493.md) (2015-02-03) - Regin is linked to the NSA's QWERTY tooling, the GHOST glibc flaw gets a proper explanation, a SQRL breach question is answered, and Steve explains why Tor's anonymity fails against traffic analysis by nation-state adversaries.
* [SN 0494 - Listener Feedback #206](../../sources/sn-0494.md) (2015-02-10) - February 2015 Patch Tuesday, the Anthem breach of 80 million records, a new US cyber threat integration center, car hacking on 60 Minutes, Samsung TV voice privacy, GnuPG funding, plus Q&A on Tor limits, email links, HSTS header injection and Flash.
* [SN 0495 - HTTP/2](../../sources/sn-0495.md) (2015-02-17) - Kaspersky's Equation Group hard-drive firmware malware and Carbanak bank heists, Google softening Project Zero deadlines, credit freezes, and a deep dive into how HTTP/2 multiplexes and compresses web traffic over one connection.
* [SN 0496 - Listener Feedback #207](../../sources/sn-0496.md) (2015-02-24) - Lenovo's preinstalled Superfish adware and the Komodia HTTPS-interception SDK it relied on, GCHQ and NSA theft of Gemalto SIM keys, TrueCrypt audit progress, HSTS preload, plus Q&A on drive firmware malware, egress filtering and SQRL in shared workstations.
* [SN 0497 - Vehicle Hacking](../../sources/sn-0497.md) (2015-03-03) - A router with a hidden "super" login, an encryption web service lacking authentication, Google buying top-level domains and a knowledge-based trust ranking, then an interview with Galois researchers about hacking cars and formally verified embedded software for DARPA.
* [SN 0498 - FREAK and RowHammer](../../sources/sn-0498.md) (2015-03-12) - March 2015 Patch Tuesday, Snowden documents on CIA attacks against Apple, a HSTS include-subdomains mishap on GRC, the Apple Watch and MacBook, then deep dives into the FREAK export-cipher downgrade attack and the RowHammer DRAM bit-flipping exploit.
* [SN 0499 - Listener Feedback #208](../../sources/sn-0499.md) (2015-03-17) - TeslaCrypt ransomware, Yahoo's SMS on-demand passwords, the Live.fi certificate issued through a hostmaster alias, InstantCryptor's second flawed attempt at authenticated encryption, and Q&A on credit freezes, HTTP/2 and TLS, SQRL multiple accounts, RowHammer details, USB-C power safety and TPMs.
* [SN 0500 - Windows Secure Boot](../../sources/sn-0500.md) (2015-03-24) - Episode 500 covers Pwn2Own 2015, a rogue Egyptian intermediate certificate issued under CNNIC, an iPhone PIN-counter power-cut trick, and a deep explainer on UEFI, Secure Boot and Measured Boot.
* [SN 0501 - Listener Feedback #209](../../sources/sn-0501.md) (2015-03-31) - Q&A episode covering the Great Cannon-style JavaScript DDoS against GitHub and GreatFire.org, an open rsync flaw in hotel InnGate routers, a 10-million-password analysis, miniLock, and UEFI and certificate-authority questions.
* [SN 0502 - The TrueCrypt Audit](../../sources/sn-0502.md) (2015-04-07) - Google drops the CNNIC root from Chrome, Firefox adds opportunistic encryption, Google's SMTP certificate chain expires, and Steve walks through the four findings of the TrueCrypt phase-two audit.
* [SN 503 - Listener Feedback #210](../../sources/sn-0503.md) (2015-04-14) - Listener Feedback #210 with guest host Mike Elgan, covering the EFF podcast-patent win, browser reactions to CNNIC, the Simda botnet takedown, Apple's Rootpipe flaw, key-escrow proposals, and ten listener questions.
* [SN 0504 - Great Firewalls & Cannons](../../sources/sn-0504.md) (2015-04-21) - Explains how China's Great Firewall (passive TCP reset injection) and the Great Cannon (in-path traffic hijacking used to DDoS GitHub) work, plus a serious IIS http.sys range-header bug, the Chrome NPAPI phase-out, and Let's Encrypt progress.
* [SN 0505 - Listener Feedback #211](../../sources/sn-0505.md) (2015-04-28) - Q&A episode covering RSA Conference disclosures (iOS WiFi crash bug, Samsung fingerprint weaknesses, CryptoWall via malicious Flash ads), the AFNetworking domain-name default, a YubiKey NEO PIN bypass, ad blocking ethics, RC4, SNI, and a programming-languages chat.
* [SN 0506 - Law Enforcement Backdoors](../../sources/sn-0506.md) (2015-05-05) - WPS weak-nonce router attacks, Mozilla's plan to deprecate plain HTTP, JavaScript-based DDoS and Subresource Integrity, an anti-analysis spyware strain, and the 2015 congressional testimony of Matt Blaze and Jonathan Mayer against encryption backdoors.
* [SN 0507 - Listener Feedback #212](../../sources/sn-0507.md) (2015-05-12) - Q&A episode with news on James Clapper's testimony excuse, the Second Circuit ruling on bulk phone records, the Jellyfish GPU malware proof of concept, weak smart-grid crypto, SSD data-retention specs, and listener questions on cert pinning, VPNs and links in email.
* [SN 0508 - Exploiting Keyless Entry](../../sources/sn-0508.md) (2015-05-19) - Explains why passive keyless entry and start systems in cars can be bypassed by extending radio range (a relay problem), plus Starbucks account drain, a router botnet, the Venom QEMU bug, a claimed in-flight hack, and carrier-level ad blocking.
* [SN 0509 - LOGJAM:  Imperfect Forward Secrecy](../../sources/sn-0509.md) (2015-05-26) - Explains the Logjam downgrade attack on Diffie-Hellman in TLS (export-grade 512-bit primes, shared primes and precomputation, and the suspicion that 1024-bit groups are within NSA reach), plus Let's Encrypt terms, NetUSB router bug, and the encryption backdoor debate.
* [SN 0510 - Listener Feedback #213](../../sources/sn-0510.md) (2015-06-02) - Q&A episode covering the iMessage text-rendering crash bug, a Mac firmware write-protection flaw after sleep, Microsoft's Windows 10 upgrade nag, Google's Vault and Soli projects, a live SQRL login demo, and listener questions on Windows 10, keyless-entry pouches, ad-blocking VPN apps, NoScript and key escrow.
* [SN 0511 - Listener Feedback #214](../../sources/sn-0511.md) (2015-06-09) - Q&A episode with the OPM breach of unencrypted records, a House vote limiting NIST work with the NSA, a hard-drive-firmware rootkit research project, Let's Encrypt root certificates, the UK IPv4 sale, a second SQRL demo, and listener questions on phone wiping, Apple routers, keyless fobs and certificate dates.
* [SN 0512 - Mozilla's Tracking Protection](../../sources/sn-0512.md) (2015-06-16) - Covers the LastPass network breach and why Steve stays, the larger second OPM breach, a bogus Snowden-cache story, Telekom Malaysia's BGP leak, Wikipedia moving to HTTPS, a Facebook and Carnegie Mellon SSD reliability study, and a debate on Firefox tracking protection and ad ethics.
* [SN 0513 - Listener Feedback #215](../../sources/sn-0513.md) (2015-06-23) - Q&A episode covering the XARA cross-app resource access research on OS X and iOS, an unencrypted update flaw in the Samsung-bundled SwiftKey keyboard, real-world LastPass cracking math, the WebAssembly announcement, a Let's Encrypt launch date, and ten listener questions on passwords, HSTS, IPv6 and buffer overflows.
* [SN 0514 - Tor's Astoria Client](../../sources/sn-0514.md) (2015-06-30) - Flash zero-day used by a Chinese APT group, Samsung disabling Windows Update, EM-leak key extraction from laptops, privacytools.io, ISP throttling, and research on Tor's Astoria client.
* [SN 0515 - A Crazy News Week!](../../sources/sn-0515.md) (2015-07-07) - A pure news episode covering Firefox 39, the ICANN WHOIS privacy debate, RIPv1 DDoS reflection, Amazon's s2n TLS library, ARIN IPv4 exhaustion, the Hacking Team breach, XKEYSCORE documents and Windows 10 WiFi Sense.
* [SN 0516 - SQRL Revisited](../../sources/sn-0516.md) (2015-07-14) - More Hacking Team fallout (Flash zero-days, UEFI rootkit), a short-lived OpenSSL certificate-verification bug, a crypto experts' paper against exceptional access, the OPM fingerprint disclosure, and a full walkthrough of the SQRL protocol.
* [SN 0517 - Listener Feedback #216](../../sources/sn-0517.md) (2015-07-21) - Remote takeover of Chrysler Uconnect vehicles, an out-of-band Windows font-handling patch from the Hacking Team leak, improved RC4 attacks, and listener Q&A on SQRL, least privilege, Sandboxie and WiFi Sense.
* [SN 0518 - HORNET:  A Fix for TOR?](../../sources/sn-0518.md) (2015-07-28) - Chrysler recall follow-up, the Stagefright Android media-library flaws, Google's expert versus non-expert security habits survey, a DMCA ruling, phishing statistics, and a correction of press hype about the HORNET onion-routing research.
* [SN 0519 - The Win10 Privacy Tradeoff](../../sources/sn-0519.md) (2015-08-04) - Stagefright mitigation, a BIND denial-of-service bug, the PagerDuty breach with salted-and-peppered hashing, a Mac privilege escalation, Steve's move toward Sandboxie, and a walkthrough of Windows 10's privacy settings.
* [SN 0520 - The Quest for Surfing Safety](../../sources/sn-0520.md) (2015-08-11) - Stagefright testers and patch cadence, a Firefox PDF-viewer zero-day, the x86 SMM "Memory Sinkhole" talk, Privacy Badger 1.0, Oracle's blog post against code analysis, and Steve's idea of browsing in a throwaway virtual machine.
* [SN 0521 - Security Is Difficult](../../sources/sn-0521.md) (2015-08-18) - A flawed Stagefright patch and a seventh bug, Windows 10 network chatter, malvertising delivering Angler and ransomware, Kaspersky false-positive allegations, Lenovo BIOS file injection, Android fingerprint storage flaws, AT&T and the NSA, and the web advertising debate.
* [SN 0522 - Listener Feedback 217](../../sources/sn-0522.md) (2015-08-25) - Q&A episode covering Lenovo's withdrawal of its BIOS injection feature, an open-source ransomware sample, Firefox extension signing and WebExtensions, Google's auto-updating router, physical server security, and listener questions on wiping drives, sandboxes, VMs and uBlock Origin.
* [SN 0523 - uBlock Origin](../../sources/sn-0523.md) (2015-09-01) - A deep dive on uBlock Origin as an HTML firewall and the ethics of ad blocking, plus Chrome and Amazon curbing Flash, the end of RC4 in browsers, Angler malvertising on MSN, and telemetry updates back-ported to Windows 7 and 8.
* [SN 0524 - Listener Feedback #218](../../sources/sn-0524.md) (2015-09-08) - Q&A episode covering Seagate wireless drives with hard-coded Telnet access, the Adblock Browser app, preinstalled Android malware, ad-network proxy tricks to evade blockers, uBlock Origin blacklist mode, glass platters and device self-updating.
* [SN 0525 - Disconnect](../../sources/sn-0525.md) (2015-09-15) - Correction that glass is not a fluid, clarification of a LastPass "remember password" talk, iMessage key-management trust, Canary Tokens, Let's Encrypt's schedule, and an interview with Disconnect cofounder Patrick Jackson on web and mobile tracking.
* [SN 0526 - iOS Content Filters](../../sources/sn-0526.md) (2015-09-22) - XcodeGhost, iOS 9 security fixes, a Flash update, AVG's data-selling policy, Cisco SYNful Knock router implants, Ashley Madison password hashes, the VW emissions defeat device, India's draft encryption policy, and a survey of the first iOS content blockers.
* [SN 0527 - Listener Feedback #219](../../sources/sn-0527.md) (2015-09-29) - Q&A episode covering a TrueCrypt kernel-driver privilege escalation and the move to VeraCrypt, Lenovo ThinkPad telemetry, Crystal joining acceptable ads, antivirus as attack surface, steganography, peer-to-peer VPNs and Google Contributor privacy.
* [SN 0528 - Breaches & Vigilante Worms](../../sources/sn-0528.md) (2015-10-06) - News-only episode covering the Patreon, Experian/T-Mobile and Scottrade breaches, the second StageFright wave, the Linux.Wifatch vigilante router worm, and Android 6.0 Marshmallow security features.
* [SN 0529 - Listener Feedback #220](../../sources/sn-0529.md) (2015-10-13) - LastPass founder Joe Siegrist discusses the LogMeIn acquisition, followed by Patch Tuesday, SHA-1 collision progress and the CA/Browser Forum SHA-1 extension ballot, and ten listener questions.
* [SN 0530 - Doing It Wrong](../../sources/sn-0530.md) (2015-10-20) - A Flash zero-day patch and the Youmi iOS SDK private-API abuse, then four examples of security done wrong - 1Password's unencrypted metadata, Target's PA system, chip-and-PIN shims and Sandboxie's advice to disable updates.
* [SN 0531 - Listener Feedback #221](../../sources/sn-0531.md) (2015-10-27) - Q&A episode revisiting the 1Password metadata design flaw, the Logjam and weak Diffie-Hellman precomputation story, Let's Encrypt cross-signing, the withdrawal of the SHA-1 extension ballot, VeraCrypt 1.16 and ten listener questions.
* [SN 0532 - Verifying iOS App Conduct](../../sources/sn-0532.md) (2015-11-03) - News on Google's action against Symantec certificates, the Hacking Team's return, Tor Messenger, UK versus US crypto policy and the Sniffly HSTS probe, followed by an explanation of why Objective-C dynamic binding makes iOS app vetting fundamentally weak.
* [SN 0533 - Listener Feedback #222](../../sources/sn-0533.md) (2015-11-10) - Q&A episode covering the Linux.Encoder ransomware aimed at web servers via a Magento flaw, Comodo's baseline-requirement violations, Microsoft and Mozilla accelerating SHA-1 deprecation, Firefox 42 tracking protection, the ProtonMail DDoS and ten listener questions.
* [SN 0534 - Encryption:  Law Enforcement's Whipping Boy](../../sources/sn-0534.md) (2015-11-17) - Carnegie Mellon's FBI-paid Tor deanonymization, large-scale IoT firmware analysis, Conficker on police body cameras and WhatsApp call metadata, then a rebuttal of post-Paris calls for encryption backdoors.
* [SN 0535 - Listener Feedback #223](../../sources/sn-0535.md) (2015-11-24) - Dell's eDellRoot certificate with exposed private key, researchers' analysis of LastPass local attack surface, Windows 10 silently removing apps, the Manhattan DA's smartphone-encryption proposal, and listener questions on Amazon password resets and Diffie-Hellman settings.
* [SN 0536 - Listener Feedback #224](../../sources/sn-0536.md) (2015-12-01) - Microsoft revokes Dell's leaked certificates, Arris cable-modem backdoor password scheme, BlackBerry refuses Pakistan, a patent troll suing 67 companies over elliptic-curve TLS, and ten listener questions.
* [SN 0537 - A Mega News Week](../../sources/sn-0537.md) (2015-12-08) - All-news episode on December Patch Tuesday and an Adobe Flash update, Microsoft's pressure toward Windows 10, vendor-installed services from Dell and Lenovo, AOL Desktop's unauthenticated scripting protocol, Let's Encrypt public beta, post-Paris policy moves and CryptoWall 4.
* [SN 0538 - Listener Feedback #225](../../sources/sn-0538.md) (2015-12-15) - Government versus encryption updates (Kazakhstan, FBI director, Russia data localisation), the SHA-1 sunset and Facebook and CloudFlare certificate-switching compromise, Google distrusting a Symantec root, Bell HomeHub WPS flaw, Telegram crypto thesis, and a listener question on why computers need clocks.
* [SN 0539 - Listener Feedback #226](../../sources/sn-0539.md) (2015-12-22) - Year-end episode on public Wi-Fi attacks, the Juniper ScreenOS backdoors tied to Dual_EC_DRBG, the FTC settlement with Oracle over Java updates, a Grub2 backspace bypass, WhatsApp blocked in Brazil, and eight listener questions.
* [SN 0541 - New Year's News](../../sources/sn-0541.md) (2016-01-05) - First episode of 2016 covering IPv6 adoption, Windows 10 upgrade nagging and market share, Microsoft uploading disk-encryption keys, vulnerability counts for 2015, Android patches, JavaScript ransomware (Ransom32), the Wi-Fi HaLow standard and LastPass 4 emergency access.
* [SN 0542 - Listener Feedback #227](../../sources/sn-0542.md) (2016-01-12) - Trend Micro antivirus password-manager component exposing remote code execution to any website, Symantec's five late SHA-1 certificates, Firefox's SHA-1 rollback, and LastPass emergency access explained, plus Q&A.
* [SN 0543 - LostPass](../../sources/sn-0543.md) (2016-01-19) - The LostPass phishing attack on the LastPass browser extension, plus IoT weaknesses (Ring doorbell, D-Link webcam), a Fortinet hardcoded login, Microsoft's Windows 10 upgrade push and its new-silicon support policy.
* [SN 0544 - Listener Feedback #228](../../sources/sn-0544.md) (2016-01-26) - California's smartphone-decryption bill, a Dutch consumer group suing Samsung over Android updates, Google's Brotli compression, the CrashSafari history-API bug, and Q&A on why two-factor does not stop man-in-the-middle phishing.
* [SN 0545 - Three Dumb Routers](../../sources/sn-0545.md) (2016-02-02) - Steve explains why a Y-shaped layout of three plain NAT routers gives true isolation for IoT devices, after Oracle ends the Java browser plugin, Android February fixes, and his Zeo EEG sleep monitor discovery.
* [SN 0546 - Router Q&A Follow-up](../../sources/sn-0546.md) (2016-02-09) - Apple's Error 53, Comodo's Chromodo browser disabling same-origin policy, a Windows 10 traffic-analysis claim debunked, an eBay script-filter bypass, and follow-up Q&A on the three-router isolation layout.
* [SN 0547 - GRC Is Down](../../sources/sn-0547.md) (2016-02-16) - A DNS-reflection DDoS knocks GRC.com offline, plus the glibc getaddrinfo stack overflow, ECDH key extraction through a wall, Bruce Schneier's encryption product survey, and 5D glass data storage.
* [SN 0548 - DDoS Attack Mitigation](../../sources/sn-0548.md) (2016-02-23) - Apple versus the FBI court order over the San Bernardino iPhone, the backdoored Linux Mint ISO, more Comodo problems, a hospital ransom payment, glibc follow-up, and Steve's account of living through the GRC DDoS attacks.
* [SN 0549 - Listener Feedback #229](../../sources/sn-0549.md) (2016-03-01) - Apple's formal reply to the FBI order and a backdoor definition, iPhone passcode derivation limits, removing admin rights as mitigation, internal port scanning for IoT, and Q&A on three-router IoT isolation, NAS placement and IPv6.
* [SN 0550 - CacheBleed](../../sources/sn-0550.md) (2016-03-08) - Let's Encrypt reaches a million certificates, KeRanger Mac ransomware via a trojaned Transmission installer, Facebook's unlimited password-reset code guessing, printed-ink fingerprint spoofing, and the CacheBleed cache-bank side-channel attack.
* [SN 0551 - Listener Feedback #230](../../sources/sn-0551.md) (2016-03-15) - DoJ escalation in the Apple encryption fight and the WhatsApp standoff, the Trane thermostat IoT flaws, TFTP as a new DDoS amplifier, Microsoft's Windows 10 upgrade push in a security update, plus listener Q&A.
* [SN 0552 - D.R.O.W.N.](../../sources/sn-0552.md) (2016-03-22) - The DROWN attack on TLS servers that still support SSLv2, plus the FBI's delay of its Apple hearing, Matthew Green's iMessage attachment attack, a cheap EM side-channel on phones, a malvertising wave, Pwn2Own 2016 and a new Stagefright exploit.
* [SN 0553 - Too Much News](../../sources/sn-0553.md) (2016-03-29) - The FBI drops its Apple case, a California bill requiring decryptable phones, TrueCrypt's reported origins, StartSSL's broken domain validation, hospital ransomware, the Badlock preannouncement, the Wi-Fi Alliance's IoT provisioning work, and Steve's Never10 utility.
* [SN 0554 - Listener Feedback #231](../../sources/sn-0554.md) (2016-04-05) - Google/Nest abandoning the Revolv smart-home hub, Schneier on the FBI's iPhone method, WhatsApp end-to-end encryption arrival, domain-transfer woes, and ten listener questions on SMB exposure, Locky, TDE, SNMP, VLANs and self-encrypting drives.
* [SN 0555 - WhatsApp](../../sources/sn-0555.md) (2016-04-12) - Steve's analysis of the Signal protocol now built into WhatsApp (with two settings to enable), plus the Badlock anticlimax, the Burr-Feinstein draft, FBI purchase of an iPhone tool, Firefox extension namespace flaws, CCTV DVR malware, Petya ransomware and a UK smart-meter key blunder.
* [SN 0556 - SMTP STS](../../sources/sn-0556.md) (2016-04-19) - Episode on the 60 Minutes SS7 phone-hacking demo, Canadian police holding BlackBerry's global key, the end of QuickTime for Windows, and the SMTP STS proposal.
* [SN 0557 - Listener Feedback #232](../../sources/sn-0557.md) (2016-04-26) - News on Let's Encrypt growth, the cost of the FBI iPhone unlock, a regsvr32 AppLocker bypass, Opera's "VPN", TeslaCrypt and fake DDoS extortion, plus listener Q&A on firewalls, spam and antivirus.
* [SN 0558 - Listener Feedback #233](../../sources/sn-0558.md) (2016-05-03) - Email Privacy Act passes the House, the Snowden/Zakaria encryption debate, compelled fingerprint unlocking, Android Mediaserver flaws, WhatsApp blocked in Brazil, the Craig Wright Satoshi claim, plus listener Q&A.
* [SN 0559 - Dumb SmartThings](../../sources/sn-0559.md) (2016-05-10) - May 2016 Patch Tuesday, the end of the Craig Wright Satoshi saga, a Windows 7 Secure Boot update breakage on ASUS boards, curl-to-bash trickery, and an analysis of SmartThings and ZigBee home-automation insecurity.
* [SN 0560 - Z-Wave Goodbye](../../sources/sn-0560.md) (2016-05-17) - Z-Wave's closed design and a door-lock flaw, plus Windows 10 upgrade nagging, the Oracle v. Google API copyright fight, burner-phone ID legislation, McAfee's fake WhatsApp hack and a bogus encryption Kickstarter.
* [SN 0561 - Listener Feedback #234](../../sources/sn-0561.md) (2016-05-24) - TeslaCrypt authors release the master key, Chrome's HTML5-by-default plan for Flash, 117 million old LinkedIn credentials for sale, Google Allo encryption choices, EME and the W3C, Microsoft's deceptive Windows 10 upgrade dialog, plus listener Q&A.
* [SN 0562 - IoT Infancy](../../sources/sn-0562.md) (2016-05-31) - Collapse of the Burr-Feinstein encryption bill, Google wins the Oracle fair-use trial, Blue Coat's Symantec intermediate certificate, insecure laptop bloatware updaters, SQRL status and part one of a look at IoT insecurity.
* [SN 0563 - IoT Infancy Part 2](../../sources/sn-0563.md) (2016-06-07) - A win32k zero-day for sale, the A2 analog hardware backdoor, June Android bulletin, Messenger encryption, SQRL's identity lock, and Rapid7's findings on insecure Internet baby monitors.
* [SN 0564 - Listener Feedback #235](../../sources/sn-0564.md) (2016-06-14) - Symantec buys Blue Coat, a Chrome PDF bug, the hacker Peace and stolen credential markets, Let's Encrypt's mailing glitch, Visual Studio telemetry, and listener Q&A on PINs, password managers, biometrics and SQRL.
* [SN 0565 - Control-Flow Enforcement Technology (CET)](../../sources/sn-0565.md) (2016-06-21) - News on Palantir's red-team report, SS7 interception of SMS codes, Netgear Arlo camera re-registration, GoToMyPC resets and the Intel Management Engine, then a deep dive into Intel CET with ENDBRANCH and the shadow stack.
* [SN 0566 - Listener Feedback #236](../../sources/sn-0566.md) (2016-06-28) - Microsoft's Windows 10 upgrade lawsuit settlement, a court ruling on FBI hacking and IP privacy, a Windows 7 update-slowness fix, Comodo's Let's Encrypt trademark grab, JavaScript ransomware, fan-speed data exfiltration, a Netgear router flaw, and listener Q&A.
* [SN 0567 - Hacking Certificates](../../sources/sn-0567.md) (2016-07-05) - Android ransomware trends, extraction of Android full-disk-encryption keys, Tavis Ormandy's flaws in Symantec/Norton engines, WhatsApp funds frozen in Brazil, an insecure IoT smart plug, and StartCom's flawed StartEncrypt certificate automation.
* [SN 0568 - Listener Feedback #237](../../sources/sn-0568.md) (2016-07-12) - Facebook Messenger Secret Conversations, Russia's decryption-key mandate, the Lenovo/IBV system-management-mode firmware flaw, password-sharing and the CFAA, Chrome's post-quantum experiment, Pokemon Go risks and port isolation with managed switches.
* [SN 0569 - Messenger, CryptoDrop, & Riffle](../../sources/sn-0569.md) (2016-07-19) - Password-sharing rulings, FDIC breach concealment, Tor operator departure, a 20-year-old Windows Point-and-Print driver flaw, Juniper certificate validation bug, CertWatch, and deep dives on Messenger's franking, CryptoDrop and MIT's Riffle.
* [SN 0570 - Listener Feedback #238](../../sources/sn-0570.md) (2016-07-26) - Apple image-parsing flaws patched, Russian hacking of the DNC and Yahoo state-actor warnings, Microsoft's Irish-email court win, the CUJO appliance's ARP trick, and a dozen listener questions.
* [SN 0571 - Phishing & Filtering](../../sources/sn-0571.md) (2016-08-02) - LastPass Firefox add-on bug, unencrypted wireless keyboards, NIST deprecating SMS 2FA, then why website spoofing defeats every second factor and how multi-interface router filtering works.
* [SN 0572 - DEF CON & Black Hat, Part 1](../../sources/sn-0572.md) (2016-08-09) - Windows 10 Anniversary Update problems, Apple's new bug bounty, Qualcomm Android flaws, battery-API tracking, Let's Encrypt root in Firefox, Drobo BeyondRAID and ZFS scrub, HEIST and HTTP/2 attacks, and the 19-year-old Windows SMB credential leak.
* [SN 0573 - News & Memory](../../sources/sn-0573.md) (2016-08-16) - Correcting the "Microsoft golden key" story about Secure Boot, ad-block arms race, an Avast certificate hijack, a TCP side channel in Linux/Android, Windows Update rollups, SMB credential leak mitigation, DiskFiltration, and Intel/Micron 3D XPoint memory.
* [SN 0574 - Routers & Micro Kernels](../../sources/sn-0574.md) (2016-08-23) - Shadow Brokers' leak of alleged Equation Group tools, exploit-broker bidding against Apple, a GnuPG random-number-generator flaw, EFF on Windows 10 telemetry, Chrome SHA-1 warnings, iMessage cryptanalysis, hidden router switch-chip features, and what a microkernel is.
* [SN 0575 - Pegasus & Trident](../../sources/sn-0575.md) (2016-08-30) - Pegasus/Trident iOS spyware and the emergency iOS 9.3.5 patch, plus Dropbox and Opera breach handling, the WoSign misissuance scandal, WhatsApp-Facebook data sharing, FBI election-site alert and a prime-number puzzle.
* [SN 0576 - Flip Feng Shui](../../sources/sn-0576.md) (2016-09-06) - Weaponised Rowhammer ("Flip Feng Shui") against cloud VMs sharing memory, plus Mozilla's case against WoSign, the Sweet32-style birthday attack on 64-bit block ciphers, ambient-light tracking, a phishing study, a USB network-adapter attack and the SHA-1 appliance puzzle answer.
* [SN 0577 - Listener Feedback #239](../../sources/sn-0577.md) (2016-09-13) - Q&A episode with Flip Feng Shui follow-up, Android's rough week, Bluetooth LE privacy leaks, a datacenter taken down by fire-suppression noise, and listener questions on drive wiping, EdgeRouter X, VPNs, SQRL versioning, Rowhammer vs ECC and lithium-ion care.
* [SN 0578 - GRC's XSS Adventure](../../sources/sn-0578.md) (2016-09-20) - Rule 41 expansion of remote hacking warrants, the IANA stewardship transition, a coming larger DNSSEC root key, Chrome's plan to mark HTTP as insecure, the iPhone 5c NAND mirroring attack, and Steve's account of fixing a cross-site scripting hole on GRC with the Tinfoil Security scanner.
* [SN 0579 - A Very Busy Week](../../sources/sn-0579.md) (2016-09-27) - Brian Krebs dropped by Akamai after a record DDoS and picked up by Project Shield, Yahoo's half-billion-account breach, weakened iOS 10 backup password hashing, an iPhone 7 jailbreak, the GWX remover, an OpenSSL OCSP memory-exhaustion flaw, Mozilla moving to distrust WoSign/StartCom, and BitTorrent Sync becoming Resilio.
* [SN 0580 - Listener Feedback #240](../../sources/sn-0580.md) (2016-10-04) - Windows 10 Edge to run in a lightweight VM (Enterprise only), HP's printer-ink lockout reversal, dynamic DNS via CNAME, browser session-store writes wearing out SSDs, and listener questions on DNSSEC, HSTS, HPKP, ISP port blocking, Let's Encrypt and eBay phone-number lockout.
* [SN 0581 - Yahoo & Primal Worries](../../sources/sn-0581.md) (2016-10-11) - Windows 7/8.1 move to monolithic monthly rollups, a $1.5 million iOS exploit bounty, a Signal-for-Android length bug, NIST "security fatigue" findings, WoSign's response to Mozilla, Samsung Note 7 fires, Yahoo's secret email scanning and blocked forwarding, and the trapdoored 1024-bit prime research.
* [SN 0582 - Listener Feedback #241](../../sources/sn-0582.md) (2016-10-18) - Compelled fingerprint unlocking warrant, the Quarkslab audit of VeraCrypt, browser SSD write fatigue follow-up, Elon Musk's simulation remark, lithium-ion battery shorts, and eleven listener questions.
* [SN 0583 - DRAMMER](../../sources/sn-0583.md) (2016-10-25) - The Mirai-driven DDoS on Dyn's DNS, the Dirty COW Linux kernel race condition, the five-year average life of kernel bugs, and the Drammer attack that turns Rowhammer into a root exploit on Android phones.
* [SN 0584 - Listener Feedback #242](../../sources/sn-0584.md) (2016-11-01) - Branch-predictor side channel that defeats ASLR, a compiler optimization breaking constant-time crypto, the Battery Status API tracking problem, DMCA exemptions, Windows 10 update control and a Google-disclosed Windows zero-day.
* [SN 0585 - The Windows AtomBomb](../../sources/sn-0585.md) (2016-11-08) - Compression side channels in encrypted VoIP, OAuth 2.0 formal analysis and sloppy app implementations, spoofed-packet egress testing, and the AtomBombing code-injection technique built on the Windows global atom table.
* [SN 0586 - The BlackNurse Attack](../../sources/sn-0586.md) (2016-11-15) - Listener results showing NAT routers drop spoofed packets, LessPass found to be badly designed, a ZigBee takeover of Philips Hue bulbs, Web of Trust data selling, Pixel exploit at PwnFest, WiFi CSI keystroke inference and the low-bandwidth ICMP BlackNurse DoS.
* [SN 0587 - Mobile & IoT Nightmares](../../sources/sn-0587.md) (2016-11-22) - PoisonTap USB attack on locked computers, spyware-laden low-cost Android firmware from Adups and an unauthenticated Ragentek update channel, an iPhone lock-screen bypass, Schneier's testimony on IoT, the UK Investigatory Powers Act, and a ransomware author asking for help.
* [SN 0588 - Listener Feedback #243](../../sources/sn-0588.md) (2016-11-29) - San Francisco Muni ransomware, Chrome's SHA-1 removal date, speakers repurposed as microphones, an NTP denial-of-service fix, a WordPress update-signing near miss, plus listener Q&A on DNS providers, imaging software and internet background radiation.
* [SN 0589 - Listener Feedback #244](../../sources/sn-0589.md) (2016-12-06) - Gooligan Android malware, BitLocker exposure during Windows 10 feature upgrades, a Mirai variant knocking out UK routers, AirDroid's weak crypto, a card-detail guessing attack, a new Cloudflare-observed botnet, Sony camera backdoors and the Muni ransomware crook's email being breached.
* [SN 0590 - Listener Feedback #245](../../sources/sn-0590.md) (2016-12-13) - BOTS Act against ticket bots, Popcorn Time ransomware's pay-or-infect-others option, a Netgear router command-injection flaw, Yahoo mail XSS, steganographic ad malware, Signal press coverage, OpenVPN audits and listener Q&A on DMZ, stealth ports and VPN port scans.
* [SN 0591 - Law Meets Internet](../../sources/sn-0591.md) (2016-12-20) - Russian election hacking commentary, Yahoo's billion-account breach, internet-related legislation and court rulings, Linux desktop media-parser exploits, the Mac Thunderbolt FileVault fix, router-targeting steganographic malvertising, V2V mandates, camera encryption and the IPv6 address space.
* [SN 0593 - I'm Not a Robot!  (Really)](../../sources/sn-0593.md) (2017-01-03) - Smart-home data in a murder case, an incomplete PHPMailer fix leaving millions of sites exposed, Vermont utility laptop malware hype, password length and cracking rigs, another Netgear flaw, TLS 1.3 finalisation, and how Google's no-captcha checkbox works.
* [SN 0594 - A Look Into PHP Malware](../../sources/sn-0594.md) (2017-01-10) - The FTC sues D-Link over insecure routers and cameras, voice-triggered Echo orders, Google's invisible reCAPTCHA, why long passwords still need high entropy, XMPP with OMEMO, the flawed US government Grizzly Steppe indicator list and the PHP web shell it named.
* [SN 0595 - What's Up With WhatsApp?](../../sources/sn-0595.md) (2017-01-17) - GoDaddy's domain-validation bug affecting thousands of certificates, car and IoT data demanded by police, McDonald's web flaws, the Cellebrite data breach, Google's RAISR super-resolution, a formal definition of maximal password strength, and the debate over WhatsApp's key-change handling.
* [SN 0596 - Password Complexity Calculations](../../sources/sn-0596.md) (2017-01-24) - Symantec's further invalid certificates found via Certificate Transparency, a Cisco WebEx Chrome extension remote-execution flaw, browser autofill leakage, Tor's next-generation hidden services, Heartbleed three years on, Pwn2Own 2017 prizes, and logarithm tricks for comparing password entropy.
* [SN 0597 - Traitors in Our Midst](../../sources/sn-0597.md) (2017-01-31) - The robot-arm reCAPTCHA stunt, a wider Cisco WebEx extension flaw, Netgear password-disclosure bugs, Gmail blocking .js attachments, browser makers' complaints about antivirus, a copy-paste hidden-text trick, SQRL shared access design, and a survey of security flaws in network printers.
* [SN 0598 - Two-Armed Bandits](../../sources/sn-0598.md) (2017-02-07) - A printer-hijacking stunt exposing 150,000 devices via UPnP, the Vizio TV tracking settlement, an SMB client crash bug disclosed after 90 days, Android pattern-lock video attacks, iOS apps skipping TLS certificate checks, SQL Slammer's return, the EME dispute at W3C and slot machines predicted via weak random numbers.
* [SN 0599 - TLS Interception INsecurity](../../sources/sn-0599.md) (2017-02-14) - A delayed Patch Tuesday, uBlock Origin on Safari, a university's IoT botnet outbreak, libsodium joining PHP, rising fileless malware, an AES Crypt tax-document tip, Samsung Note 7 battery fires, and a large study showing HTTPS interception by middleboxes and antivirus weakens security.
* [SN 600 - The MMU Side-Channel Attack](../../sources/sn-0600.md) (2017-02-21) - Cancelled February 2017 Patch Tuesday, Operation BugDrop, resold connected cars, Firefox add-on changes, and the VUSec MMU cache side channel that defeats ASLR from JavaScript.
* [SN 601 - The First SHA-1 Collision](../../sources/sn-0601.md) (2017-02-28) - Cloudbleed at Cloudflare, another expired Project Zero deadline for Microsoft, CloudPets and airport data exposures, Chrome 56 TLS 1.3 vs Blue Coat, and Google's first SHA-1 collision.
* [SN 602 - Let's Spoof!](../../sources/sn-0602.md) (2017-03-07) - Amazon S3 outage post-mortem, Android password manager flaws, DNSMessenger fileless malware, Firefox Containers, UEFI ransomware proof of concept, and Let's Encrypt issuing phishing-prone PayPal certificates.
* [SN 603 - Vault 7](../../sources/sn-0603.md) (2017-03-14) - March 2017 Patch Tuesday, Invisible reCAPTCHA, preinstalled Android malware, MAC randomization failures, the Apache Struts 2 zero-day, the Confide messenger critique, and Steve's take on WikiLeaks' Vault 7 CIA release.
* [SN 604 - Taming Web Ads](../../sources/sn-0604.md) (2017-03-21) - Windows 7 updates blocked on new CPUs, a Cisco IOS Telnet flaw found in Vault 7, Ubiquiti airOS bug, Yahoo breach indictment details, Pwn2Own 2017 results, another LastPass bug from Tavis Ormandy, and a proposal for browser-enforced ad restrictions.
* [SN 605 - Google vs. Symantec](../../sources/sn-0605.md) (2017-03-28) - Another Tavis Ormandy LastPass flaw, a large iOS 10.3 security update, GiftGhostBot gift card brute forcing, Microsoft Docs.com public-by-default exposure, UK and US privacy and encryption politics, a slow-patched Nest camera flaw, and Google's plan to distrust Symantec certificates.
* [SN 606 - Proactive Privacy](../../sources/sn-0606.md) (2017-04-04) - iOS 10.3.1 WiFi chip fix, Smart TV over-the-air attack, Z-Wave S2, the technical detail of Tavis Ormandy's LastPass content-script bug, EU anti-encryption plans, Samsung S8 face unlock, and the 40th anniversary of Alice and Bob.
* [SN 607 - Proactive Privacy, Really!](../../sources/sn-0607.md) (2017-04-11) - Symantec links 40 past attacks to Vault 7 tools, DNS CAA records become mandatory for CAs, an Office OLE zero-day, Brickerbot, a Brazilian bank DNS hijack, fake VPN phishing, and a full survey of online tracking technologies and privacy countermeasures.
* [SN 608 - News & Feedback Potpourri](../../sources/sn-0608.md) (2017-04-18) - PINlogger.js motion-sensor PIN theft, MasterPrint fingerprint spoofing, a Linux kernel UDP MSG_PEEK flaw, Shadow Brokers' Windows exploit dump, punycode phishing, the full DefenseCode UPnP disclosure, and follow-ups on last week's privacy survey.
* [SN 609 - The Double Pulsar](../../sources/sn-0609.md) (2017-04-25) - DoublePulsar infections via the leaked EternalBlue exploit, the one-byte patch removing Microsoft's CPU update block, Microsoft Authenticator passwordless login, Chrome's planned ad blocker, MasterCard's biometric card, and Linksys router flaws.
* [SN 610 - Intel's Mismanagement Engine](../../sources/sn-0610.md) (2017-05-02) - Intel confirms a critical remote flaw in its Management Engine, open ports in Android apps, an unpatched Edge same-origin bypass, the Hajime vigilante botnet, Chrome dropping the common name fallback, and the FCC move against Net Neutrality.
* [SN 611 - Go FCC Yourself](../../sources/sn-0611.md) (2017-05-09) - The Intel AMT authentication bypass explained, Microsoft's malware engine flaw found by Project Zero, SMS two-factor theft via SS7, Google's OAuth phishing worm, ultrasonic tracking apps, and how to comment on Net Neutrality.
* [SN 612 - Makes You WannaCry](../../sources/sn-0612.md) (2017-05-16) - The WannaCry worm built on the leaked EternalBlue SMB exploit, plus an HP audio-driver keylogger, Windows 10 S browser restrictions, the OpenVPN audit, Android Project Treble and NIST's updated password guidance.
* [SN 613 - WannaCry Aftermath](../../sources/sn-0613.md) (2017-05-23) - WannaCry follow-ups (NSA stockpiling, the PATCH Act, kill switch logic, key recovery, infection counts), Stack Exchange's HTTPS migration, Mar-a-Lago WiFi, UK internet regulation plans, certificate revocation and OCSP Must-Staple, and SSD disturbance attacks.
* [SN 614 - Vulnerabilities Galore!](../../sources/sn-0614.md) (2017-05-30) - Episode covering SambaCry, the Android Cloak & Dagger UI attacks, subtitle-based media player takeover, 1Password Travel Mode and Google's offline ad-sales tracking.
* [SN 615 - Legacy's Long Tail](../../sources/sn-0615.md) (2017-06-06) - Episode on the OneLogin breach, UK Trident submarines running Windows XP, the Verizon SMS-hijack bitcoin theft, Fireball adware, Chrome WebRTC recording and homomorphic-style blinding.
* [SN 616 - Things Are Getting Worse](../../sources/sn-0616.md) (2017-06-13) - Episode on steganographic malware command channels, real-time 2FA phishing in the Russian election hacking, new Microsoft malware-engine bugs, clipboard bitcoin malware, Foscam camera flaws and PowerPoint hover malware.
* [SN 617 - When Governments React](../../sources/sn-0617.md) (2017-06-20) - Episode on French-British, Japanese, Russian and German moves against encryption and anonymity, the CIA CherryBlossom router implant, unpatched HP printers, the end of SMBv1 and a record ransomware payout.
* [SN 618 - Research: Useful & Otherwise](../../sources/sn-0618.md) (2017-06-27) - Episode on NIST lightweight cryptography, post-quantum RSA with terabit keys, Cisco's doubtful encrypted-malware detection claim, OpenVPN fuzzing bugs, Intel Skylake/Kaby Lake hyperthreading microcode flaw and NaviStone form tracking.
* [SN 619 - All the Usual Suspects](../../sources/sn-0619.md) (2017-07-11) - Episode covering W3C adopting EME, OpenBSD KARL, Apple's failing bug bounty, WoSign/StartCom distrust, Let's Encrypt wildcards, Broadpwn, Windows Controlled Folder Access and DNSthingy.
* [SN 620 - Calm Before the Storm](../../sources/sn-0620.md) (2017-07-18) - Pre-Black Hat episode covering the Net Neutrality fight, MySpace's weak account recovery, paste-blocking password fields, Australia's encryption law, pacemaker evidence, a lawful-interception certificate idea and SQRL's signed self-updater.
* [SN 621 - Crypto Tension](../../sources/sn-0621.md) (2017-07-25) - Episode on the arrest of a Budapest ethical hacker, Verizon video throttling, the Flash end-of-life schedule, Firefox Quantum performance, a debunked Echo 911 story and the TLS 1.3 static Diffie-Hellman wiretap controversy.
* [SN 622 - Hack the Vote](../../sources/sn-0622.md) (2017-08-01) - DEF CON fallout including the Voting Machine Village, SMBloris, Microsoft's enlarged bug bounty, the Broadpwn worm demonstration, misreported Facebook AI language news and Apple pulling VPN apps in China.
* [SN 623 - Inching Forward](../../sources/sn-0623.md) (2017-08-08) - Episode on DigiCert buying Symantec's certificate business, LastPass Premium's price increase, the arrest of Marcus Hutchins, TrickBot SMBv1 spreading, the US IoT Cybersecurity Improvement Act, Echo rooting, Mozilla Send and Hotspot Shield VPN spying.
* [SN 624 - Twelve and Counting](../../sources/sn-0624.md) (2017-08-15) - Year-12 finale covering the Marcus Hutchins case update, the history of NIST's password-rotation advice, debunked DNA-malware and stop-sign headlines, Microsoft distrusting WoSign/StartCom, TunnelBear's public audit and ElcomSoft's password-manager cracker.
* [SN 625 - Security Politics](../../sources/sn-0625.md) (2017-08-22) - Episode on the Marcus Hutchins case and code-attribution debate, the leaked Apple Secure Enclave firmware key, Chrome extension hijacks, the CAN bus design flaw, an ElcomSoft benchmark correction, Cloudflare's lava lamps and the iOS 11 Touch ID kill switch.
* [SN 626 - Shattering Trust](../../sources/sn-0626.md) (2017-08-29) - Episode on the collapsed Hutchins defense fund, the CIA ExpressLane document, Zerodium's rising prices, a deprecated macOS authorization API, disabling the Intel Management Engine via an NSA-linked flag, SIM hijacking and malicious replacement touchscreens.
* [SN 627 - Sharknado](../../sources/sn-0627.md) (2017-09-05) - Episode on SharknAT&To flaws in AT&T U-Verse modems, Brian Krebs's Hutchins research, DKIM verification of leaked email, formally verified HMAC-DRBG, the Abbott pacemaker recall, CIA AngelFire and Net Neutrality comment analysis.
* [SN 628 - The Equifax Fiasco](../../sources/sn-0628.md) (2017-09-12) - Equifax's breach of 143 million US consumers and its weak PINs, plus a D-Link router with ten zero-days, the Symantec CA sunset and Comodo ignoring CAA records.
* [SN 629 - Apple Bakes Cookies](../../sources/sn-0629.md) (2017-09-19) - Equifax follow-up, the EFF leaving the W3C over EME, the Payment Request API, SMS as a false second factor, the CCleaner breach, BlueBorne and iOS 11's Intelligent Tracking Prevention.
* [SN 630 - The Great DOM Fuzz-Off](../../sources/sn-0630.md) (2017-09-26) - Equifax speculation, NSA-sourced ciphers dropped from a standard, CCleaner's targeted second stage, ISP-delivered FinFisher, iOS 11 radios, the FTC v. D-Link ruling and Project Zero's DOM fuzzing of five browsers.
* [SN 631 - Private Contact Discovery](../../sources/sn-0631.md) (2017-10-03) - Face ID security design, browser cryptojacking, Bloomberg's account of the Equifax intrusion, DKIM spoofing, dnsmasq flaws, EFI firmware neglect and Signal's SGX-based private contact discovery.
* [SN 632 - The DNSSEC Challenge](../../sources/sn-0632.md) (2017-10-10) - Disqus's model breach response, Apple's APFS password-hint bug, the proposed security.txt file, XP TLS and Firefox end of life, and why DNSSEC and the ICANN root key rollover are still unfinished after 20 years.
* [SN 633 - KRACKing WiFi](../../sources/sn-0633.md) (2017-10-17) - ROCA weak RSA keys from Infineon chips, the economics of web cryptomining, iOS password-dialog spoofing, Google's Advanced Protection Program and a detailed explanation of the KRACK WPA2 key-reinstallation attack.
* [SN 634 - IoT Flash Botnets](../../sources/sn-0634.md) (2017-10-24) - ROCA smartcard fallout, the DUHK flaw in ANSI X9.31 random number generation, the Kaspersky transparency pledge, DNS over TLS for Android, a trojanized Mac player, browser mining and the two-million-device IoT Reaper botnet.
* [SN 635 - Reaper Redux](../../sources/sn-0635.md) (2017-10-31) - WannaCry attributed to North Korea, Firefox canvas fingerprint blocking, Chrome dropping HPKP, CAPTCHA breaking, the Coinhive DNS hijack, Bad Rabbit, Windows Controlled Folder Access and a calmer re-evaluation of the Reaper botnet.
* [SN 636 - ROCA Pain](../../sources/sn-0636.md) (2017-11-07) - Signed malware and weak antivirus signature checks, a login-state leak via favicons, Intel Management Engine and MINIX, a Tor IP leak and Tor v3, a Signal desktop app, an IoT update draft standard and a faster ROCA attack that forced Estonia to suspend its ID cards.
* [SN 637 - Schneier on Equifax](../../sources/sn-0637.md) (2017-11-14) - Face ID doubts, the AVGator quarantine-junction attack, WikiLeaks Vault 8 Hive, a USB-reachable JTAG interface in Intel chips, who is behind cryptojacking, WebAssembly, new Chrome anti-malvertising features and Bruce Schneier's congressional testimony on Equifax.
* [SN 638 - Quad Nine](../../sources/sn-0638.md) (2017-11-21) - Intel's response to the JTAG-over-USB finding and a firmware detection tool, exposed Pentagon cloud buckets, session replay scripts, Spain's 60 million Infineon ID cards, Amazon Key flaw, Firefox Quantum and the Quad9 DNS service.
* [SN 639 - News and Feedback](../../sources/sn-0639.md) (2017-11-28) - Breaking macOS High Sierra root login bug, critical Exim flaws, remotely exploitable HP enterprise printers, vulnerable JavaScript libraries, ComputerCOP spyware, Firefox breach warnings, and Quad9 listener feedback.
* [SN 640 - More News and Feedback](../../sources/sn-0640.md) (2017-12-05) - End of StartCom, anatomy of the macOS blank-root bug, Facebook's face-photo checks, Office DDE exploitation, hidden pop-under cryptominers, Mailsploit Unicode email spoofing, Chrome blocking code injection and Mirai on ZyXEL routers.
* [SN 641 - The iOS 11 Security Tradeoff](../../sources/sn-0641.md) (2017-12-12) - How researchers reached Intel ME over USB JTAG, Project Zero's iOS kernel debugging release, EV certificate social engineering, a fake MyEtherWallet app, ProtonMail Bridge, and ElcomSoft's critique of iOS 11 backup-password reset.
* [SN 642 - BGP](../../sources/sn-0642.md) (2017-12-19) - Estonia's elliptic-curve fix for Infineon ID cards, Loapi and Zealot cryptominers, vBulletin zero-days, Mozilla's Mr. Robot add-on, the Keeper bundling flaw, WPAD/PAC risks, Mirai's Minecraft origins, and an explainer on BGP and route hijacking.
* [SN 644 - NSA Fingerprints](../../sources/sn-0644.md) (2018-01-02) - Hidden login-autofill tracking scripts, the first public hints of the Intel kernel page-table isolation issue, a macOS kernel zero-day, Huawei router botnet exploitation, exposed Sonos web servers, and new evidence tying the NSA to the Dual EC / Extended Random backdoor.
* [SN 645 - The Speculation Meltdown](../../sources/sn-0645.md) (2018-01-09) - Steve's first full treatment of Meltdown and Spectre, plus Microsoft's antivirus-compatibility registry gate for the January 2018 updates, WD My Cloud hard-coded backdoors, the announcement of WPA3, and Let's Encrypt's 2017 results.
* [SN 646 - The InSpectre](../../sources/sn-0646.md) (2018-01-16) - Steve releases the InSpectre freeware checker for Meltdown and Spectre, alongside Intel AMT default-password concerns, Skype adopting the Signal protocol, UK researcher protections, the push to secure contexts, and Google's Retpoline.
