* [SN 647 - The Dark Caracal](../../sources/sn-0647.md) (2018-01-23) - Intel tells vendors to stop deploying its flawed Spectre microcode, Linus Torvalds rants, and the EFF/Lookout report exposes Dark Caracal, a Lebanese state-linked cross-platform espionage campaign.
* [SN 648 - Post Spectre?](../../sources/sn-0648.md) (2018-01-30) - Microsoft's out-of-band update to disable the Spectre variant 2 mitigation, Blizzard's mishandling of Tavis Ormandy, Tinder's missing encryption, Strava heat-map leaks, a record Coincheck heist, malicious ads, state-level Net Neutrality moves, and Steve's InSpectre updates.
* [SN 649 - Meltdown & Spectre Emerge](../../sources/sn-0649.md) (2018-02-06) - State-level Net Neutrality efforts, the Smominru EternalBlue Monero botnet, a new Flash zero-day from North Korea, the Autosploit tool, Firefox 59 referer trimming, and the first Meltdown/Spectre malware samples appearing in the wild.
* [SN 650 - Cryptocurrency Antics](../../sources/sn-0650.md) (2018-02-13) - Slow Intel Spectre firmware progress, a worse-than-expected Cisco ASA flaw, the iBoot source code leak, a Lenovo Broadcom Wi-Fi driver fix, WordPress auto-update glitch, and a string of cryptomining stories including the BrowseAloud script compromise.
* [SN 651 - Russian Meddling Technology](../../sources/sn-0651.md) (2018-02-20) - New MeltdownPrime/SpectrePrime attack classes, Intel lawsuits, New York bank cybersecurity rules, authorized browser mining via AuthedMine, mal-mining on Jenkins servers, NCC Group's EternalGlue test, and a technical look at the Russian social-media operation described in the Mueller indictment.
* [SN 652 - WebAssembly](../../sources/sn-0652.md) (2018-02-27) - Intel's Spectre/Meltdown microcode status, stealthy cryptojacking at Tesla, Tavis Ormandy's uTorrent findings, a Georgia bill criminalizing unauthorized access, Cellebrite's iPhone unlocking claims, Apple's China data move, forged code-signing certificates, a Cloudflare/Pwned Passwords integration, and an explainer on WebAssembly.
* [SN 653 - "MemCrashed" DDoS Attacks](../../sources/sn-0653.md) (2018-03-06) - Memcached reflection DDoS attacks reaching 1.7 Tbps, Microsoft delivering Intel microcode through Windows, ten new 4G LTE protocol attacks, the TLS 1.3 forward-secrecy fight, and the Trustico private-key fiasco.
* [SN 654 - AMD Chipset Disaster](../../sources/sn-0654.md) (2018-03-13) - CTS Labs' 24-hour-notice disclosure of 13 flaws in AMD's Ryzen and EPYC security processor and Promontory chipset, ISP-level malware injection with Sandvine devices, Windows 10 S mode, a heap of cryptojacking stories, Pwn2Own without Chinese teams, and week-two memcached DDoS follow-up.
* [SN 655 - Pwn2Own 2018](../../sources/sn-0655.md) (2018-03-20) - Aftermath of CTS Labs' AMD disclosure, Intel's plans for hardware Spectre/Meltdown fixes, Microsoft's expanding microcode updates and side-channel bounty, VPN testing failures, the first pedestrian death involving a self-driving car, and the results of Pwn2Own 2018 without Chinese teams.
* [SN 656 - TLS v1.3](../../sources/sn-0656.md) (2018-03-27) - US election-system oversight, the Symantec 2018 threat report, Netflix and Dropbox bug-bounty moves, the CLOUD Act, Facebook's Android call-log collection, a critical Drupal update, DNS over HTTPS in Firefox, and the ratification of TLS 1.3.
* [SN 657 - ProtonMail](../../sources/sn-0657.md) (2018-04-03) - Drupalgeddon2 fallout, Cloudflare's 1.1.1.1 resolver benchmarked with GRC's DNS Benchmark, a Windows 7 x64 Meltdown patch permission blunder, the unfixed iOS QR code spoof, WebRTC VPN leaks, Hack the Pentagon bounties, the BIMI logo-in-email proposal, and a walkthrough of ProtonMail's design.
* [SN 658 - Deprecating TLS 1.0 & 1.1](../../sources/sn-0658.md) (2018-04-10) - Intel stops Spectre microcode for some old CPUs, Telegram faces a Russian ban, bug-bounty downsides, Quad9 vs Cloudflare DNS follow-up, and the industry wind-down of TLS 1.0/1.1.
* [SN 659 - Never a Dull Moment](../../sources/sn-0659.md) (2018-04-17) - AMD Spectre v2 microcode, Telegram blocked in Russia, Drupalgeddon2 attacks, UPnProxy router abuse, Android patch-level misreporting, Chrome HTTP cookie limits, and an aquarium-thermometer casino breach.
* [SN 660 - Azure Sphere](../../sources/sn-0660.md) (2018-04-24) - Drupalgeddon2 fallout, the Orangeworm campaign against healthcare, FDA software bill of materials plans, the Cybersecurity Tech Accord, consumer router neglect, Google's unencrypted RCS chat, and Microsoft's Azure Sphere IoT security platform.
* [SN 661 - Securing Connected Things](../../sources/sn-0661.md) (2018-05-01) - Windows 10 1803 release problems, Microsoft Spectre microcode updates, a Golf connected-car hack, Oracle WebLogic patch bypass, PDF NTLM hash leaks, Echo skill eavesdropping, a hotel keycard master-key attack, and Microsoft's Trusted Cyber-Physical Systems proposal.
* [SN 662 - Spectre - NextGen](../../sources/sn-0662.md) (2018-05-08) - Eight new Spectre-NG flaws reported, domain fronting shut down, Drupalgeddon2 compromised-site list, GLitch GPU Rowhammer on Android, Twitter's plaintext password logging, Android P features and GDPR.
* [SN 663 - Ultra-Clever Attacks](../../sources/sn-0663.md) (2018-05-15) - The eFail attack on PGP and S/MIME email, the Throwhammer network-based Rowhammer attack, UPnProxy evolution, an Electron framework flaw, EternalBlue anniversary, GPON botnets and the first persistent IoT botnet.
* [SN 664 - SpectreNG Revealed](../../sources/sn-0664.md) (2018-05-22) - Spectre variants 3a and 4 (speculative store bypass) disclosed with a 2-8% performance cost if mitigated, plus Signal desktop XSS, DNS hijacking of routers, voice-assistant skill squatting, and Chrome's HTTP warnings.
* [SN 665 - VPNFilter](../../sources/sn-0665.md) (2018-05-29) - The 500,000-router VPNFilter botnet attributed to APT28 and the FBI domain seizure, plus Java's planned end of serialization, Ghostery's GDPR email blunder, BackSwap banking trojan, Echo fuzzy-match recording, Z-Wave downgrade hype and an npm HTTP 418 error.
* [SN 666 - Certificate Transparency](../../sources/sn-0666.md) (2018-06-05) - Apple's WWDC privacy changes, CSS blend-mode cross-origin pixel leak, Microsoft post-quantum OpenVPN, an unpatched JScript zero-day, Steam client RCE, exposed Redis servers, Chrome dropping HPKP, and an introduction to Certificate Transparency.
* [SN 667 - Zippity Do or Don't](../../sources/sn-0667.md) (2018-06-12) - VPNFilter grows to 71 router models, Operation Prowli, Drupalgeddon2 patch status, wormable open Android Debug Bridge, Cisco hardcoded backdoors, stealthier cryptominers, MyHeritage breach, a Flash zero-day, and the Zip Slip archive-extraction flaw.
* [SN 668 - Lazy FP State Restore](../../sources/sn-0668.md) (2018-06-19) - A large June Patch Tuesday (DNS and HTTP.sys remote code execution), Cortana lock-screen abuse, Microsoft's security servicing criteria, the Zacinlo adware rootkit, Satori's exploit-chasing, clipboard-hijacking malware, Chrome's end of inline extension installs, and the Lazy FPU state restore Intel flaw.
* [SN 669 - Cellular Location Privacy](../../sources/sn-0669.md) (2018-06-26) - TLBleed hyperthreading side channel and OpenBSD's response, the Mandiant "hack back" controversy, WebAssembly and shared-array-buffer timing, Firebase misconfiguration leaks, converting a dead XP machine to a VM, and the Carpenter Supreme Court ruling on cell-site location records.
* [SN 670 - Wi-Fi Protected Access v3](../../sources/sn-0670.md) (2018-07-03) - A zero-day PDF found on VirusTotal, the aLTEr attack on unauthenticated LTE radio links, partially defeated browser Spectre mitigations, UPnP-based randomization of DDoS source ports, a WordPress path-traversal bug, and the announced WPA3 features.
* [SN 671 - STARTTLS Everywhere](../../sources/sn-0671.md) (2018-07-10) - Fortnite cheat malware installing a rogue root certificate, Polar fitness map leaks, browser-freeze tech-support scams, picky Rakhni malware, D-Link's lost code-signing certificate, iOS USB Restricted Mode, unwiped memory cards, and the EFF's STARTTLS Everywhere email initiative.
* [SN 672 - All Up in Their Business](../../sources/sn-0672.md) (2018-07-17) - New Spectre 1.1/1.2 variants, GPS spoofing of navigation, Chrome strict site isolation, Netgear default-FTP router hacks, Bitcanal BGP hijacking, and the DOJ indictment of 12 GRU officers.
* [SN 673 - The Data Transfer Project](../../sources/sn-0673.md) (2018-07-24) - SpectreRSB, RAMpage and ZebRAM, leaked Exobot banking-trojan source, DNS rebinding revival, public-by-default Venmo, flash botnets, a fifth Cisco backdoor, and the Data Transfer Project.
* [SN 674 - Attacking Bluetooth Pairing](../../sources/sn-0674.md) (2018-07-31) - NetSpectre remote speculation attack, the Death botnet, DOD HTTPS gaps and Chrome 68 warnings, cloned-TLD sites, Google's ban on mining apps, a font-package supply-chain miner, Titan keys, and the Bluetooth invalid-curve pairing flaw.
* [SN 675 - New WiFi Password Attack](../../sources/sn-0675.md) (2018-08-07) - MikroTik router mass compromise injecting Coinhive, Reddit's SMS 2FA breach, WannaCry halting TSMC, targeted BGP plus DNS-cache hijacks, SamSam ransomware, and the Hashcat PMKID attack on WPA2 passwords.
* [SN 676 - The Mega FaxSploit](../../sources/sn-0676.md) (2018-08-14) - Black Hat and DEF CON 2018 roundup (Android firmware flaws, man-in-the-disk, VIA C3 Rosenbridge, WhatsApp spoofing, macOS click bug) and the Check Point fax-line attack on HP all-in-one printers.
* [SN 677 - The Foreshadow Flaw](../../sources/sn-0677.md) (2018-08-21) - Foreshadow/L1TF Intel speculation flaw, a 60-fix Patch Tuesday, Skype Signal-protocol chats, Chrome blocking code injection, Microsoft domain takedowns, ReDoS regular-expression attacks, and Coinhive earnings.
* [SN 678 - Never a Dull Moment](../../sources/sn-0678.md) (2018-08-28) - Symantec certificate distrust, BygoneSSL second-hand domain certificates, the Fortnite Android installer flaw, a new Apache Struts RCE, Sora Mirai, a Windows ALPC zero day, Turla email C2, Ghostscript bugs, EM-based RSA key extraction, OpenSSH username enumeration, and Android AT commands.
* [SN 679 - SonarSnoop](../../sources/sn-0679.md) (2018-09-04) - Struts exploitation after the new RCE, a micropatch for the Windows task-scheduler zero day, Android pre-Pie broadcast leaks, Instagram dropping SMS 2FA, GDPR blocking by US news sites, Misfortune Cookie in medical gateways, Magento skimmers, a TPM 2.0 sleep-state flaw, CVE funding, Firefox tracker blocking, and the SonarSnoop acoustic attack.
* [SN 680 - Exploits & Updates](../../sources/sn-0680.md) (2018-09-11) - Windows 7 paid extended updates, MikroTik routers sniffing traffic, Chrome 69 URL changes and password generator, the hacked MEGA Chrome extension, PowerPool exploiting the ALPC zero day, Vodafone's 1234 passwords, Tesla's research policy and fob cloning, CroniX Struts miner, OpenVPN plugin risk, and Trend Micro's Mac apps.
* [SN 681 - The Browser Extension Ecosystem](../../sources/sn-0681.md) (2018-09-18) - Presidential Alert test, Chrome's www-hiding rollback, Comodo cold-calling Let's Encrypt users, BGP route origin validation standards, California IoT bill, ice-cold-boot BitLocker attack, Safari Ripper crash, DDoS growth, and the browser extension ecosystem.
* [SN 682 - SNI Encryption](../../sources/sn-0682.md) (2018-09-25) - Chrome UI and auto-login controversies, Xbash cross-platform malware, a published Jet database zero day, ReaperBugs browser crashes, Western Digital My Cloud authentication bypass, unwiped NCIX drives, the Equifax UK fine, and Cloudflare's encrypted SNI.
* [SN 683 - The Facebook Breach](../../sources/sn-0683.md) (2018-10-02) - Cloudflare at-cost domains, DOJ suit over California net neutrality, Python malware trends, VPNFilter's final report, Chrome cookie-clearing controversy, the first in-the-wild UEFI rootkit, Torii botnet, RDP warnings, notification-permission tricks, and Facebook's 50-million-account View As token breach.
* [SN 684 - The Supply Chain](../../sources/sn-0684.md) (2018-10-09) - Windows 10 1809 file-deletion fiasco, chained MikroTik exploit proof of concept, WhatsApp voicemail account takeover, the Google+ API bug, Chrome extension policy tightening, WiFi 6 naming, Intel 9th-gen mitigations, PDF reader patch floods, and the Bloomberg Supermicro hardware implant story.
* [SN 685 - Good Samaritans?](../../sources/sn-0685.md) (2018-10-16) - A vigilante patching MikroTik routers, the Supermicro report fallout, Microsoft's October patching problems and a still-unfixed Jet flaw, Facebook's revised breach numbers, TLS 1.0/1.1 retirement, Android control-flow integrity, DOD weapon cyber weakness, PHP 5 end of life, and AV test results.
* [SN 686 - Libssh's Big Whoopsie!](../../sources/sn-0686.md) (2018-10-23) - FreeRTOS TCP/IP stack flaws, unpatched D-Link routers, Drupal and LIVE555 bugs, the jQuery File Upload zero-day, a hijacked abandoned S3 bucket, and the libssh authentication bypass.
* [SN 687 - Securing the Vending Machine](../../sources/sn-0687.md) (2018-10-30) - Windows 10 zip-copy bug follow-up, Google's Android patching contract terms, SandboxEscaper's new Windows zero-day, Hadoop DemonBot, exposed Docker APIs, DDoS-for-hire pricing, Firefox 63 tracking protection, and the vending-machine puzzler answer.
* [SN 688 - PortSmash](../../sources/sn-0688.md) (2018-11-06) - BleedingBit Bluetooth flaws in TI chips, Google requiring JavaScript for sign-in, an Edge zero-day, Windows Defender sandboxing, reCAPTCHA v3, the 30th anniversary of the Morris Worm, and the PortSmash hyper-threading side channel.
* [SN 689 - Self-Decrypting Drives](../../sources/sn-0689.md) (2018-11-13) - Radboud University research showing flawed hardware encryption in Crucial and Samsung SSDs (and BitLocker's reliance on it), plus Privacy International GDPR complaints, the UPnP Hunter botnet, a VirtualBox zero-day and Cloudflare's 1.1.1.1 app.
* [SN 690 - Are Passwords Immortal?](../../sources/sn-0690.md) (2018-11-20) - Pwn2Own Mobile Tokyo results, the systematization of transient-execution (Spectre/Meltdown) attacks, a boot-time Intel microcode workaround, Firefox Monitor, HTTP/3 over QUIC, and Steve's response to Troy Hunt on why passwords persist.
* [SN 691 - ECCploit](../../sources/sn-0691.md) (2018-11-27) - Apple's App Store monopoly case at the US Supreme Court, browsers considering dropping FTP, Google's proposed Writable Files API, listener feedback on SQRL after the passwords debate, and the ECCploit Rowhammer attack against ECC memory.
* [SN 692 - GPU RAM Image Leakage](../../sources/sn-0692.md) (2018-12-04) - Sennheiser's Superfish-style root certificate blunder, the Marriott/Starwood, Quora and Data & Leads breaches, UPnProxy "EternalSilence" router abuse, printer spam, SamSam ransom proceeds, Mozilla DoH tests, Edge moving to Chromium, and GPU memory leaking web page images.
* [SN 693 - Internal Bug Discovery](../../sources/sn-0693.md) (2018-12-11) - Australia's Assistance and Access encryption bill, a 12-state HIPAA suit against a medical records firm, more mega data exposures, Microsoft's call to regulate facial recognition, Tor funding, the CoAP DDoS amplification risk, and Google's self-found Google+ API bug.
* [SN 694 - The SQLite RCE Flaw](../../sources/sn-0694.md) (2018-12-18) - Year-end episode on the Magellan SQLite remote code execution bug, a lawsuit over Google's disclosed API flaw, Signal's response to Australia's encryption law, PewDiePie printer hacks, CBP retaining traveler data, an Android PayPal trojan, and Firefox 64.
* [SN 696 - Here Comes 2019!](../../sources/sn-0696.md) (2019-01-08) - NSA's planned release of the Ghidra reverse engineering tool, the Chromecast "CastHack" via UPnP, an out-of-cycle IE zero-day patch, SandboxEscaper's latest zero-day, No More Ransom decryptors, Windows Sandbox, and SQRL's service provider API.
* [SN 697 - Zerodium](../../sources/sn-0697.md) (2019-01-15) - January 2019 Patch Tuesday fallout, expired government TLS certificates during a shutdown, WhatsApp number-reassignment leak, DNS over TLS from Google, cryptomining malware economics, a 51% attack on Ethereum Classic, a court ruling on biometric unlocking, Flash's end, and Zerodium's sharply raised exploit payouts.
* [SN 698 - Which Mobile VPN Client?](../../sources/sn-0698.md) (2019-01-22) - Marvell Avastar WiFi chip zero-click flaw, Tesla Model 3 added to Pwn2Own, Russia's failed blocking efforts, Anubis banking malware evading sandboxes, Google tightening SMS and call-log permissions, Tim Cook on data brokers, a Cisco default-account flaw, and the case for the OpenVPN for Android client.
* [SN 699 - Browser Extension Security](../../sources/sn-0699.md) (2019-01-29) - A DNS hijacking campaign prompting a CISA emergency directive, iOS and macOS updates and the FaceTime bug, URLhaus malware-site takedowns, Chrome blocking sandboxed iframe downloads, exploited Cisco RV320/RV325 routers, and Chrome Manifest v3 plans versus extension security research.
* [SN 0700 - 700 & Counting](../../sources/sn-0700.md) (2019-02-05) - Chrome lookalike-URL suggestions, systemd-journald exploit, Facebook's paid root-certificate VPN app, Japan's planned IoT credential scan, Firefox 65 vs antivirus root certificates, and Ubiquiti port 10001 abuse.
* [SN 0701 - Adiantum](../../sources/sn-0701.md) (2019-02-12) - iOS 12.1.4 zero-days, Android PNG flaws, reverse-RDP client attacks, LibreOffice path traversal, Microsoft's 70% memory-safety statistic, Russia's Runet disconnection test and Google's Adiantum disk-encryption mode.
* [SN 0702 - Authenticity on the Internet](../../sources/sn-0702.md) (2019-02-19) - February 2019 Patch Tuesday, SHA-2-only Windows 7 updates, Monero-mining Store apps, Trusted Types, Xiaomi scooter hijacking, Tor app fingerprinting, the missing Equifax data, GAO privacy report, and OpenAI's withheld GPT-2.
* [SN 0703 - Out in the Wild](../../sources/sn-0703.md) (2019-02-26) - Drupal remote-code-execution flaw exploited within days, WinRAR dropping ACE support, an IIS HTTP/2 denial of service, DarkMatter's root-store request, Edge's hidden Flash whitelist, ICANN's DNSSEC plea and Apple's ITP 2.1.
* [SN 0704 - Careers in Bug Hunting](../../sources/sn-0704.md) (2019-03-05) - Retpoline for Spectre variant 2 on Windows 10 1809, ColdFusion zero-day, Kaspersky vs Chromecast self-signed certificates, the runc Docker breakout, Coinhive shutting down, Wireshark 3.0, Thunderclap DMA attacks, credential-stuffing stats and bug bounty careers.
* [SN 0705 - Spoiler](../../sources/sn-0705.md) (2019-03-12) - Zerodium's $500k hypervisor bounty, NSA's Ghidra release, Firefox letterboxing, a Chrome plus Windows 7 zero-day pair, Citrix breach, hackable car alarms, Marriott/Starwood breach forensics and the SPOILER Intel speculation attack.
* [SN 0706 - Open Source eVoting](../../sources/sn-0706.md) (2019-03-19) - March 2019 Patch Tuesday and the win32k zero-days, WinRAR ACE exploitation, 63-bit certificate serial numbers affecting GoDaddy, Apple and Google, a stronger Mirai, malicious Counter Strike servers, Android Q privacy, Application Guard extensions and DARPA's open-source voting system.
* [SN 0707 - Tesla, Pwned](../../sources/sn-0707.md) (2019-03-26) - ASUS Live Update supply-chain attack (ShadowHammer), Norsk Hydro LockerGoga ransomware, TCPDF deserialization flaw, SHAKEN/STIR caller authentication, Microsoft Defender ATP for Mac and Pwn2Own Vancouver 2019 including a Tesla Model 3 infotainment hack.
* [SN 0708 - Android Security](../../sources/sn-0708.md) (2019-04-02) - Google's Android Security 2018 report at the platform's 10th birthday, plus WinRAR exploit campaigns, Russian GPS spoofing research, Tesla Autopilot adversarial tests and the cracked ASUS ShadowHammer MAC address list.
* [SN 0709 - URL "Ping" Tracking](../../sources/sn-0709.md) (2019-04-09) - Hyperlink ping tracking becoming permanent in Chrome, official Chromium-based Edge, the UK Online Harms White Paper, spoofed Galaxy S10 fingerprints, weak Android financial apps, Ghidra's source release, Facebook asking for email passwords and Windows 10 1809's new removable-drive caching default.
* [SN 0710 - Dragonblood](../../sources/sn-0710.md) (2019-04-16) - Ping-attribute DDoS abuse, continued WinRAR exploitation, April 2019 Patch Tuesday antivirus conflicts and zero-days, Chrome's plan to block insecure executable downloads, the TajMahal APT framework, and the Dragonblood attacks on WPA3.
* [SN 0711 - DNSpionage](../../sources/sn-0711.md) (2019-04-23) - Google Sensorvault geofence warrants, Facebook's email-password and contact harvesting, Russia's Internet cutoff bill, USB Killer, Marcus Hutchins's plea, Edge changes, Mozilla enabling link ping, and Cisco Talos's report on the Sea Turtle DNS hijacking campaign.
* [SN 0712 - Credential Stuffing Attacks](../../sources/sn-0712.md) (2019-04-30) - Facebook's FTC provision and Congress's Sensorvault letter, slow Windows 10 1809 uptake, the CSRSS update behind April's antivirus failures, NIST fuzzing, an unattributed 80-million-household database, and Recorded Future's economics of credential stuffing.
* [SN 0713 - Post-Coinhive Cryptojacking](../../sources/sn-0713.md) (2019-05-07) - Mozilla's expired add-on signing certificate, Google's auto-delete location controls, the zero-day seller Volodya, Mozilla banning obfuscated extensions, a hacker raiding botnet servers, the unencrypted D-Link DCS-2132L camera, Edge compatibility issues and the state of cryptojacking after Coinhive.
* [SN 0714 - Android "Q"](../../sources/sn-0714.md) (2019-05-14) - WhatsApp VoIP remote-code-execution flaw, Chrome's SameSite cookie default change, a Linux RDS kernel flaw, Intel ZombieLoad, and Android Q security hardening.
* [SN 0715 - CPU.fail](../../sources/sn-0715.md) (2019-05-21) - Microarchitectural Data Sampling (ZombieLoad, RIDL, Fallout), the wormable BlueKeep-class RDP flaw patched even for Windows XP, Cisco Thrangrycat secure-boot bypass, and the Google Titan Bluetooth key flaw.
* [SN 0716 - RDP:  Really Do Patch](../../sources/sn-0716.md) (2019-05-28) - BlueKeep (CVE-2019-0708) exploit confirmations and Internet scanning, SandboxEscaper's Windows zero-day dump, Google's 14-year plaintext G Suite password bug, Windows 10 1903, Firefox 67, and validly signed malware.
* [SN 0717 - The Nansh0u Campaign](../../sources/sn-0717.md) (2019-06-04) - BlueKeep outlook (cryptominers rather than worms), GandCrab ransomware-as-a-service shutting down, anti-robocall law and SHAKEN/STIR, Russia and China moving off Windows, Sign in with Apple, and Guardicore's Nansh0u MS-SQL cryptomining campaign.
* [SN 0718 - Update Exim Now!](../../sources/sn-0718.md) (2019-06-11) - Critical Exim mail server command-execution flaw (CVE-2019-10149) found by Qualys, the SQRL 1.0 release, SandboxEscaper's latest bypass, BlueKeep updates, the GoldBrute RDP botnet, and an FBI warning about HTTPS phishing.
* [SN 0719 - Exim Under Siege](../../sources/sn-0719.md) (2019-06-18) - The Exim worm exploiting CVE-2019-10149, RAMBleed, the Linux TCP SACK kernel-panic flaw, a Windows Bluetooth hardcoded-key block, a missed Project Zero deadline, GandCrab keys released, and Yubico FIPS key entropy bug.
* [SN 0720 - Bug Bounty Business](../../sources/sn-0720.md) (2019-06-25) - Firefox zero-days used against Coinbase, a BGP route leak via Verizon that rerouted a slice of the Internet through a steel mill, OpenSSH key shielding against RAMBleed, NASA JPL's Raspberry Pi intrusion, ransomware payment costs, and the HackerOne bug bounty business.
* [SN 0721 - Exposed Cloud Databases](../../sources/sn-0721.md) (2019-07-02) - Municipal Ryuk ransomware in Florida and Georgia, the Trump administration's "going dark" encryption debate, a Sophos BlueKeep demo, and three exposed cloud databases including Orvibo's two-billion-record smart home log.
* [SN 0722 - Gem Hack & Ghost Protocol](../../sources/sn-0722.md) (2019-07-09) - Backlash against Mozilla's DNS-over-HTTPS, facial recognition and license plate scanning concerns, satellite Internet constellations, a hijacked Ruby gem (strong_password), and GCHQ's "ghost" participant proposal for encrypted chats.
* [SN 0723 - Encrypting DNS](../../sources/sn-0723.md) (2019-07-16) - July Patch Tuesday and a Windows 7 telemetry annoyance, LaPorte County Ryuk ransomware and insurers, Marriott and British Airways GDPR fines, Mozilla distrusting DarkMatter, keyboard-LED data exfiltration, and an overview of DNSSEC, DNSCrypt, DNS over TLS and DNS over HTTPS.
* [SN 0724 - Hide Your RDP Now!](../../sources/sn-0724.md) (2019-07-23) - Kazakhstan's government-mandated HTTPS interception root certificate, Firefox and Chrome privacy changes, Rustls, Microsoft ElectionGuard, exposed ProFTPD servers, and Sophos RDP honeypot data showing relentless password guessing.
* [SN 0725 - Urgent/11](../../sources/sn-0725.md) (2019-07-30) - Marcus Hutchins sentenced to time served, Attorney General Barr on warrant-proof encryption, underground malware popularity, school-district ransomware in Louisiana, attacks on exposed NAS devices, and the Urgent/11 flaws in the VxWorks network stack.
* [SN 0726 - Steve's File Sync Journey](../../sources/sn-0726.md) (2019-08-06) - A debunked Forbes claim about WhatsApp backdoors, more Georgia ransomware, Five Eyes calls for encryption access, Russian APT28 using IoT devices as footholds and VLAN segmentation, Chrome 76 changes, and Steve's search for a reliable file sync tool.
* [SN 0727 - Black Hat and DEF CON](../../sources/sn-0727.md) (2019-08-13) - Black Hat and DEF CON highlights: Microsoft and Apple expanded bug bounties (Apple up to 1 million USD), a hotel mobile-key design flaw, Eclypsium's insecure signed kernel drivers, Chrome's incognito-detection cat-and-mouse, plus Syncthing and file sync feedback.
* [SN 0728 - The KNOB Is Broken](../../sources/sn-0728.md) (2019-08-20) - August 2019 Patch Tuesday colliding with Symantec/Norton AV, Kaspersky user tracking, the CTF protocol flaw, Texas municipal ransomware, EV certificate demise, Netflix's HTTP/2 findings and the Bluetooth KNOB attack.
* [SN 0729 - Next Gen Ad Privacy](../../sources/sn-0729.md) (2019-08-27) - Texas ransomware reinterpreted as an outsourced-IT breach, Kazakhstan's blocked root CA, Webmin and RubyGems backdoors, Chrome breach notification, and Google's Privacy Sandbox proposals.
* [SN 0730 - The Ransomware Epidemic](../../sources/sn-0730.md) (2019-09-03) - Google's expanded bug bounties, Project Zero's analysis of a multi-year iOS watering-hole campaign, and a long look at the 2019 ransomware wave, Sodinokibi/REvil and cyber insurance.
* [SN 0731 - DeepFakes](../../sources/sn-0731.md) (2019-09-10) - Flagstaff school closures and ransomware lessons, Apple's pushback on Project Zero's iOS report, Zerodium's new payouts, BlueKeep exploit code, PHP updates, and a first look at deepfake media and detection efforts.
* [SN 0732 - Simjacking](../../sources/sn-0732.md) (2019-09-17) - DNS over HTTPS plans in Chrome and Firefox, Chrome dropping EV indicators, iOS 13 lockscreen bug, Firefox Private Network, September Patch Tuesday, an Exim TLS flaw, and the Simjacker SIM Toolkit attack.
* [SN 0733 - Top 25 Bug Classes](../../sources/sn-0733.md) (2019-09-24) - Monero cryptomining resurgence, an SMB threat report (SSH, unsupported Windows, old Linux kernels), SOHOpelessly Broken 2.0 router/NAS results, an urgent Chrome update, and MITRE's 2019 CWE Top 25.
* [SN 0734 - The Joy of Sync](../../sources/sn-0734.md) (2019-10-01) - Mozilla's staged DNS-over-HTTPS rollout and the EFF's view, followed by Steve's conclusions on secure file synchronization with Sync.com, Syncthing and Cryptomator.
* [SN 0735 - Makes Ya WannaCry](../../sources/sn-0735.md) (2019-10-08) - Ransomware roundup (schools, Demant, DCH hospitals, Victorian hospitals), GandCrab-to-Sodinokibi continuity, Windows 7 ESU for small businesses, a vBulletin zero-day, and why WannaCry variants never re-exploded.
* [SN 0736 - CheckM8](../../sources/sn-0736.md) (2019-10-15) - A hardware implant proof of concept, the governments-versus-Facebook encryption letter, cracking of historic Unix passwords, photo geolocation risks, OpenPGP in Thunderbird, Tamper Protection, and the unpatchable checkm8 Apple boot ROM exploit.
* [SN 0737 - Biometric Mess](../../sources/sn-0737.md) (2019-10-22) - Checkm8 jailbreak progress, WAV-file steganography by malware, Firefox 70 UI and security changes, Microsoft ElectionGuard bounty, a Realtek Linux WiFi driver overflow, YubiKey Windows login, and the Pixel 4 and Galaxy S10 biometric failures.
* [SN 0738 - A Foregone Conclusion](../../sources/sn-0738.md) (2019-10-29) - Chrome 78 colliding with old Symantec Endpoint Protection, Windows Defender Offline Scan, an Adobe database exposure, Johannesburg's data-theft extortion, Nginx/PHP-FPM remote code execution, RCS plans, and an Oregon ruling that a phone passcode can be compelled.
* [SN 0739 - BlueKeep and DoH](../../sources/sn-0739.md) (2019-11-05) - A Windows TLS handshake enforcement breaking Chrome connections, the Chrome WizardOpium zero-day, QSnatch on QNAP NAS devices, MSP-driven ransomware, a rental-car app flaw, Comcast's DoH lobbying and the first BlueKeep attacks.
* [SN 0740 - Credential Delegation](../../sources/sn-0740.md) (2019-11-12) - Checkra1n public beta, laser injection of voice commands, BlueKeep exploit reliability and Marcus Hutchins' worm analysis, Mozilla's reply to Comcast on DoH, browser DoH support, a libarchive flaw, and TLS Delegated Credentials.
* [SN 0741 - TPM-FAIL](../../sources/sn-0741.md) (2019-11-19) - November Patch Tuesday and Windows 7's last patches, checkra1n maturing, GitHub Security Lab, a US court limiting border device searches, another WhatsApp flaw, ZombieLoad v2, the Bytecode Alliance, and timing attacks on TPM 2.0 chips (TPM-Fail).
* [SN 0742 - Pushing DoH](../../sources/sn-0742.md) (2019-11-26) - Android kernel overhaul and pre-installed-app flaws, VNC vulnerabilities, Twitter dropping mandatory SMS 2FA, a Pennsylvania ruling on compelled passwords, and Microsoft's plan for DNS-over-HTTPS with server-pushed DNS.
* [SN 0743 - Android StrandHogg](../../sources/sn-0743.md) (2019-12-03) - Free Windows 10 upgrades still working, an HPE SSD 32,768-hour firmware bug, EU-US encryption tension, CISA's vulnerability disclosure directive, the VCPI Ryuk attack, Firefox anti-fingerprinting, DLL hijack bugs, and the StrandHogg Android task-hijacking malware.
* [SN 0744 - VPN-geddon Denied](../../sources/sn-0744.md) (2019-12-10) - Forced Windows 10 feature updates, a BypassESU hack for Windows 7, Microsoft's Rust experiments, Avast/AVG extensions harvesting browsing data, WireGuard heading into Linux, and why the "VPN hijack" headlines were overblown.
* [SN 0745 - Plundervolt](../../sources/sn-0745.md) (2019-12-17) - Google Sensorvault geofence warrants, Google's Verified SMS, the Senate encryption hearing, iOS 13.3 security-key support, the RSA shared-prime survey of IoT certificates, Chrome and Windows news, Steve handing SQRL off, and the Plundervolt undervolting attack on Intel SGX.
* [SN 0746 - A Decade of Hacks](../../sources/sn-0746.md) (2019-12-23) - Year-end episode covering SQRL login on TWiT's forum, Microsoft Security Essentials confusion, 2019 school ransomware tally, Apple and Google bounty changes, NextDNS in Firefox, a Drupal patch, and a 2010-2019 retrospective of major hacks.
* [SN 0748 - Our Malware Lexicon](../../sources/sn-0748.md) (2020-01-07) - A proposed machine-readable warrant canary standard, Xiaomi camera feeds leaking into Nest Hubs, Microsoft's RDP brute-force study, point-of-sale breaches, Russia's Runet disconnect test, Python 2.7 end of life, HackerOne's top payers, SQLite Magellan 2.0, and a seven-category malware lexicon.
* [SN 0749 - Windows 7 - R.I.P.](../../sources/sn-0749.md) (2020-01-14) - Windows 7's final Patch Tuesday and a Crypt32 flaw, the FBI asking Apple to unlock another iPhone, Princeton research on SIM-swap authentication, unpatched Pulse Secure VPNs leading to ransomware, and the Cable Haunt flaw in Broadcom-based cable modems.
* [SN 0750 - The Crypto CurveBall](../../sources/sn-0750.md) (2020-01-21) - iPhones as Google security keys, Barr versus Apple, an Internet Explorer JScript zero-day, Chromium Edge, Pulse Secure VPN attacks attributed to nation-states, a new RD Gateway RCE, and the CryptoAPI CurveBall certificate-spoofing flaw reported by the NSA.
* [SN 0751 - SHAmbles](../../sources/sn-0751.md) (2020-01-28) - Apple's iCloud backup encryption dropped after FBI objections, a Microsoft support database exposure, New York ransomware-payment bills, Tomato router attacks, extension store crackdowns, NIST's privacy framework, a leaked telnet credential list, a 0patch IE micropatch, and the first practical SHA-1 chosen-prefix collision.
* [SN 0752 - The Little Red Wagon](../../sources/sn-0752.md) (2020-02-04) - CacheOut (L1D eviction sampling) on Intel CPUs, Windows 10's moving-target versions, a petition to open-source Windows 7, the BlueGate RDG exploit, Google's record bounties, Facebook's biometric settlement, Clearview AI, Avast shutting down Jumpshot, WireGuard in Linux 5.6, and a Google Maps traffic-jam hack with a wagon of phones.
* [SN 0753 - Promiscuous Cookies](../../sources/sn-0753.md) (2020-02-11) - Security Essentials updates continuing on Windows 7, more Windows 7 update breakage, an Android Bluetooth remote code execution bug, Clearview AI cease-and-desists and scraping law, NIST forensics on destroyed phones, screen-brightness exfiltration, the Crypto AG revelation, and Chrome 80's SameSite cookie change.
* [SN 0754 - The Internet of Troubles](../../sources/sn-0754.md) (2020-02-18) - February Windows update failures traced partly to Adobe services, a critical GDPR cookie plugin flaw for WordPress, a survey of CISO stress, Microsoft's ElectionGuard pilot, the Philips Hue Zigbee bulb-to-bridge attack, and the SweynTooth Bluetooth Low Energy vulnerabilities.
* [SN 0755 - Apple's Cert Surprise](../../sources/sn-0755.md) (2020-02-25) - Windows 10 February rollup data-loss reports, Windows 2004 optional driver updates, the IMP4GT impersonation attack on 4G and 5G, Firefox DoH default and RLBox sandboxing, a Chrome zero-day, SpinRite AHCI plans, and Apple's unilateral 398-day TLS certificate limit.
* [SN 0756 - Kr00k](../../sources/sn-0756.md) (2020-03-03) - Let's Encrypt's billionth certificate, Pakistan's content rules, Clearview AI's leaked customer list, Swiss fallout from Crypto AG, the Ghostcat Tomcat flaw, why OCSP Must-Staple was the better answer, and the Kr00k WiFi flaw in Broadcom and Cypress chips.
* [SN 0757 - The Fuzzy Bench](../../sources/sn-0757.md) (2020-03-10) - Free pandemic-era telework offers, the DOD vulnerability disclosure program's results, Android update neglect, AMD cache way-predictor attacks, an unfixable Intel CSME boot ROM flaw, a pppd overflow, TLS 1.0/1.1 removal and Google's FuzzBench.
* [SN 0758 - The SMBGhost Fiasco](../../sources/sn-0758.md) (2020-03-17) - March 2020 Patch Tuesday aftermath, the leaked and then rushed SMBv3 compression bug (SMBGhost), open source vulnerability statistics and the EARN IT act aimed at encryption.
* [SN 0759 - TRRespass](../../sources/sn-0759.md) (2020-03-24) - An unpatched Adobe Type Manager font zero-day in all Windows versions, Mozilla pausing TLS 1.0/1.1 removal, 0patch micropatches, Chrome release pause, Avast JavaScript emulator flaw, CookieThief, virtual Pwn2Own and the TRRespass research showing Rowhammer still works on DDR4.
* [SN 0760 - Folding Proteins](../../sources/sn-0760.md) (2020-03-31) - Surging RDP and VPN exposure during lockdowns, an iOS VPN leak, Chrome URL and Firefox HTTPS-only changes, Intel CET shadow stacks in Windows 10, the OpenWRT package-verification flaw, a Cloudflare 1.1.1.1 audit and Folding@home.
* [SN 0761 - Zoom Go Boom!](../../sources/sn-0761.md) (2020-04-07) - Browser news (Firefox zero-days, a Safari exploit chain, TLS 1.0/1.1 and SameSite delays, Edge), STIR/SHAKEN, Cloudflare for Families, MANRS routing security and the many security and privacy problems found in Zoom.
* [SN 0762 - Virus Contact Tracing](../../sources/sn-0762.md) (2020-04-14) - Zoom follow-ups, Chrome 81 and Firefox 75 changes, an Android app backdoor study, Sandboxie going open source, a tribute to John Conway, and a technical walkthrough of the Apple/Google exposure notification contact tracing design.
* [SN 0763 - The COVID Effect](../../sources/sn-0763.md) (2020-04-21) - How COVID-19 reshaped security news: Zoom's remediation program and zero-day market rumors, Chrome flaw and FTP reprieve, April 2020 Patch Tuesday, delayed Basic Authentication shutdowns, Signal on EARN IT, Tor layoffs, Cloudflare's switch to hCaptcha and SpinRite AHCI progress.
* [SN 0764 - RPKI](../../sources/sn-0764.md) (2020-04-28) - Updates to the Apple/Google exposure notification design, the overhyped ZecOps iOS Mail crash bugs, VPN jurisdiction and the 14 Eyes, RubyGems typosquatting, VirtualBox licensing caution and a deep dive into RPKI for securing BGP.
* [SN 0765 - An Authoritarian Internet?](../../sources/sn-0765.md) (2020-05-05) - Bruce Schneier's doubts about contact tracing apps, US federal guidance against third-party DoH, a power-supply acoustic exfiltration trick, the SaltStack authentication bypass, Chrome Web Store cleanup rules, rising RDP scanning and China's New IP proposal.
* [SN 0766 - Thunderspy](../../sources/sn-0766.md) (2020-05-12) - Firefox 76 and Lockwise, Edge and browser notification muting, WordPress plugin and vBulletin attacks, virtual Black Hat and DEF CON, a Samsung Qmage MMS flaw, Zoom buying Keybase, SpinRite UEFI planning and the Thunderspy Thunderbolt weaknesses.
* [SN 0767 - WiFi 6](../../sources/sn-0767.md) (2020-05-19) - May 2020 Patch Tuesday trend, DNS over HTTPS arriving in Windows 10, the hidden pktmon tool, FBI complaints about Apple, an incomplete RDP path traversal fix, face masks versus facial recognition, Utah's tracing app and a history of Ethernet and WiFi leading to WiFi 6.
* [SN 0768 - Contact Tracing Apps R.I.P.](../../sources/sn-0768.md) (2020-05-26) - Firefox 77 maxlength warnings, Chrome 83 hidden features, Adobe out-of-cycle patches, the unc0ver iOS jailbreak, the NXNS DNS amplification attack, the Bluetooth BIAS impersonation flaw, eBay localhost port probing and Steve's argument that voluntary contact tracing apps are doomed.
* [SN 0769 - Zoom's E2EE Design](../../sources/sn-0769.md) (2020-06-02) - Browser drive-by download weaknesses, Chrome notification abuse blocking, ThreatMetrix localhost port scanning on hundreds of sites, an ACLU suit against Clearview AI, StrandHogg 2.0, SHA-1 in OpenSSH, USB fuzzing results and Zoom's four-phase end-to-end encryption design.
* [SN 0770 - Zoom's E2EE Debacle](../../sources/sn-0770.md) (2020-06-09) - Firefox 77's DoH rollout overloading providers, IBM exiting general facial recognition, Cisco Talos path traversal flaws in Zoom, the CallStranger UPnP SUBSCRIBE flaw, the new Edge rollout and Zoom's contradictory messages about end-to-end encryption for free users.
* [SN 0771 - Lamphone](../../sources/sn-0771.md) (2020-06-16) - Brave's affiliate-tagging slip, Microsoft's record 129-CVE Patch Tuesday and Windows 10 2004 breakage, SMBleed, Intel SGX side channels and CET, and the Lamphone light-bulb eavesdropping research.
* [SN 0772 - Ripple20](../../sources/sn-0772.md) (2020-06-23) - Edge pushed to Windows 7, 106 malicious Chrome extensions removed, Zoom E2EE plans, a Netgear router flaw across 79 models, a record 2.3 Tbps DDoS, the InitDisk freeware, and the Ripple20 Treck TCP/IP stack flaws.
* [SN 0773 - Ripple20 Too](../../sources/sn-0773.md) (2020-06-30) - Browsers align on 398-day certificates, Apple rejects 16 fingerprint-prone web APIs and adds DoH/DoT, Mozilla ties Firefox DoH to Comcast, US face-recognition and lawful-access bills, and a detailed follow-up on affected Ripple20 devices.
* [SN 0774 - 123456](../../sources/sn-0774.md) (2020-07-07) - Out-of-band Windows codec fixes delivered via the Microsoft Store, iOS 14 clipboard alerts, HackerOne's top bounty programs and Sony's entry, NIST's face-recognition bias study, the critical F5 BIG-IP TMUI flaw, and password statistics from a billion leaked credentials.
* [SN 0775 - Tsunami](../../sources/sn-0775.md) (2020-07-14) - Firefox Send suspended over abuse, a Zoom Windows 7 remote-code flaw micropatched by 0patch, the revised EARN IT Act, stalkerware, C-Data fiber gear with hardcoded telnet backdoors, drone-operator geolocation, the Fraunhofer router report, and Google's Tsunami scanner.
* [SN 0776 - A Tale of Two Counterfeits](../../sources/sn-0776.md) (2020-07-21) - The July 2020 Twitter account takeover, the wormable Windows DNS Server flaw SIGRed, Cloudflare's BGP outage, leaky free VPNs, and the C-Data and fake Cisco counterfeit-hardware stories.
* [SN 0777 - rwxrwxrwx](../../sources/sn-0777.md) (2020-07-28) - F5 BIG-IP exploitation follow-up, Twitter hack DM exposure, the GnuTLS session-ticket encryption bug, the Garmin WastedLocker ransomware outage, a Cisco ASA/FTD path traversal under attack, and surprising SpinRite storage benchmark findings.
* [SN 0778 - BootHole](../../sources/sn-0778.md) (2020-08-04) - Twitter hack arrests, Garmin's WastedLocker ransom details, a researcher dropping Tor "zero-days", Zoom's unrate-limited PINs, SHA-1 download retirement, QNAP QSnatch, and the GRUB2 BootHole Secure Boot bypass.
* [SN 0779 - Geneva](../../sources/sn-0779.md) (2020-08-11) - Virtual Black Hat and Defcon, Graz's speculative dereferencing research reviving Foreshadow, Canon's Maze ransomware, an irresponsibly disclosed vBulletin zero-day, DoH in Windows 10, Have I Been Pwned going open source, and the Geneva genetic algorithm that evades Great Firewall censorship.
* [SN 0780 - Microsoft's 0-Day Folly](../../sources/sn-0780.md) (2020-08-18) - Qualcomm's Achilles DSP flaws, the third-largest Patch Tuesday, Mozilla's layoffs and Canon's leaked data, an expired-certificate COVID reporting glitch, Control Flow Guard in Rust and LLVM, Threema video calls, and Microsoft's long delay fixing two exploited flaws including the GlueBall MSI signature issue.
* [SN 0781 - SpiKey](../../sources/sn-0781.md) (2020-08-25) - Chrome WebGL flaw, new ransomware victims and double extortion, RDP as the top ransomware entry vector, the Zero Day Initiative's 15 years, remote-access and sync tool recommendations, and SpiKey inferring physical keys from the sound of insertion.
* [SN 0782 - I Know What You Did Last Summer](../../sources/sn-0782.md) (2020-09-01) - Chrome 85 features, a foiled Russian bribery plot against a Tesla employee, ETH Zurich's formal-verification attack on Visa contactless PIN checks, listener feedback, and research showing browsing history uniquely re-identifies users.
* [SN 0783 - IoT Isolation Strategies](../../sources/sn-0783.md) (2020-09-08) - DoH for Chrome on Android, Firefox blocking drive-by downloads, Threema going open source, the WordPress File Manager plugin flaw, Facebook and US-government disclosure policies, Tor memberships, Intel microcode, and Steve's practical IoT network isolation advice.
* [SN 0784 - BlindSide & BLURtooth](../../sources/sn-0784.md) (2020-09-15) - Chrome's heavy-ad intervention, malvertising exploiting IE and Flash, September Patch Tuesday, WordPress File Manager aftermath, Zoom two-factor authentication, the Raccoon TLS attack, and the BlindSide Spectre-based blind ROP and BLURtooth Bluetooth key-overwrite attacks.
* [SN 0785 - Formal Verification](../../sources/sn-0785.md) (2020-09-22) - Zerologon and CISA Emergency Directive 20-04, a Firefox for Android SSDP flaw, Mozilla ending Send and Notes, DuckDuckGo growth, Android 11 and iOS 14 privacy features, and BLESA as a case for formal protocol verification.
* [SN 0786 - Zerologon++](../../sources/sn-0786.md) (2020-09-29) - Chrome 85 fixes, the FortiGate SSL VPN default-certificate weakness, a one-week ransomware roundup including the UHS Ryuk attack, Ring's flying camera, and the Zerologon exploit explosion.
* [SN 0787 - Why Win7 Lives On](../../sources/sn-0787.md) (2020-10-06) - Google's Android app security team and Fuzzilli grants, Defender updates for offline Windows images, WSL 2 bypassing the firewall, unpatched Exchange servers, Cloudflare API Shield, Treasury ransomware-payment guidance, the MosaicRegressor UEFI bootkit, and a poll on why Windows 7 persists.
* [SN 0788 - Well-Known URIs](../../sources/sn-0788.md) (2020-10-13) - Chrome 86 security changes, ransomware victims and access-broker markets, French open-WiFi logging arrests, a large Apple bug bounty haul, Zerologon in the wild, Global Privacy Control, the Five Eyes encryption demand, and the IANA well-known URI mechanism.
* [SN 0789 - Anatomy of a Ryuk Attack](../../sources/sn-0789.md) (2020-10-20) - Manifest V3 extension changes arriving in Edge, October 2020 Patch Tuesday including the Bad Neighbor IPv6 flaw, Zoom's end-to-end encryption preview, a critical SonicWall NSA bug, and a forensic walkthrough of a five-hour Ryuk ransomware intrusion.
* [SN 0790 - The 25 Most Attacked Vulnerabilities](../../sources/sn-0790.md) (2020-10-27) - A Chrome zero-day in the FreeType library, Chrome notification-spam blocking, Firefox Fission site isolation, forced WordPress plugin updates for Loginizer, and the NSA's list of 25 vulnerabilities used by Chinese state hackers.
* [SN 0791 - Chrome's Root Program](../../sources/sn-0791.md) (2020-11-03) - A Windows kernel crypto driver zero-day chained with the Chrome FreeType bug, Microsoft's Zerologon reminder, Google One VPN with blind signatures, CERT's Vulnonym naming scheme, a botched WordPress 5.5.2 rollout, and Google's new Chrome Root Program.
* [SN 0792 - "Slipstream" NAT Firewall Bypass](../../sources/sn-0792.md) (2020-11-10) - Let's Encrypt's cross-signed root expiry and old Android devices, more Chrome and Apple zero-days, big ransomware victims, China's Tianfu Cup, the EU encryption resolution, WordPress plugin flaws, and Samy Kamkar's NAT Slipstreaming attack.
* [SN 0793 - SAD DNS](../../sources/sn-0793.md) (2020-11-17) - Two more Chrome zero-days, ransomware-as-a-service landscape and a Ragnar Locker Facebook ad, November 2020 Patch Tuesday, research on where Android unwanted apps come from, and the SAD DNS side-channel attack that revives DNS cache poisoning.
* [SN 0794 - Cicada](../../sources/sn-0794.md) (2020-11-24) - Chrome 87 and Firefox 83 updates, Egregor printing ransom notes, WordPress theme attacks and fake-store hijacks, long-unpatched internet-exposed flaws, Android Messages adopting the Signal protocol for RCS, and the Chinese APT group Cicada.
* [SN 0795 - DNS Consolidation](../../sources/sn-0795.md) (2020-12-01) - Chrome omnibox commands, ransomware victims including DoppelPaymer, a critical Drupal archive-library flaw, insecure no-name video doorbells, a Tesla Model X key fob attack, credential leaks, SpinRite's SSD future, and research on DNS provider concentration.
* [SN 0796 - Amazon Sidewalk](../../sources/sn-0796.md) (2020-12-08) - Foxconn and other ransomware victims, Ian Beer's zero-click AWDL iPhone exploit, Oblivious DoH, the vulnerable Google Play Core Library in Android apps, Apple M1 x86 emulation, and a detailed look at Amazon Sidewalk's design.
* [SN 0797 - SolarWinds](../../sources/sn-0797.md) (2020-12-15) - Chrome heavy-ad blocking, Adrozek ad injection malware, December 2020 Patch Tuesday and Microsoft's no-CVE policy for auto-updating products, Cisco Jabber and D-Link flaws, Amnesia:33 TCP/IP stack bugs, Flash's end, and the first full look at the SolarWinds Orion supply chain compromise found via FireEye.
* [SN 0799 - Sunburst & Supernova](../../sources/sn-0799.md) (2020-12-29) - Chrome's rollback of insecure-form warnings, Firefox network partitioning, browsers blocking Kazakhstan's root certificate, a new Ransomware Task Force, Contact Form 7 and Treck TCP/IP flaws, the ReadSpeed release, and new findings on the SolarWinds Sunburst and Supernova backdoors.
* [SN 0800 - SolarBlizzard](../../sources/sn-0800.md) (2021-01-05) - Security Now episode 800 covering Chrome's retry kludge for antivirus file locks, a new Zyxel hardcoded backdoor, IoT swatting, PHP Zend and Yii deserialization bugs, SSD trimming after SpinRite, and SolarWinds follow-ups.
* [SN 0801 - Out With the Old](../../sources/sn-0801.md) (2021-01-12) - Security Now episode 801 covering critical Firefox and Chromium fixes, Ryuk and REvil ransomware profits, Intel's dubious ransomware-detection claim, a hijacked IoT chastity device, Sunburst/Kazuar code overlaps, WhatsApp's metadata policy backlash, and SpinRite's legacy DOS code.
* [SN 0802 - Where the Plaintext Is](../../sources/sn-0802.md) (2021-01-19) - Security Now episode 802 covering Chrome sync enforcement, DuckDuckGo growth, Project Zero's analysis of a 2020 watering-hole exploit campaign, January 2021 Patch Tuesday, Zerologon enforcement, NSA guidance on enterprise DoH, the Titan security key side-channel attack, and the WhatsApp metadata exodus.
* [SN 0803 - Comparative Smartphone Security](../../sources/sn-0803.md) (2021-01-26) - Security Now episode 803 covering browser password managers in Chrome and Edge 88, Flash end-of-life fallout, DDoS added to ransomware extortion, Microsoft's SolarWinds second-stage analysis, Malwarebytes breach, calling-state-machine bugs in messengers, and the Johns Hopkins iOS versus Android security study.
* [SN 0804 - NAT Slipstreaming 2.0](../../sources/sn-0804.md) (2021-02-02) - Security Now episode 804 covering Chrome's distrust of Camerfirma, a libgcrypt 1.9.0 heap overflow, the NoxPlayer update-channel compromise, the ten-year-old sudo Baron Samedit bug, Apple's BlastDoor iMessage hardening, SpinRite's platform decision, and NAT Slipstreaming 2.0.
* [SN 0805 - SCADA Scandal](../../sources/sn-0805.md) (2021-02-09) - Security Now episode 805 covering a trio of Chrome stories (The Great Suspender, a V8 zero-day used against researchers, extension C2 via Chrome sync), a Defender false positive, NextGEN Gallery flaws, Plex SSDP DDoS amplification, new SolarWinds vulnerabilities, and the Oldsmar water plant intrusion.
* [SN 0806 - C.O.M.B.](../../sources/sn-0806.md) (2021-02-16) - Security Now episode 806 covering Oldsmar water plant follow-up, February 2021 Patch Tuesday, SHAREit Android vulnerabilities, the rise of web shells, a WordPress plugin flaw, SpinRite device discovery progress, and the 3.2-billion-entry COMB credential compilation.
* [SN 0807 - Dependency Confusion:  The Build Chain Hack](../../sources/sn-0807.md) (2021-02-23) - Security Now episode 807 covering SHAREit's late patch, favicon-cache browser tracking, Brave's .onion DNS leak, email tracking pixels, Microsoft's final Solorigate report, an Android app sold and turned into adware, SSD refresh after SpinRite, and Alex Birsan's dependency confusion attack.
* [SN 0808 - CNAME Collusion](../../sources/sn-0808.md) (2021-03-02) - Security Now episode 808 covering Chrome's HTTPS-first omnibox, Firefox 86 Total Cookie Protection, SolarWinds password testimony, a Rockwell PLC shared-key flaw, VMware vCenter exploitation, a SpinRite debugging story, and CNAME-cloaked tracking.
* [SN 0809 - Hafnium](../../sources/sn-0809.md) (2021-03-09) - Security Now episode 809 covering Chrome 89 fixes, a chip-crunch Chrome extension, Google-funded Linux security maintainers, Winamp's revival, the Intel ring-interconnect side channel, dependency confusion copycats, and the Hafnium Exchange Server mass compromise.
* [SN 0810 - ProxyLogon](../../sources/sn-0810.md) (2021-03-16) - Security Now episode 810 covering the third Chrome zero-day of 2021, Google's JavaScript Spectre proof of concept, a script-free CSS cache side channel, March Patch Tuesday, the Sigstore code-signing project, and the continuing ProxyLogon Exchange Server disaster including DearCry ransomware.
* [SN 0811 - What the FLoC?](../../sources/sn-0811.md) (2021-03-23) - Security Now episode 811 covering Defender's automatic ProxyLogon mitigation, Black Kingdom ransomware, Firefox 87's trimmed Referer policy, F5 BIG-IP and MyBB remote code execution flaws, China's CAID tracking identifier, and Google's FLoC proposal with the EFF's objections.
* [SN 0812 - GIT Me Some PHP](../../sources/sn-0812.md) (2021-03-30) - Security Now episode 812 covering ProxyLogon patch progress, Linux Spectre mitigation bypasses via eBPF, two OpenSSL high-severity flaws, SolarWinds Orion fixes, Cloudflare Browser Isolation, API security, SpinRite 6.1 progress, and the compromise of the PHP project's Git server.
* [SN 0813 - A Spy in Our Pocket](../../sources/sn-0813.md) (2021-04-06) - Security Now episode 813 (Jason Howell filling in for Leo) covering the Ubiquiti breach cover-up allegations, the leaked Facebook data on 533 million users, a Kansas water-district intrusion, Google's Android package-visibility restriction, malicious Warzone cheats, QNAP NAS flaws, and Trinity College research on phone telemetry sent to Apple and Google.
* [SN 0814 - PwnIt and OwnIt](../../sources/sn-0814.md) (2021-04-13) - Chrome blocks port 10080, EFF's "Am I FLoCed" test, the PHP git server hack explained, Cisco abandoning EOL routers, FortiGate SSL VPN patch failures, and the Spring 2021 Pwn2Own results.
* [SN 0815 - Homogeneity Attacks](../../sources/sn-0815.md) (2021-04-20) - Vivaldi's anti-FLoC stance, Chrome 89/90 updates, the FBI removing Exchange web shells under court order, WordPress FLoC blocking, and Google's t-closeness protections against homogeneity attacks in FLoC.
* [SN 0816 - The Mystery of AS8003](../../sources/sn-0816.md) (2021-04-27) - Remembrance of Dan Kaminsky, April 2021 Patch Tuesday with NSA-found Exchange flaws, Project Zero's new 30-day patch grace, the University of Minnesota hypocrite-commits controversy, Cloudflare vs patent troll Sable, and the DoD's AS8003 BGP announcement.
* [SN 0817 - The Ransomware Task Force](../../sources/sn-0817.md) (2021-05-04) - REvil's Quanta/Apple extortion, the Scripps Health ransomware outage, the Emotet botnet takedown and self-destruct, Have I Been Pwned's new notification services, and the Ransomware Task Force framework report.
* [SN 0818 - News from the DarkSide](../../sources/sn-0818.md) (2021-05-11) - TsuNAME DNS looping flaw, confusing Google two-step verification news, Qualys' 21 Exim vulnerabilities, malicious Tor exit nodes, and the DarkSide ransomware attack on Colonial Pipeline.
* [SN 0819 - The WiFi Frag Attacks](../../sources/sn-0819.md) (2021-05-18) - DarkSide fallout (Mandiant analysis, blockchain tracing, dark web forums banning ransomware), May 2021 Patch Tuesday, and the FragAttacks design and implementation flaws in WiFi.
* [SN 0820 - The Dark Escrow](../../sources/sn-0820.md) (2021-05-25) - Firefox Fission site isolation, the Conti attack on Ireland's HSE, CNA Financial's $40M ransom and cyber-insurance retreat, "mean time to inventory" scanning speed, onion-site TLS certificates, and Steve's prediction of ransomware escrow services.
* [SN 0821 - Epsilon Red](../../sources/sn-0821.md) (2021-06-01) - Chrome 91 features and fixes, trusted third-party ransomware decryptors, Amazon Sidewalk going live, the overhyped Nobelium/USAID phishing wave, WhatsApp vs India, and a teardown of the amateurish PowerShell-based Epsilon Red ransomware.
* [SN 0822 - Extrinsic Password Managers](../../sources/sn-0822.md) (2021-06-08) - Steve's rant on why computer security is broken, Firefox background updates and Edge Automatic HTTPS, Colonial Pipeline breach findings and the FBI's DarkSide bitcoin seizure, WordPress plugin flaws, GitHub's proof-of-concept policy, NAT vs IPv6, and Tavis Ormandy's case against extension-based password managers.
* [SN 0823 - TLS Confusion Attacks](../../sources/sn-0823.md) (2021-06-15) - Timeline of an April 2021 Chrome and Windows zero-day chain, the June 2021 industry-wide patch day, TikTok's biometric privacy policy, the ANOM sting, a BitLocker laptop recovery story, and the ALPACA cross-protocol TLS attacks.
* [SN 0824 - Avaddon Ransonomics](../../sources/sn-0824.md) (2021-06-22) - Another Chrome zero-day, initial access brokers and the shift to smaller ransomware targets, the iOS Wi-Fi SSID format-string bug, Windows 11 leak notes, a long SpinRite 6.1 progress report, and why Avaddon shut down and released keys.
* [SN 0825 - Halfway Through 2021](../../sources/sn-0825.md) (2021-06-29) - An Edge translation XSS bug that earned a $20,000 bounty, Google's FLoC delay, Ireland's $600M HSE ransomware recovery estimate, the Western Digital My Book Live mass wipe, John McAfee's death, and the muddle over Windows 11 hardware requirements.
* [SN 0826 - The Kaseya Saga](../../sources/sn-0826.md) (2021-07-06) - PrintNightmare Windows print spooler flaws and the emergency patch, the GRU brute-force advisory, the Western Digital My Book Live and My Cloud OS 3 follow-up, and the REvil attack through Kaseya VSA that hit over a thousand organizations.
* [SN 0827 - REvil's Clever Crypto](../../sources/sn-0827.md) (2021-07-13) - PrintNightmare's partially fixed state ("vulnerable by design" Point and Print settings), why the Kaseya attack was less costly than feared, the Ransomwhere payment tracker, a Word-Excel Zloader macro bypass, ransomware negotiators and access brokers, and a deep dive into the REvil/Sodinokibi key hierarchy.
* [SN 0828 - REvil Vanishes!](../../sources/sn-0828.md) (2021-07-20) - Chrome zero-day and Candiru, Firefox SmartBlock 2.0, the iOS WiFi SSID format-string bug, more PrintNightmare flaws, a record Patch Tuesday, Telegram's homegrown crypto, the NSO Pegasus Project and REvil's sudden disappearance.
* [SN 0829 - SeriousSAM & PetitPotam](../../sources/sn-0829.md) (2021-07-27) - Chrome's faster colour-profile phishing detection, Kaseya's universal REvil decryptor, a 16-year-old HP/Samsung/Xerox driver flaw, GitHub supply-chain features for Go, and the SeriousSAM and PetitPotam Windows flaws.
* [SN 0830 - The BlackMatter Interview](../../sources/sn-0830.md) (2021-08-03) - Firefox user decline, Chrome dropping the HTTPS padlock, the initial access broker market, DarkSide's rebrand as BlackMatter and a Recorded Future interview with that group, Microsoft's "won't fix" list, and Tailscale on WireGuard.
* [SN 0831 - Apple's CSAM Mistake](../../sources/sn-0831.md) (2021-08-10) - IoT hardware random number generator failures, Pulse Secure and Cisco VPN flaws, the 14 INFRA:HALT NicheStack bugs, Edge's JIT-less Super Duper Secure Mode, and Apple's CSAM Detection and Communication Safety announcements.
* [SN 0832 - Microsoft's Culpable Negligence](../../sources/sn-0832.md) (2021-08-17) - Firefox 91 Enhanced Cookie Clearing, Facebook Messenger end-to-end encrypted calls, PrintNightmare abuse in ransomware, a prefetch-disabling miner botnet, the Avast-NortonLifeLock merger, and Steve's argument that Microsoft is negligent about known flaws.
* [SN 0833 - Microsoft's Reasoned Neglect](../../sources/sn-0833.md) (2021-08-24) - Firefox blocking mixed-content downloads, the T-Mobile breach and credit locks, ProxyShell attacks on Exchange, the Razer installer privilege flaw, the ThroughTek Kalay IoT flaw, overlay networks beyond Tailscale, and a speculative explanation of Microsoft's slow patching.
* [SN 0834 - Life:  Hanging by a PIN](../../sources/sn-0834.md) (2021-08-31) - Credit freeze versus lock correction, the T-Mobile attacker's account, Windows 11 hardware confusion, the ProxyToken Exchange flaw, SSD bait-and-switch component swaps, and why cellular account PINs and SMS recovery are a weak link.
* [SN 0835 - TPM v1.2 vs 2.0](../../sources/sn-0835.md) (2021-09-07) - A registry setting against plug-and-play installer privilege abuse, ProtonMail's IP logging disclosure, Apple pausing CSAM plans, BrakTooth Bluetooth flaws, an unpatched Fortress alarm, and a feature-by-feature comparison of TPM 1.2 and 2.0 for Windows 11.
* [SN 0836 - The Meris Botnet](../../sources/sn-0836.md) (2021-09-14) - REvil returns, the MHTML zero-day CVE-2021-40444 in Office, remote-work security surveys, the Spook.js Spectre attack on Chrome, and the record 21.8 million requests-per-second Meris DDoS botnet built from MikroTik routers.
* [SN 0837 - Cobalt Strike](../../sources/sn-0837.md) (2021-09-21) - The multi-day ransom DDoS against VoIP.ms, Patch Tuesday side effects for network printing, Android permission auto-reset backport, two more Chrome zero-days, the Epik breach in Have I Been Pwned, and an overview of the Cobalt Strike red-team tool abused by criminals.
* [SN 0838 - Autodiscover.fiasco](../../sources/sn-0838.md) (2021-09-28) - Chrome's 12th zero-day of 2021, Apple's bug-bounty disputes, Epik's confirmed breach, Windows 11 launch prep, a malformed-certificate trick that evades OpenSSL-based scanners, and Guardicore's discovery that Exchange Autodiscover leaks Windows domain credentials.
* [SN 0839 - Something Went Wrong](../../sources/sn-0839.md) (2021-10-05) - The six-hour Facebook BGP/DNS outage explained, plus two more Chrome zero-days, Windows 11 launch caveats, an FCC SIM-swap rulemaking, the GriftHorse Android trojan and an Apple Pay with Visa transit-mode relay flaw.
* [SN 0840 - 0-Day Angst](../../sources/sn-0840.md) (2021-10-12) - Windows 11 upgrade-bypass registry key and early bugs, Excel XLM macros disabled only for some, Google's APT28 warnings and automatic two-step verification, US ransomware reporting bills, the Twitch source leak, the Apache 2.4.49 path traversal zero-day and an essay putting year-to-date zero-day counts in context.
* [SN 0841 - Minh Duong's Epic Rickroll](../../sources/sn-0841.md) (2021-10-19) - Windows 11 and PrintNightmare patch fallout, REvil's second and final shutdown, the 30-nation counter-ransomware meeting, Tianfu Cup 2021 results, the MyKings clipboard-hijacking botnet, LinkedIn's China retreat and a student's disclosed school-district Rickroll prank.
* [SN 0842 - The More Things Change...](../../sources/sn-0842.md) (2021-10-26) - Windows 11 preview fixes, the law-enforcement re-hack of REvil, Zerodium's VPN exploit bounty, the overhyped Gummy Browsers paper, the UAParser.js npm compromise and a 2001 TV clip showing how little has changed.
* [SN 0843 - Trojan Source](../../sources/sn-0843.md) (2021-11-02) - Record Chrome zero-days, malicious Firefox proxy add-ons, Windows 11 and printing woes, Adobe's giant patch, VoIP DDoS extortion, SpinRite 6.1 benchmarks and the Unicode-based Trojan Source attack on source code.
* [SN 0844 - Bluetooth Fingerprinting](../../sources/sn-0844.md) (2021-11-09) - Ransomware arrests and sanctions, Pwn2Own Austin 2021 results, an unpatched GitLab ExifTool flaw feeding DDoS botnets, more npm hijacks, the first CISA known-exploited catalog and UCSD research on fingerprinting Bluetooth radios.
* [SN 0845 - Blacksmith](../../sources/sn-0845.md) (2021-11-16) - Palo Alto GlobalProtect CVE-2021-3064, a 0patch micropatch for an unfixed Windows flaw, November 2021 Patch Tuesday, more ransomware and ad-fraud prosecutions, and the Blacksmith Rowhammer attack that defeats DDR4 mitigations.
* [SN 0846 - HTTP REQUEST SMUGGLING](../../sources/sn-0846.md) (2021-11-23) - An IETF proposal to reclaim most of 127/8 for public use, Microsoft honeypot password data, the GoDaddy Managed WordPress breach, a critical Netgear UPnP flaw, and an explanation of HTTP request smuggling.
* [SN 0847 - Bogons Begone!](../../sources/sn-0847.md) (2021-11-30) - Edge's Super Duper Secure Mode, MediaTek audio DSP flaws in 37% of phones, the RATDispenser JavaScript loader, an unfixed Windows Installer privilege escalation now exploited, and the IPv4 Cleanup Project's case for reclaiming bogon address space.
* [SN 0848 - XSinator](../../sources/sn-0848.md) (2021-12-07) - A memory-corruption bug in Mozilla NSS signature verification, spyware-like children's smartwatches, more Russian VPN bans, RTF template injection, the Glupteba botnet's bitcoin fallback, HP printer firmware flaws and the XSinator cross-site leak study.
* [SN 0849 - Log4j & Log4Shell](../../sources/sn-0849.md) (2021-12-14) - The Log4Shell (CVE-2021-44228) catastrophe in Apache Log4j 2, plus the AWS network outage, AirTag abuse by car thieves, Windows 11's Edge lock-in and a WordPress Epsilon Framework attack wave.
* [SN 0850 - It's a Log4j Christmas](../../sources/sn-0850.md) (2021-12-21) - Follow-up on Log4Shell (patches 2.16 and 2.17, Google's dependency-depth analysis, ransomware and nation-state exploitation), plus the 16th Chrome zero-day of 2021, a Firefox OCSP-stapling outage, Windows Cloud Clipboard and cell handover and WiFi-Bluetooth coexistence attacks.
* [SN 0852 - December 33rd](../../sources/sn-0852.md) (2022-01-04) - Log4j's fifth fix (2.17.1), Defender false positives, Alibaba disciplined by China's regulator, DHS's low Log4j bounty, rising cyber insurance costs, DuckDuckGo's planned desktop browser and Microsoft's Y2K22 Exchange date-overflow bug.
* [SN 0853 - URL Parsing Vulnerabilities](../../sources/sn-0853.md) (2022-01-11) - Log4j status (CISA assessment, H2 database JNDI flaw, FTC enforcement warning), Chrome 97 update, Brave growth, WordPress 5.8.3, what Microsoft Pluton is, and the Team82/Snyk study of inconsistent URL parsing libraries.
* [SN 0854 - Anatomy of a Log4j Exploit](../../sources/sn-0854.md) (2022-01-18) - Pentagon and White House responses to Log4j, a mixed January 2022 Patch Tuesday, the KCodes NetUSB router flaw (port 20005), Chrome's Private Network Access standard, WordPress plugin flaws and Check Point's analysis of Iranian APT35's Log4j toolkit.
* [SN 0855 - Inside the NetUSB Hack](../../sources/sn-0855.md) (2022-01-25) - Cyber-insurance fallout after the Merck NotPetya ruling, WordPress plugin flaws and the AccessPress supply-chain compromise, EU bug bounty funding, the MoonBounce UEFI bootkit and a code-level explanation of the NetUSB integer-overflow bug.
* [SN 0856 - The "Topics" API](../../sources/sn-0856.md) (2022-02-01) - Google's Topics API replacing FLoC, plus PwnKit (a 12-year-old Linux pkexec privilege escalation), Apple's first 2022 zero-days, Log4j attacks on UniFi, Zerodium's new bounties and the DRAWNAPART GPU fingerprinting technique.
* [SN 0857 - The Inept Panda](../../sources/sn-0857.md) (2022-02-08) - Beijing Olympics travel warnings and the badly secured MY2022 app, a critical Samba bug, living-off-the-land binaries, Microsoft disabling MSIX remote install and blocking Internet macros.
* [SN 0858 - InControl](../../sources/sn-0858.md) (2022-02-15) - Chrome and Apple zero-days, CISA's patch deadlines for old exploited bugs, Magento emergency patch and PHP Everywhere plugin, Google bounty and Project Zero statistics, WMIC removal and GRC's InControl freeware.
* [SN 0859 - A BGP Routing Attack](../../sources/sn-0859.md) (2022-02-22) - UpdraftPlus force-update, Xenomorph Android banking trojan, Hive ransomware's flawed keystream, pixelation redaction attacks, Magento patch bypass, HTTPS-flood DDoS on Ukraine, and the KLAYswap BGP hijack.
* [SN 0860 - Trust Dies in Darkness](../../sources/sn-0860.md) (2022-03-01) - Surveys on paying ransomware, the Daxin Chinese kernel backdoor, Log4j scanning after two months, Russia-Ukraine cyber effects, and the Samsung TrustZone Keymaster AES-GCM IV-reuse flaw.
* [SN 0861 - Rogue Nation Cyber Consequences](../../sources/sn-0861.md) (2022-03-08) - A single-theme episode on the cyber side of Russia's invasion of Ukraine, from Ukraine's volunteer IT Army and hacktivist alignments to platform sanctions, Starlink, Telegram and Russia's possible disconnection from the Internet.
* [SN 0862 - QWACs On? or QWACs Off?](../../sources/sn-0862.md) (2022-03-15) - Patch Tuesday recap, Firefox zero-days, InsydeH2O UEFI flaws, the Lapsus$ NVIDIA breach, Linux kernel bugs, Russia's own certificate authority, WordPress plugin statistics and the EU's QWAC proposal.
* [SN 0863 - Use After Free](../../sources/sn-0863.md) (2022-03-22) - The US cyber incident reporting law, node-ipc protest sabotage, Browser-in-the-Browser phishing, Trickbot via MikroTik routers, an OpenSSL certificate-parsing infinite loop, Duo fail-open abuse by Russian actors, a Windows profile bug that keeps resurfacing, and a primer on use-after-free.
* [SN 0864 - Targeted Exploitation](../../sources/sn-0864.md) (2022-03-29) - Chrome's second 2022 zero-day, Ukraine ISP resilience, an npm @azure name-confusion attack, Honda Civic fixed-code keyless entry, the FCC's Kaspersky listing, and Google TAG's analysis of North Korean exploitation of the first Chrome zero-day.
* [SN 0865 - Port Knocking](../../sources/sn-0865.md) (2022-04-05) - Spring4Shell, QNAP NAS troubles, an exploited Sophos Firewall bug, Browser-in-the-Browser used by Ghostwriter, RED-LILI's npm factory, FinFisher's collapse, Lapsus$ arrests, Wyze camera flaws, and a primer on port knocking and single-packet authorization.
* [SN 0866 - Spring4Shell](../../sources/sn-0866.md) (2022-04-12) - A claimed Nginx zero-day that turns out to hit only the LDAP reference implementation, Microsoft Autopatch, another npm protestware change, Windows 10 end of service dates, Coinbase account enumeration, port-knocking feedback, and Spring4Shell turning into a full outbreak.
* [SN 0867 - A Critical Windows RPC RCE](../../sources/sn-0867.md) (2022-04-19) - Chrome's third zero-day of 2022, Microsoft's 128-fix April Patch Tuesday, an Elementor plugin flaw, Apache Struts OGNL again, why old nuclear systems resist hacking, SpinRite driver work, and the wormable Windows RPC bug CVE-2022-26809.
* [SN 0868 - The Zero-Day Explosion](../../sources/sn-0868.md) (2022-04-26) - CISA's known-exploited catalog and its old entries, Lenovo UEFI "SecureBackDoor" drivers in over 100 laptop models, the Ever Surf web wallet flaw, the Java ECDSA signature bug, a SpinRite driver milestone, and Mandiant's decade of zero-day data showing 80 in 2021.
* [SN 0869 - Global Privacy Control](../../sources/sn-0869.md) (2022-05-03) - The DoD's DIB-VDP bug bounty pilot, OpenSSF's Package Analysis project, Connecticut's privacy bill, Moxie Marlinspike's port-knocking rationale, authenticator QR backups, and a detailed look at the Global Privacy Control browser signal.
* [SN 0870 - THAT "PASSKEYS" THING](../../sources/sn-0870.md) (2022-05-10) - An Android kernel patch, Connecticut's privacy law, ransomware roundup and the Conti bounty, Operation CuckooBees by Winnti, the White House quantum memo, a predictable-DNS-ID flaw in uClibc, F5 BIG-IP's critical bug, and Steve's analysis of the FIDO passkeys announcement.
* [SN 0871 - The New EU Surveillance State](../../sources/sn-0871.md) (2022-05-17) - May 2022 episode covering a troubled Patch Tuesday, Google's Open Source Maintenance Crew, the Conti attack on Costa Rica, F5 BIG-IP exploitation and the EU's proposed CSAM/grooming scanning regulation.
* [SN 0872 - Dis-CONTI-nued: The End of Conti?](../../sources/sn-0872.md) (2022-05-24) - May 2022 episode on the fixed Active Directory patch, Clearview AI's facial recognition, Pwn2Own Vancouver 2022, the DOJ's good-faith research policy, a Bluetooth smart-lock relay attack and the deliberate dissolution of the Conti brand.
* [SN 0873 - DuckDuckGone?](../../sources/sn-0873.md) (2022-05-31) - May 2022 episode on New South Wales' insecure Digital Driver's Licence, the Follina Microsoft Office zero-day, the Ghost Touch EMI attack, carrier supercookie plans (TrustPiD) and DuckDuckGo's Microsoft tracker carve-out.
* [SN 0874 - Passkeys, Take 2](../../sources/sn-0874.md) (2022-06-07) - June 2022 episode covering ServiceNSW's defence of its digital licence, India's VPN logging rules, exam-time internet shutdowns, Follina in the wild, ransomware vs sanctions, Conti's UEFI/Management Engine research and Steve's concern about passkey lock-in.
* [SN 0875 - The PACMAN Attack](../../sources/sn-0875.md) (2022-06-14) - June 2022 episode clarifying Apple's passkey sync, FIDO2/WebAuthn versus SQRL, the accidental Windows 11 offer to unsupported PCs, a listener puzzle on double encryption, and MIT's PACMAN attack on ARM Pointer Authentication in Apple M1.
* [SN 0876 - Microsoft's Patchy Patches](../../sources/sn-0876.md) (2022-06-21) - June 2022 episode answering the double-encryption puzzle, covering passkey sync and password managers, Firefox Total Cookie Protection, a record HTTPS DDoS, the DFSCoerce NTLM relay, Ninja Forms and FastJson flaws, and criticism of Microsoft's vulnerability handling.
* [SN 0877 - The Hertzbleed Attack](../../sources/sn-0877.md) (2022-06-28) - June 2022 episode (Jason Howell hosting) correcting the Firefox cookie test, covering the passkey roadmap at LastPass, Germany's pushback on EU CSAM scanning, Conti's final shutdown, Log4Shell persistence, OT:ICEFALL, and the Hertzbleed frequency-scaling side channel.
* [SN 0878 - The ZuoRAT](../../sources/sn-0878.md) (2022-07-05) - July 2022 episode on Chrome's fourth zero-day, Firefox query-parameter stripping, a malicious insider at HackerOne, OT protocol security, the Matter smart-home standard and ZuoRAT, a router-based remote access trojan targeting remote workers.
* [SN 0879 - The Rolling Pwn](../../sources/sn-0879.md) (2022-07-12) - July 2022 episode on an OpenSSL 3.0.4 AVX-512 heap bug, NIST's first four post-quantum algorithms, Apple's Lockdown Mode, Microsoft's macro-blocking reversal, the FBI's ANOM sting app and the Honda rolling-code flaw.
* [SN 0880 - RetBleed](../../sources/sn-0880.md) (2022-07-19) - July 2022 episode (Jason Howell guest-hosting) on Honda's response to Rolling-Pwn, IPv4 price rises, a Council on Foreign Relations report on the fragmented internet, Facebook's opaque links, Clearview AI fines, searchable ransomware leak sites and the RetBleed speculative-execution attack.
* [SN 0881 - The MV720](../../sources/sn-0881.md) (2022-07-26) - July 2022 episode on a USB printer bug after Patch Tuesday, Microsoft resuming its Office macro block, Windows 11's default RDP lockout, SpinRite 6.1 coming alive, pfSense with Tailscale, and the badly insecure MiCODUS MV720 GPS tracker with fuel cut-off.
* [SN 0882 - Rowhammer's Nine Lives](../../sources/sn-0882.md) (2022-08-02) - August 2022 episode on hard-coded credentials in a Confluence add-on, the TLS-Anvil test suite, another Google delay of third-party cookie removal, attackers abandoning Office macros, SpinRite bit-width theory and the Half-Double Rowhammer attack.
* [SN 0883 - The Maker's Schedule](../../sources/sn-0883.md) (2022-08-09) - August 2022 episode on the break of the SIKE post-quantum candidate, VirusTotal's "Deception at Scale" report, Windows 11 VAES data corruption, Microsoft's External Attack Surface Management, passkey interoperability, a teaser on Bernstein's lawsuit and Paul Graham's essay on maker versus manager schedules.
* [SN 0884 - TLS PRIVATE KEY LEAKAGE](../../sources/sn-0884.md) (2022-08-16) - August 2022 episode closing the podcast's 17th year, covering the large August Patch Tuesday (DogWalk), Facebook Messenger's default end-to-end encryption test, exposed passwordless VNC servers, domain-name ownership and research showing TLS RSA private keys can be recovered from faulty signatures.
* [SN 0885 - THE BUMBLEBEE LOADER](../../sources/sn-0885.md) (2022-08-23) - Realtek eCos SDK router flaw (CVE-2022-27255), Google's record 46M rps DDoS, Chrome and Apple zero-days, RubyGems MFA mandate, and a walkthrough of the Bumblebee malware loader.
* [SN 0886 - Wacky Data Exfiltration](../../sources/sn-0886.md) (2022-08-30) - LastPass developer-environment breach disclosure, FTC and FCC action on location data brokers, an interview with a Russian ransomware operator, DuckDuckGo Email Protection, unpatched Hikvision cameras, and Ben-Gurion University air-gap exfiltration research.
* [SN 0887 - Embedding AWS Credentials](../../sources/sn-0887.md) (2022-09-06) - Google's open source vulnerability rewards program, the disputed TikTok breach claim, Chrome's sixth zero-day and clipboard-write concerns, pip download running setup scripts, a quantum computing skeptic, and Symantec's finding of hard-coded AWS credentials in 1,859 mobile apps.
* [SN 0888 - The EvilProxy Service](../../sources/sn-0888.md) (2022-09-13) - Albania cuts ties with Iran after a state cyberattack, crypto heist roundup, a White House tech accountability session, a Dutch regulator's resignation, DeadBolt ransomware on QNAP NAS, MooBot on D-Link routers, and the EvilProxy phishing-as-a-service that bypasses MFA.
* [SN 0889 - Spell-Jacking](../../sources/sn-0889.md) (2022-09-20) - September 2022 Patch Tuesday, Lloyd's nation-state exclusion for cyber insurance, the Uber and Rockstar breaches, LastPass's final post-mortem, a WPGateway WordPress zero-day, Retbleed mitigation costs, Chrome's MiraclePtr, and spell-jacking via enhanced spellcheck.
* [SN 0890 - DarkNet Politics](../../sources/sn-0890.md) (2022-09-27) - Europol data retention dispute, Denmark joins others ruling Google Analytics unlawful under GDPR, Teapot arrest, Mozilla's walled-gardens complaint, Chrome Manifest V3, stale embedded Chromium, SMB rate limiting in Windows 11, Iran's 14-month Albanian intrusion, MFA fatigue, and the LockBit 3.0 builder leak.
* [SN 0891 - Poisoning Akamai](../../sources/sn-0891.md) (2022-10-04) - Microsoft Teams plaintext token storage, SmartScreen rewrite, Roskomnadzor blocking SoundCloud, the Exchange "ProxyNotShell" zero-day, Cloudflare Turnstile CAPTCHA alternative, Google's "Hacking Google" series, and an HTTP request smuggling flaw in Akamai's CDN that enabled cache poisoning.
* [SN 0892 - Source Port Randomization](../../sources/sn-0892.md) (2022-10-11) - Celsius bankruptcy filing exposing customer data, Meta's malicious-app findings, Uber's former CSO convicted, Binance bridge hack, UK plan to leave the GDPR, incident-responder stress survey, Microsoft's Exchange response, SpinRite 6.1 progress and the ZimaBoard, and Linux's TCP source port algorithm enabling device tracking.
* [SN 0893 - Password Change Automation](../../sources/sn-0893.md) (2022-10-18) - Microsoft Office 365 Message Encryption using ECB mode (won't fix), the stale Windows vulnerable-driver blocklist (BYOVD), local admin lockout policy, passkeys.dev, Cloudflare's 2.5 Tbps DDoS, and the W3C well-known URL for changing passwords.
* [SN 0894 - Data Breach Responsibility](../../sources/sn-0894.md) (2022-10-25) - Firefox 106, Google's seL4-based KataOS, the Mango Markets crypto exploit, a Windows Mark-of-the-Web bypass used by Magniber ransomware, Apple's ninth zero-day, the demise of banking trojans, a critical VMware XStream flaw, and Australia's proposed heavy privacy-breach fines.
* [SN 0895 - After 20 Years in GCHQ](../../sources/sn-0895.md) (2022-11-01) - The Windows vulnerable-driver blocklist finally updated, Microsoft's narrow CVE definition, an OpenSSL 3.0 advisory, malicious GitHub proofs of concept, the 22-year-old SQLite "Stranger Strings" bug, PayPal passkeys and SMS-login weakness, and Ian Levy's farewell lessons from the UK NCSC.
* [SN 0896 - Something for Everyone](../../sources/sn-0896.md) (2022-11-08) - A news-heavy week covering Dropbox's phishing breach, OpenSSL 3.0.7 details, the FTC action against Chegg, 2021 ransomware payments, phishing kits, initial access broker prices, bank heist mechanics, Russia leaving Windows, a digital Red Cross emblem, and the UK NCSC's vulnerability scanning.
* [SN 0897 - Memory-Safe Languages](../../sources/sn-0897.md) (2022-11-15) - November 2022 Patch Tuesday and its Kerberos regression, the open-sourced Shennina automated attack framework, LiteSpeed web server flaws, Shufflecake hidden volumes, Australia's offensive stance after the Medibank ransomware attack, and the NSA's advisory urging memory-safe languages.
