* [SN 0898 - Wi-Peep](../../sources/sn-0898.md) (2022-11-22) - Google's $391.5 million location-tracking settlement, Red Hat's signed zip distributions, the FBI's Pegasus purchase and Greece's Predator spending, the passkeys.directory, an OMB quantum inventory order, state attorneys general urging FTC privacy rules, and the Wi-Peep WiFi device-location research.
* [SN 0899 - Freebie Bots & Evil Cameras](../../sources/sn-0899.md) (2022-11-29) - Takedown of the iSpoof caller ID spoofing service, freebie bots exploiting mispriced goods, a live crypto-exchange phishing scam, Karakurt's recruitment post, the outdated Boa web server in IoT gear, US and UK bans on Chinese networking cameras, and why third-party "passkey" logins can be phished.
* [SN 0900 - LastPass Again](../../sources/sn-0900.md) (2022-12-06) - Security Now episode 900 covering Australia's new privacy fines, the Medibank data dump, Mozilla's removal of the TrustCor root, leaked Android platform certificates, the Eufy camera privacy scandal and a second LastPass breach.
* [SN 0901 - Apple Encrypts the Cloud](../../sources/sn-0901.md) (2022-12-13) - Security Now episode 901 covering Chrome passkey support, the Sync.com outage, fake crypto trading sites, Telegram username auctions, TikTok bans, the Rackspace ransomware fallout and a detailed analysis of Apple's Advanced Data Protection for iCloud.
* [SN 0902 - A Generic WAF Bypass](../../sources/sn-0902.md) (2022-12-20) - Security Now episode 902 covering the URSNIF leaker, Pwn2Own Toronto 2022, December Patch Tuesday, the Teqtivity/Uber breach, Twitter's bot-blocking mishap, Meta's coordinated inauthentic behavior report, SHA-1 retirement and Team82's JSON-based SQL injection bypass of major web application firewalls.
* [SN 0904 - Leaving LastPass](../../sources/sn-0904.md) (2023-01-03) - Single-topic Security Now episode 904 analysing LastPass's December 2022 disclosure that customer vault backups were stolen, the weaknesses in its legacy defaults, and why Steve Gibson migrated to Bitwarden.
* [SN 0905 - 1](../../sources/sn-0905.md) (2023-01-10) - Security Now episode 905 follows up the LastPass vault theft with a community PowerShell tool that reveals the unencrypted vault metadata, the discovery that many accounts had a PBKDF2 iteration count of 1, and the arithmetic of password entropy versus iteration counts.
* [SN 0906 - The Rule of Two](../../sources/sn-0906.md) (2023-01-17) - Security Now episode 906 revisits LastPass server-side hashing claims, the Norton LifeLock account takeovers, Chrome distrusting TrustCor, vulnerable-driver abuse by Scattered Spider, and explains Google's Rule of Two behind the adoption of Rust in Chromium.
* [SN 0907 - Credential Reuse](../../sources/sn-0907.md) (2023-01-24) - Security Now episode 907 covering the PayPal credential-stuffing incident, Apple's global rollout of Advanced Data Protection, ChatGPT-assisted malware, Meta's third EU fine, Bitwarden's Passwordless.dev purchase and rising PBKDF2 defaults, and a primer on defending against credential reuse.
* [SN 0908 - Data Operand Independent Timing](../../sources/sn-0908.md) (2023-01-31) - Security Now episode 908 covering Android 14 blocking outdated apps, the Hive ransomware takedown, Kevin Rose's NFT phishing loss, Poland's CERT scanning programme, made-up-word master passwords, and the Intel DOITM / ARM DIT data-dependent instruction timing problem for cryptography.
* [SN 0909 - How ESXi Fell](../../sources/sn-0909.md) (2023-02-07) - Security Now episode 909 covering the EU's proposed mandatory CSAM scanning regulation, the Vastaamo clinic extortion suspect, HSTS-preloaded top-level domains, Chrome's early-stable rollout, QNAP and CVSS-downgrading news, OTP app questions, and the worldwide ESXiArgs ransomware attack on unpatched VMware ESXi servers.
* [SN 0910 - Ascon](../../sources/sn-0910.md) (2023-02-14) - Security Now episode 910 covering ESXiArgs follow-ups, criminal use of the ChatGPT API, Google's Titan key giveaway, Brave's HTTPS by Default, Russia's amnesty idea for patriotic hackers, Amazon S3 secure-by-default buckets, and NIST's selection of the Ascon lightweight cryptography family.
* [SN 0911 - A Clever Regurgitator](../../sources/sn-0911.md) (2023-02-21) - Security Now episode 911 covering the multi-year GoDaddy compromise, Section 230 Supreme Court arguments, Twitter ending free SMS two-factor, Bitwarden's Argon2 rollout, record Cloudflare DDoS, the V3G4 Mirai variant, the Hyundai/Kia USB theft flaw, and Steve's view of ChatGPT as a clever regurgitator.
* [SN 0912 - The NSA @ Home](../../sources/sn-0912.md) (2023-02-28) - Security Now episode 912 covering mistaken Windows 11 upgrade offers, Edge's built-in VPN, LastPass's detailed account of its second breach, Signal's threat to leave the UK, a PyPI malware flood, QNAP's bug bounty, a BIOS bug affecting large USB drives in SpinRite, and the NSA's home-network best practices guide.
* [SN 0913 - A Fowl Incident](../../sources/sn-0913.md) (2023-03-07) - Security Now episode 913 covering DDoS attacks on Mastodon instances, the TikTok ban debate, rogue stingray use, the US National Cybersecurity Strategy's software liability objective, GitHub secret scanning, a CISA red-team exercise, TPM library flaws, and a Chick-fil-A account takeover that Steve traces to Facebook login credential reuse.
* [SN 0914 - Sony Sues Quad9](../../sources/sn-0914.md) (2023-03-14) - Security Now episode 914 covering a cookie-stealing Chrome extension, Germany's planned Huawei/ZTE ban, the DFIR report on phishing as the top intrusion route, the Polynonce ECDSA attack on Bitcoin wallets, the Plex flaw behind the LastPass breach, and Sony's German lawsuit forcing Quad9 to block a piracy site.
* [SN 0915 - Flying Trojan Horses](../../sources/sn-0915.md) (2023-03-21) - Google Project Zero's withheld Samsung Exynos baseband flaws, Google's 90-day certificate lifetime plan, CISA's ransomware vulnerability warning pilot, and a forensic look at DJI's drone app.
* [SN 0916 - Microsoft's Email Extortion](../../sources/sn-0916.md) (2023-03-28) - Pwn2Own 2023 results, GitHub's exposed SSH host key, malicious package counts, Zoom's bug bounty, the NUIT ultrasound voice attack, and Steve's rant on Microsoft blocking mail from old Exchange servers.
* [SN 0917 - Zombie Software](../../sources/sn-0917.md) (2023-04-04) - The Oldsmar water-plant incident reassessed, AI regulation worries and Italy's ChatGPT ban, India shopping for spyware, FDA device-security rules, and an analysis of CISA's KEV list as "zombie software".
* [SN 0918 - A Dangerous Interpretation](../../sources/sn-0918.md) (2023-04-11) - Court-backed takedown of cracked Cobalt Strike, Samsung engineers leaking code to ChatGPT, Apple's zero-day patches, the Balada Injector WordPress campaign, SSD read disturb, and the H26FORGE video-decoder research.
* [SN 0919 - Forced Entry](../../sources/sn-0919.md) (2023-04-18) - April 2023 Patch Tuesday, a roundup of crypto-driven breaches, Google Assured OSS, WhatsApp's new security features, the Vastaamo clinic verdict, and Project Zero's analysis of NSO Group's ForcedEntry iMessage exploit.
* [SN 0920 - An End-to-End Encryption Proposal](../../sources/sn-0920.md) (2023-04-25) - Lockdown Mode versus NSO zero-clicks, the UK NCSC on commercial hacking tools, stolen ChatGPT accounts, secrets on used routers, Cisco SNMP malware, a legal defense fund for researchers, and Steve's end-to-end encryption compromise idea.
* [SN 0921 - OSB OMG and Other News!](../../sources/sn-0921.md) (2023-05-02) - The UK Online Safety Bill's effect on encrypted messaging and age checks, SLP amplification DDoS, Google Authenticator cloud sync, Mirai exploiting a TP-Link patch gap, Intel TDX pre-release audits, and a new Intel side channel.
* [SN 0922 - Detecting Unwanted Location Trackers](../../sources/sn-0922.md) (2023-05-09) - Google's Passkeys launch, a ransomware breach of the US Marshals' surveillance unit, Chrome dropping the padlock, AI-generated news sites, Russia's "own protocol" claims, Vint Cerf's regrets, and part one of the Apple/Google unwanted-tracker draft standard.
* [SN 0923 - Location Tracker Behavior](../../sources/sn-0923.md) (2023-05-16) - A SpinRite data-recovery rewrite and coding-philosophy aside, then a deep dive into the required behavior of trackers in the Apple/Google IETF draft on detecting unwanted location trackers, including the law-enforcement-accessible pairing registry.
* [SN 0924 - VCaaS - Voice Cloning as a Service](../../sources/sn-0924.md) (2023-05-23) - HP's firmware update that bricked OfficeJet Pro printers, the Supreme Court leaving Section 230 intact, the overhyped KeePass master-password memory issue, Privacy Sandbox timing, FBI FISA misuse, PyPI pausing registrations, a SpinRite 6.0 division-overflow patch, and voice cloning sold as a service.
* [SN 0925 - Brave's Brilliant Off the Record Request](../../sources/sn-0925.md) (2023-05-30) - Google's risky .zip and .mov top-level domains, HP's still-unresolved printer bricking, PyPI mandating 2FA and hashing IPs after a DOJ subpoena, a leaky "no-logs" VPN, Meta's 1.2 billion euro GDPR fine, and Brave's Request-OTR browser feature.
* [SN 0926 - Windows Platform Binary Table](../../sources/sn-0926.md) (2023-06-06) - Windows 11 mandatory SMB signing, OWASP's LLM Top 10 draft, Operation Triangulation on iPhones, Gigabyte firmware silently downloading code through the Windows Platform Binary Table, plus a Chrome exploit bounty race and the US "Know Your App Act".
* [SN 0927 - Scanning the Internet](../../sources/sn-0927.md) (2023-06-13) - Cloud cryptomining indemnification, Twitter's alleged unpaid cloud bills, a 5x rise in IoT DDoS bots, a Chinese CA abusing acme.sh, Apple's WWDC 2023 privacy features, France's remote-activation law, Russia blocking scanners, and a deep dive on ZMap and Censys.
* [SN 0928 - The Massive MOVEit Maelstrom](../../sources/sn-0928.md) (2023-06-20) - June 2023 Patch Tuesday, video-based cryptanalysis recovering keys from a device's power LED, the SpinRite 6.1 DOS-side milestone, and the Cl0p mass extortion campaign exploiting SQL injection in Progress MOVEit Transfer.
* [SN 0929 - Operation Triangle](../../sources/sn-0929.md) (2023-06-27) - Episode covering Kaspersky's analysis of the TriangleDB iPhone implant (Operation Triangulation), plus new browsers from DuckDuckGo and Opera, Russian hosting rules, face-unlock weaknesses and MOVEit lawsuits.
* [SN 0930 - Rowhammer Indelible Fingerprinting](../../sources/sn-0930.md) (2023-07-11) - Episode on a fourth round of MOVEit patches, Avast's sold browsing data, China's OpenKylin OS, Firefox Quarantined Domains, crypto-theft statistics and the Centauri Rowhammer DRAM fingerprinting paper.
* [SN 0931 - SATELLITE INSECURITY, PART 1](../../sources/sn-0931.md) (2023-07-18) - Episode on the July 2023 Patch Tuesday zero-days (including the unfixed Storm-0978/RomCom Office flaw), WormGPT, revoked Windows drivers, Clop's MOVEit victims, Google Play developer verification and the first part of a look at satellite security.
* [SN 0932 - Satellite Insecurity, Part 2](../../sources/sn-0932.md) (2023-07-25) - Episode on Apple's threat to pull FaceTime and iMessage from the UK, Google's Web Environment Integrity proposal, the EU Cyber Resilience Act, falling ransomware payment rates and the "Space Odyssey" paper on vulnerable satellite firmware.
* [SN 0933 - TETRA:BURST](../../sources/sn-0933.md) (2023-08-01) - Episode on APT groups using satellite links for command and control, Apple and Google tracker rules, VirusTotal's 2023 malware trends, listener satellite expertise, cloud repatriation and the TETRA:BURST flaws in the secret TETRA radio ciphers.
* [SN 0934 - Revisiting Global Privacy Control](../../sources/sn-0934.md) (2023-08-08) - Episode on Tenable CEO Amit Yoran's criticism of Microsoft's Azure vulnerability handling, Schneier on the stolen Azure signing key, a deep-learning keyboard acoustic attack, authentication advice and Global Privacy Control revisited via Yahoo's opt-out pop-up.
* [SN 0935 - "Topics" Arrives](../../sources/sn-0935.md) (2023-08-15) - Episode marking the podcast's 18th birthday and Steve's decision to continue past episode 999, with a detailed explanation of Google's Topics API in Chrome and listener feedback on password rules, Firefox containers and SSD encryption.
* [SN 0936 - When Heuristics Backfire](../../sources/sn-0936.md) (2023-08-22) - Episode on SUSE's planned private-equity buyout, the SanDisk/Western Digital SSD data-loss lawsuit, crypto multi-party wallet flaws, Chrome's HTTPS upgrade plans and Windows Secure Time Seeding wrongly resetting server clocks.
* [SN 0937 - The Man in the Middle](../../sources/sn-0937.md) (2023-08-29) - Episode on the actively exploited WinRAR CVE-2023-38831, HTTPS versus local HTTP, the TLS gmt_unix_time field behind Windows Secure Time Seeding, fake flash drives found by SpinRite, and how practical man-in-the-middle attacks really are.
* [SN 0938 - Apple Says No](../../sources/sn-0938.md) (2023-09-05) - Episode previewing GRC's ValiDrive fake-storage checker, Topics reaching Android apps, Apple's Security Research Device program, a University of Wisconsin study of plaintext passwords exposed to browser extensions, and Apple's public refusal to scan iCloud for CSAM.
* [SN 0939 - LastMess](../../sources/sn-0939.md) (2023-09-12) - Episode (Jason Howell filling in) on the UK softening its Online Safety Bill encryption stance, Microsoft's explanation of the Storm-0558 stolen signing key, Mozilla's finding that all 25 car brands fail on privacy, and evidence that stolen LastPass vaults are being cracked to drain crypto wallets.
* [SN 0940 - When Hashes Collide](../../sources/sn-0940.md) (2023-09-19) - Episode on how hardware security modules sign without revealing keys, how to securely erase SSDs and hard drives without repeated overwrites, Steve's plan to replace Twitter with email lists, and deliberate truncated-hash collisions for pseudonymizing library patron data.
* [SN 0941 - We Told You So!](../../sources/sn-0941.md) (2023-09-26) - Episode (Ant Pruitt filling in) on Apple dropping PostScript from macOS, China accusing the NSA of hacking Huawei, Microsoft's over-permissive SAS token exposing 38 TB, Signal's post-quantum PQXDH, why new browsers are impossible, and a Predator spyware infection of an Egyptian politician through an injected plain-HTTP redirect.
* [SN 0942 - Encrypting Client Hello](../../sources/sn-0942.md) (2023-10-03) - Episode on the unpatched Exim mail server flaws disclosed after ZDI's year-long wait, malvertising inside Bing Chat, Windows 11 passkeys, the overhyped Wiki-Eve Wi-Fi keystroke attack, and a deep dive into SNI, ESNI and Encrypted Client Hello.
* [SN 0943 - The Top 10 Cybersecurity Misconfigurations](../../sources/sn-0943.md) (2023-10-10) - Security Now episode on the 23andMe breach, Microsoft's 2023 Digital Defense Report statistics, shrinking ransomware dwell time, and the start of the NSA/CISA Top 10 misconfigurations advisory.
* [SN 0944 - Abusing HTTP/2 Rapid Reset](../../sources/sn-0944.md) (2023-10-17) - Security Now episode explaining the HTTP/2 Rapid Reset DDoS technique (CVE-2023-44487) behind record 201-398 million request-per-second attacks, plus ValiDrive follow-ups and passkey/OTP listener feedback.
* [SN 0945 - The Power of Privilege](../../sources/sn-0945.md) (2023-10-24) - Security Now episode covering the Cisco IOS XE zero-days (CVSS 10.0, 42,000 devices), the curl SOCKS5 overflow, VBScript and NTLM deprecation, Privacy Badger, and the NSA/CISA privilege-separation misconfiguration.
* [SN 0946 - Citrix Bleed](../../sources/sn-0946.md) (2023-10-31) - Security Now episode with a code-level walkthrough of the Citrix NetScaler "Citrix Bleed" information leak (CVE-2023-4966), plus whitelist DNS filtering, LastPass vault thefts and Apple contact key verification.
* [SN 0947 - Article 45](../../sources/sn-0947.md) (2023-11-07) - Security Now episode on the EU eIDAS Article 45 mandate forcing browsers to trust government root certificates, plus Microsoft's Secure Future Initiative, unpatched Exchange flaws, ActiveMQ, Citrix Bleed follow-up and CVSS 4.0.
* [SN 0948 - What if a Bit Flipped?](../../sources/sn-0948.md) (2023-11-14) - Security Now episode on Intel's Downfall (Gather Data Sampling) flaw, passive SSH private-key recovery from faulty RSA signatures, crypto heists, eIDAS Article 45 follow-up and the SpinRite 6.1 completion.
* [SN 0949 - Ethernet Turned 50](../../sources/sn-0949.md) (2023-11-21) - Security Now episode on Signal's funding costs, ALPHV filing an SEC complaint against its own victim, TETRA radio algorithms going public, Apple adopting RCS, the QWAC debate, and Ethernet's 50th anniversary.
* [SN 0950 - Leo Turns 67](../../sources/sn-0950.md) (2023-11-28) - Security Now episode (hosted with Ant Pruitt) on lingering Adobe Flash updaters, Firefox 120 privacy features, a German Do Not Track ruling, ownCloud and CrushFTP critical flaws, fingerprint-sensor bypasses and signing cameras.
* [SN 0951 - Revisiting Browser Trust](../../sources/sn-0951.md) (2023-12-05) - Security Now episode on ICANN's WHOIS data request service, WhatsApp Secret Code, Iranian attacks on default-password Unitronics PLCs, Montana's blocked TikTok ban, the EU Cyber Resilience Act, France and Olvid, and the CA market data behind the eIDAS Article 45 fight.
* [SN 0952 - Quantum Computing Breakthrough](../../sources/sn-0952.md) (2023-12-12) - Security Now episode on Senator Wyden's push-notification metadata letter, Messenger default encryption, the most exploited vulnerabilities of 2023, Log4j two years on, Quad9's court win over Sony, the Dark Web, and Harvard's error-corrected logical-qubit result.
* [SN 0953 - Active Listening](../../sources/sn-0953.md) (2023-12-19) - Security Now episode on Meta's fight with the FTC and stalled US child-protection bills, Chrome's third-party cookie phase-out, Cloudflare's 2023 Internet statistics, SpinRite 6.1 code-signing progress, and Cox Media Group's "Active Listening" ad pitch.
* [SN 0955 - The Mystery of CVE-2023-38606](../../sources/sn-0955.md) (2024-01-02) - Security Now episode devoted to Kaspersky's Operation Triangulation findings, in particular an undocumented, hash-protected hardware feature in Apple A12 to A16 chips (CVE-2023-38606), plus SpinRite 6.1 status and root-store pruning tools.
* [SN 0956 - The Inside Tracks](../../sources/sn-0956.md) (2024-01-09) - Security Now episode with Steve's theory that Apple's silicon backdoor was a deliberate last-resort feature, plus Russia's destructive Kyivstar attack, 23andMe fallout, 2023 crypto theft totals, the Merck NotPetya insurance settlement, LastPass account changes, and SpinRite disk-speed research.
* [SN 0957 - The Protected Audience API](../../sources/sn-0957.md) (2024-01-16) - Security Now episode on a compromised smart washing machine and SourceHut's DDoS outage, the KyberSlash timing flaw in post-quantum Kyber implementations, Chrome's browser dominance, and Google's Privacy Sandbox with on-device ad auctions (Protected Audience API).
* [SN 0958 - A Week of News and Listener Views](../../sources/sn-0958.md) (2024-01-23) - Midnight Blizzard's password-spray breach of Microsoft executives' email, the HHS grant-payment theft, Mozilla's "Platform Tilt" complaints, Brave dropping Strict fingerprinting mode, and listener Q&A.
* [SN 0959 - Stamos on "Microsoft Security"](../../sources/sn-0959.md) (2024-01-30) - Apple's EU Digital Markets Act browser-engine changes, the MOAB mega-leak, HP printer bricking, Mercedes-Benz GitHub token exposure, SpinRite 6.1 declared finished, and Alex Stamos's critique of Microsoft's Midnight Blizzard response.
* [SN 0960 - UNFORESEEN CONSEQUENCES](../../sources/sn-0960.md) (2024-02-06) - CISA/FBI secure-by-design guidance for SOHO routers, glibc vulnerabilities from Qualys, Fastly dropping OpenSSL for BoringSSL, passkey-upgrade discovery, PLC exposure, and the prediction that the end of third-party cookies will push sites to force user sign-ups.
* [SN 0961 - BitLocker: Chipped or Cracked?](../../sources/sn-0961.md) (2024-02-13) - The debunked three-million-toothbrush botnet story, the flood of honeypots skewing Shodan counts, passwordless email login design, first-party sign-up walls as the consequence of the cookie phase-out, and the discrete-TPM BitLocker sniffing attack.
* [SN 0962 - The Internet Dodged a Bullet](../../sources/sn-0962.md) (2024-02-20) - The KeyTrap DNSSEC algorithmic-complexity vulnerability and its quiet fix, plus the Wyze camera cross-account exposure, February 2024 Patch Tuesday, Canada's Flipper Zero ban and listener deep dives.
* [SN 0963 - Web portal?  Yes, please!](../../sources/sn-0963.md) (2024-02-27) - Nevada's bid to block end-to-end encryption for minors on Messenger, the ConnectWise ScreenConnect authentication bypass, the LockBit takedown, Chrome's V8 optimizer switch, SpinRite 6.1 release and a hardened email-link login design.
* [SN 0964 - PQ3](../../sources/sn-0964.md) (2024-03-05) - Apple's PQ3 post-quantum iMessage protocol and its marketing claims versus Signal, plus listener feedback on passwordless email login, bcrypt limits and a responsible-disclosure dilemma.
* [SN 0965 - Passkeys vs 2FA](../../sources/sn-0965.md) (2024-03-12) - Why a passkey is stronger than any strong password plus second factor, alongside the Change Healthcare ransomware aftermath, VMware USB escape bugs, Microsoft's Midnight Blizzard breach, China's Document 79 and Signal usernames.
* [SN 0966 - Morris the Second](../../sources/sn-0966.md) (2024-03-19) - Cornell researchers' Morris II zero-click worm against generative-AI assistants, plus passkey storage feedback, Tim Berners-Lee's web anniversary essay and automakers selling driving data to insurers.
* [SN 0967 - GoFetch](../../sources/sn-0967.md) (2024-03-26) - A deep dive on the GoFetch side-channel attack on Apple M-series data memory-dependent prefetchers, with the DOJ v. Apple antitrust suit, GM driving-data sharing stopped, the .internal TLD and Pwn2Own 2024.
* [SN 0968 - A Cautionary Tale](../../sources/sn-0968.md) (2024-04-02) - The XZ Utils / liblzma backdoor in sshd (a multi-year supply-chain infiltration caught by chance), plus a Linux kernel root bug, the AT&T 73-million-record leak, and Incognito Mode lawsuit settlement.
* [SN 0969 - Minimum Viable Secure Product](../../sources/sn-0969.md) (2024-04-09) - The MVSP application-security checklist gains CISA as a contributor, alongside a premature public disclosure of a D-Link NAS backdoor, Privnote phishing clones, rising zero-day prices and a Voyager 1 diagnosis.
* [SN 0970 - GhostRace](../../sources/sn-0970.md) (2024-04-16) - GhostRace speculative race conditions that bypass OS synchronisation primitives, plus AT&T's revised 51-million breach count, a 341,650-SSN consultancy breach, poor cookie-banner compliance and Outlook's 772 third parties.
* [SN 0971 - Chat (out of) Control](../../sources/sn-0971.md) (2024-04-23) - The EU Chat Control (CSAM scanning) draft with carve-outs for government communications, the Fuxnet attack on Russian sensor gateways, Voyager 1's repaired data, Gentoo's AI ban and Google delaying third-party cookie removal.
* [SN 0972 - Passkeys:  A Shattered Dream?](../../sources/sn-0972.md) (2024-04-30) - The UK PSTI Act's consumer IoT security law takes effect, Chrome delays its third-party cookie phase-out, and a WebAuthn library author's "Passkeys: a shattered dream" blog post is discussed.
* [SN 0973 - Not So Fast](../../sources/sn-0973.md) (2024-05-07) - GPS jamming and spoofing in the Baltics, the TunnelVision DHCP Option 121 VPN-bypass technique, Passkey portability questions, a possible cause for vanishing iCloud Keychain items, and the UK CMA's review of Google's cookie phase-out.
* [SN 0974 - Microsoft's Head in the Clouds](../../sources/sn-0974.md) (2024-05-14) - A heat map of 3.4 million breached four-digit PINs, eLORAN as a GPS backup, Passkey listener feedback, and Microsoft's Secure Future Initiative after the CSRB report on the Storm-0558 Exchange Online breach.
* [SN 0975 - 312 SCIENTISTS & RESEARCHERS RESPOND](../../sources/sn-0975.md) (2024-05-21) - Three Chrome zero-days in one week, Google's AI Overviews and the udm=14 web-search trick, an exposed Windows XP test, a laundry-app payment flaw, tracking after third-party cookies, and the 312-signatory scientists' letter against the EU CSAM regulation.
* [SN 0976 - The 50 Gigabyte Privacy Bomb](../../sources/sn-0976.md) (2024-05-28) - Google AI Overview failures and ways to suppress it, LastPass finally encrypting vault URLs, GRC's new listener email system, and Steve's critique of Microsoft's Windows 11 Recall screen-snapshot feature.
* [SN 0977 - A Large Language Model in Every Pot](../../sources/sn-0977.md) (2024-06-04) - Simplicity lessons from Tornado Notes, GRC's first week of email, a Telegram combolist breach, a Stack Overflow malware lure, Chrome distrusting GlobalTrust, ICQ's shutdown, and Kevin Beaumont's findings on Windows Recall with Steve's theory about a personal local LLM.
* [SN 0978 - The rise and fall of code.microsoft.com](../../sources/sn-0978.md) (2024-06-11) - Microsoft's revised Recall safeguards, Apple's Passwords app, the Linux kernel CVE flood, AI and coding careers, hardware-only code signing keys, and Microsoft's accidental honeypot on a dangling code.microsoft.com subdomain.
* [SN 0979 - THE ANGLE OF THE DANGLE](../../sources/sn-0979.md) (2024-06-18) - The Windows Wi-Fi driver flaw CVE-2024-30078, Recall pulled from the Copilot+ launch, Apple's Private Cloud Compute design, a bogus WGET CVSS 10 scare, French DNS blocking orders, and dangling-CNAME subdomain takeover.
* [SN 0980 - The Mixed Blessing of a Crappy PRNG](../../sources/sn-0980.md) (2024-06-25) - Weaponisation of the Windows Wi-Fi flaw, the US Commerce Department ban on Kaspersky, a noyb complaint against Privacy Sandbox, GRC email tracking pixels, Orange Tsai's US visa problem, and how RoboForm's time-seeded password generator let researchers recover a lost bitcoin wallet.
* [SN 0981 - The End of Entrust Trust](../../sources/sn-0981.md) (2024-07-02) - The regreSSHion OpenSSH race-condition flaw CVE-2024-6387, a dormant Satoshi-era bitcoin wallet moving, Voyager 1 recovery, GRC email and nuevoMailer, Syncthing relay slowdowns, and Chrome's decision to distrust Entrust certificates.
* [SN 0982 - The Polyfill.io Attack](../../sources/sn-0982.md) (2024-07-09) - Entrust's response to Chrome's distrust and competing CAs' marketing, the "Passkey Redaction" phishing claim, port knocking and Fail2Ban as layered defences against the OpenSSH flaw, and the Polyfill.io supply-chain attack by a new Chinese owner.
* [SN 0983 - A Snowflake's Chance](../../sources/sn-0983.md) (2024-07-16) - Subresource Integrity as the safe way to use CDNs, the CDK Global ransomware outage of 15,000 car dealers, IRS.gov's Entrust certificate, Polyfill aftermath, a listener's case that Microsoft will push Recall, and the Snowflake customer-data breaches.
* [SN 0984 - CrowdStruck](../../sources/sn-0984.md) (2024-07-23) - The CrowdStrike Falcon channel-file crash that took down about 8.5 million Windows machines, plus Cellebrite's phone-unlocking limits, Cisco's CVSS 10 flaw, Entrust's SSL.com workaround, and Google abandoning third-party cookie deprecation.
* [SN 0985 - Platform Key Disclosure](../../sources/sn-0985.md) (2024-07-30) - CrowdStrike's root-cause explanation and customer reactions, Entrust's SSL.com-backed plan, Firefox "Strict" mode not blocking cross-site cookies, a North Korean fake hire at KnowBe4, and Binarly's PKfail finding of untrusted Secure Boot platform keys in nearly 850 devices.
* [SN 0986 - How Revoking!](../../sources/sn-0986.md) (2024-08-06) - PKfail follow-up and the new IsBootSecure? tool, Firefox and W3C positions on third-party cookies, CrowdStrike lawsuits, and DigiCert's mass certificate revocation that browsers ignore anyway.
* [SN 0987 - Rethinking Revocation](../../sources/sn-0987.md) (2024-08-13) - Sitting Ducks DNS hijacking, the MadLicense RDL flaw, AMD Sinkclose, an unfixed CLFS blue-screen bug, IsBootSecure? update, and why OCSP stapling is being replaced by browser-side CRLs.
* [SN 0988 - National Public Data](../../sources/sn-0988.md) (2024-08-20) - The National Public Data breach (about 2.9 billion rows, Social Security Numbers), the OCSP experiment on revoked.grc.com showing only Firefox checks revocation, August 2024 Patch Tuesday zero-days, and IsBootSecure? release 3.
* [SN 0989 - Cascading Bloom Filters](../../sources/sn-0989.md) (2024-08-27) - A hardware backdoor in Fudan Microelectronics MIFARE-style RFID cards, counterfeit Cisco gear, a SpinRite SSD recovery story, and a deep dive into cascading Bloom filters (Mozilla CRLite) as local, instant certificate revocation.
* [SN 0990 - Is Telegram an Encrypted App?](../../sources/sn-0990.md) (2024-09-03) - Pavel Durov's arrest in France and Matthew Green's argument that Telegram is not meaningfully an encrypted messenger, plus Microsoft's free security logging, CrowdStrike fallout, Chrome bounty increases, Cox Media Group "Active Listening", and a fake-employee solicitation.
* [SN 0991 - RAMBO](../../sources/sn-0991.md) (2024-09-10) - Windows Recall uninstall "bug", the YubiKey 5 side-channel cloning research, listener feedback on Telegram vs WhatsApp and credit freezes, and the RAMBO air-gap covert channel that leaks data as radio signals from RAM.
* [SN 0992 - Password Manager Injection Attacks](../../sources/sn-0992.md) (2024-09-17) - Microsoft's post-CrowdStrike endpoint security summit, fragility of long-term digital archives, an unauthorized Starlink network on a US Navy ship, SpinRite 6.1 mailing lessons, and research showing ten password managers leaked secrets through injection side channels.
* [SN 0993 - Kaspersky Exits the U.S.](../../sources/sn-0993.md) (2024-09-24) - The Hezbollah pager and radio supply-chain attack, Ford's in-car ad patent, the MC2 Data exposure of 106 million records, native Passkeys in Chrome, an unresolved Linux unauthenticated RCE claim, and Kaspersky's abrupt swap to UltraAV on leaving the US.
* [SN 0994 - Recall's Re-Rollout](../../sources/sn-0994.md) (2024-10-01) - The full story of the CUPS Linux vulnerabilities, DNS hijack risks after a brief registrar takeover, Tor and Tails merging, Telegram's new cooperation policy, and a review of Microsoft's redesigned Windows Recall security architecture.
* [SN 0995 - uBlock Origin & Manifest V3](../../sources/sn-0995.md) (2024-10-08) - Meta's fine for plaintext passwords, PayPal's default purchase-history sharing, a record 3.8 Tbps DDoS from hijacked ASUS routers, CUPS used for DDoS amplification, router advice, and Chrome's Manifest V3 squeeze on uBlock Origin including a policy workaround.
* [SN 0996 - BIMI (Up Scotty)](../../sources/sn-0996.md) (2024-10-15) - More uBlock Origin annoyance-filter tips, National Public Data's bankruptcy, the .io domain's uncertain future, a Firefox zero-day used against Tor users, the Internet Archive breach, and a first-hand account of obtaining a BIMI verified-logo certificate.
* [SN 0997 - Credential Exchange Protocol](../../sources/sn-0997.md) (2024-10-22) - A reality check on the claimed quantum break of RSA, NPD-fueled extortion emails, the EU's new no-fault software product liability directive, Microsoft's lost logs and honeypots, DJI's lawsuit, Pentagon interest in deepfake personas, and the FIDO Credential Exchange Protocol for passkey portability.
* [SN 0998 - The Endless Journey to IPv6](../../sources/sn-0998.md) (2024-10-29) - Apple's proposal to cut TLS certificate lifetimes to 45 days, SEC fines over SolarWinds disclosures, Google Messages on-device content filtering, the Session messenger, more on EU software liability, and why IPv6 adoption has stalled.
* [SN 0999 - AI Vulnerability Discovery](../../sources/sn-0999.md) (2024-11-05) - Russia's absurd Google fine, Windows 10 end-of-support options including 0patch, a hijacked Chrome extension, Midnight Blizzard's malicious RDP files, a fake-CAPTCHA clipboard attack, GRC's planned DNS Benchmark 2.0, and Google's AI agent finding a SQLite flaw.
* [SN 1000 - One Thousand!](../../sources/sn-1000.md) (2024-11-12) - Episode 1000 of Security Now! covers the Bitwarden licensing scare, Windows Server 2025 surprise upgrades, Microsoft's Admin Protection, the Synology RISK:STATION flaw, a typosquatted PyPI package and a long listener-feedback section.
* [SN 1001 - Artificial General Intelligence (AGI)](../../sources/sn-1001.md) (2024-11-19) - Episode 1001 of Security Now! covers Microsoft's "White House Offer" lock-in of US government customers, Gmail throwaway aliases, Russia's Internet disconnect tests, an NTLM-leaking .URL file zero-day, remote-access listener tips and a long look at what AGI means and whether it is near.
* [SN 1002 - Disconnected Experiences](../../sources/sn-1002.md) (2024-11-26) - Episode 1002 of Security Now! covers the "Nearest Neighbor" Wi-Fi pivot attack by APT28, Let's Encrypt turning 10, Chinese port cranes, Palo Alto firewall zero-days, end-of-life D-Link VPN routers, the return of Windows Recall and Microsoft Office "Connected Experiences".
* [SN 1003 - A Light-Day Away](../../sources/sn-1003.md) (2024-12-03) - Episode 1003 of Security Now! covers Microsoft's denial of AI training on Office data, "digital epileptic seizures" in driver-assistance cameras, Tor WebTunnel bridges, a Zello password reset, an FTC antitrust probe, Android scareware, WireGuard exposure, model collapse, Australia's age law and Voyager 1.
* [SN 1004 - A Chat with GPT](../../sources/sn-1004.md) (2024-12-10) - Episode 1004 of Security Now! covers the Salt Typhoon telecom breach and the call for end-to-end encryption, the TPM 2.0 requirement for Windows 11, a public Microsoft activation crack, FTC action on data brokers, GRC's BIMI logo, authenticator policy and Steve's experience correcting ChatGPT on assembler syntax.
* [SN 1005 - Six-Day Certificates?  Why?](../../sources/sn-1005.md) (2024-12-17) - Episode 1005 of Security Now! covers Steve's shift toward seeing LLMs as an illusion of intelligence, a rate-limit flaw in Microsoft's Azure MFA, the unchanged TPM 2.0 policy, North Korean IT workers, malicious NPM packages and Let's Encrypt's plan for six-day certificates.
* [SN 1007 - AI Training & Inference](../../sources/sn-1007.md) (2025-01-07) - Episode 1007 of Security Now! covers US state age-verification laws, the Cyberhaven Chrome extension supply-chain compromise, unpatched SonicWall firewalls exploited by ransomware, 3.3 million servers without email encryption, Salt Typhoon evictions, HIPAA changes and Steve's primer on AI training versus test-time inference.
* [SN 1008 - HOTP & TOTP](../../sources/sn-1008.md) (2025-01-14) - Episode 1008 of Security Now! covers Meta ending third-party fact-checking, hijacked abandoned malware domains, the US Cyber Trust Mark, email encryption feedback and a deep dive into how HOTP and TOTP one-time codes are generated.
* [SN 1009 - Attacking TOTP](../../sources/sn-1009.md) (2025-01-21) - Episode 1009 of Security Now! covers January 2025's huge Patch Tuesday, Microsoft force-installing the new Outlook, the FTC order against GoDaddy, UK and US age-verification enforcement, exposed FortiGate management interfaces, DJI dropping geofencing and a brute-force analysis of 80-bit TOTP keys.
* [SN 1010 - DNS Over TLS](../../sources/sn-1010.md) (2025-01-28) - Mastercard's five-year DNS typo (akam.ne), a trojanized XWorm RAT builder hitting script kiddies, record 5.6 Tbps DDoS, Let's Encrypt six-day certificates, and Steve's DNS Benchmark findings on DNS over TLS.
* [SN 1011 - Jailbreaking AI](../../sources/sn-1011.md) (2025-02-04) - DeepSeek's emergence, ban and exposed database, Google's report on threat actors misusing Gemini, and a close look at AI jailbreak techniques (Bad Likert Judge, Crescendo, Deceptive Delight), plus the Zyxel router bug, ROUTERS Act and FADPA.
* [SN 1012 - Hiding School Cyberattacks](../../sources/sn-1012.md) (2025-02-11) - SparkCat OCR stealer in app stores, the UK's secret demand for an Apple iCloud backdoor, Firefox 135, Five Eyes edge-device guidance, Netgear and Sysinternals flaws, AI jailbreak fuzzing, and an investigation into how U.S. K-12 districts conceal ransomware attacks.
* [SN 1013 - Chrome Web Store Is a Mess](../../sources/sn-1013.md) (2025-02-18) - US lawmakers push back on the UK order against Apple's Advanced Data Protection, plus Patch Tuesday, RansomHub, TOAD scams, Texas v. DeepSeek and a deep dive into why the Chrome Web Store is unreliable.
* [SN 1014 - FREEDOM Administration Login](../../sources/sn-1014.md) (2025-02-25) - Apple pulls Advanced Data Protection for new UK users, RansomHub sanctions risk, LaLiga's Cloudflare IP blocking, OpenSSH flaws, Salt Typhoon's Cisco entry route, the record Bybit heist and an internet-exposed apartment access-control system with default credentials.
* [SN 1015 - Spatial-Domain Wireless Jamming](../../sources/sn-1015.md) (2025-03-04) - Firefox privacy-policy wording row, Signal threatening to leave Sweden, Bybit heist aftermath via Safe{Wallet}, Mozilla keeping Manifest V2, the case for memory-safe languages, and RIS-based targeted WiFi jamming research.
* [SN 1016 - The Bluetooth Backdoor](../../sources/sn-1016.md) (2025-03-11) - Utah's app-store age verification law and Apple's Declared Age Range API, North Korean fake IT workers, Bybit laundering and Safe{Wallet} post-mortem, Apple's tribunal appeal against the UK order, an IoT-webcam ransomware case and a debunking of the ESP32 "backdoor".
* [SN 1017 - Is YOUR System Vulnerable to Rowhammer?](../../sources/sn-1017.md) (2025-03-18) - Formal cryptanalysis of Telegram's key exchange, the X outage attribution, Firefox root certificate expiry, a Windows-only PHP-CGI flaw (CVE-2024-4577), Google's and Apple's age-signal proposals, and the FLIPPYR.AM tool for testing real-world Rowhammer susceptibility.
* [SN 1018 - THE QUANTUM THREAT](../../sources/sn-1018.md) (2025-03-25) - The Signal group-chat leak by US officials, ClickFix-style fake CAPTCHA attacks, Espressif's ESP32 response, Windows LNK padding abuse, a Tomcat RCE, a 10/10 BMC flaw, RCS end-to-end encryption, 23andMe's bankruptcy and an HP overview of how close the quantum threat to cryptography really is.
* [SN 1019 - EU OS](../../sources/sn-1019.md) (2025-04-01) - Kuala Lumpur airport refuses a ransom, Troy Hunt's phishing incident, Cloudflare closing HTTP port 80 for its API, malware moving to obscure languages, password-reuse statistics, the RansomLook victim site and the EU OS proposal for a Fedora/KDE public-sector desktop.
* [SN 1020 - Multi-Perspective Issuance Corroboration](../../sources/sn-1020.md) (2025-04-08) - Canon printer driver flaws, a model IoT security stance from Fisher and Paykel, WordPress mu-plugins abuse, Oracle's unacknowledged breaches, AI crawlers overloading open-source sites, Gmail client-side encryption, a CVSS 10 in Apache Parquet and the new CA/Browser Forum requirement for multi-perspective domain validation.
* [SN 1021 - Device Bound Session Credentials](../../sources/sn-1021.md) (2025-04-15) - Android Advanced Protection Mode, Oracle Cloud breach denials, Treasury OCC email breach, AI package hallucination and slopsquatting, a PHP audit, and a deep dive into Device Bound Session Credentials as a session-cookie replacement.
* [SN 1022 - Windows Sandbox](../../sources/sn-1022.md) (2025-04-22) - CVE program funding scare and CVE Foundation, China acknowledging infrastructure hacking, CISA on Oracle, credential-spraying trends, BentoML deserialization flaw, the CA/Browser Forum vote for 47-day certificates, and a deep dive into Windows Sandbox.
* [SN 1023 - Preventing Windows Sandbox Abuse](../../sources/sn-1023.md) (2025-04-29) - The mysterious inetpub folder from April's Windows patch and the junction-point update-blocking bug, North Korean fake U.S. companies luring job seekers, the Inception AI jailbreak, SSD data decay, and how malware such as MirrorFace hides inside Windows Sandbox and how to prevent it.
* [SN 1024 - Don't Blame Signal](../../sources/sn-1024.md) (2025-05-06) - Microsoft makes new accounts passwordless by default, Meta glasses privacy changes, AI writing 30% of Microsoft code, Google's Chrome security argument, a six-year-old Magento plugin backdoor, and the hack of TeleMessage's insecure Signal clone used by U.S. officials.
* [SN 1025 - Secure Conversation Records Retention](../../sources/sn-1025.md) (2025-05-13) - Security Now episode on Virginia and New Zealand social media age limits, a long-lived PyPI RAT, FBI advice on end-of-life routers, the WhatsApp protocol analysis, the UK NCSC AI outlook, and how to archive Signal chats without breaking end-to-end encryption.
* [SN 1026 - Rogue Comms Tech Found in U.S. Power Grid](../../sources/sn-1026.md) (2025-05-20) - Security Now episode on Chrome refusing admin launch, Android Key Verifier, AI added to Pwn2Own, an AI self-replication paper, the 23andMe sale, and Reuters reporting of undocumented radios in Chinese solar inverters and batteries.
* [SN 1027 - Artificial Intelligence](../../sources/sn-1027.md) (2025-05-27) - Security Now episode on the real-world status of Encrypted Client Hello, listener feedback on inverter kill switches and grid stability, and a run of AI stories covering hype cycles, blackmail and shutdown-avoidance tests, Copilot-written .NET patches and the privacy-focused Venice AI.
* [SN 1028 - AI Vulnerability Hunting](../../sources/sn-1028.md) (2025-06-03) - Security Now episode covering Pwn2Own Berlin 2025 results, PayPal's newly-registered-domain patent filing, a veteran iOS jailbreaker explaining why Apple's iOS 14 redesign ended public kernel exploits, SVG-based phishing, and how OpenAI's o3 helped find a Linux kernel SMB zero-day.
* [SN 1029 - The Illusion of Thinking](../../sources/sn-1029.md) (2025-06-10) - Security Now episode on Meta and Yandex localhost web-to-app tracking, the EU's DNS4EU resolver, a court order forcing OpenAI to keep all chats, a CVSS 10 flaw in Erlang/OTP SSH, and Apple's puzzle-based study of whether large reasoning models really reason.
* [SN 1030 - Internet Foreground Radiation](../../sources/sn-1030.md) (2025-06-17) - Security Now episode on the disputed iMessage NICKNAME zero-click report, npm supply-chain attacks, Salt Typhoon links to Comcast and Digital Realty, XChat key storage, Outlook blocking two more file types, 40,000 exposed cameras, and bots that scan new websites within minutes.
* [SN 1031 - How Salt Typhoon Gets In](../../sources/sn-1031.md) (2025-06-24) - Security Now episode on new Salt Typhoon victims (Viasat, a Canadian telecom), health-exchange website trackers, Apple adopting the FIDO credential exchange format, Austria's messaging-monitoring plan, Steve's view of AI as a linguistic simulation, and a deep dive into how Salt Typhoon used unpatched Cisco IOS XE devices.
* [SN 1032 - Pervasive Web Fingerprinting](../../sources/sn-1032.md) (2025-07-01) - Security Now episode on Let's Encrypt ending expiry emails, Microsoft's crash-screen and recovery changes, Windows 10 ESU, Russia's RuStore mandate, memory-safe language guidance, AI prompt injection in malware, two new Cisco ISE flaws, US critical-infrastructure partnership strain, and the first study linking browser fingerprinting to ad tracking.
* [SN 1033 - Going on the Offensive](../../sources/sn-1033.md) (2025-07-08) - Paragon's Graphite spyware, EU sovereignty and post-quantum timelines, Bitcoin ATM scam laws, LibXML2's burned-out maintainer, WinRAR flaw, Sophos ransomware entry statistics, Texas age verification ruling, and an Atlantic Council study on the U.S. zero-day acquisition supply chain.
* [SN 1034 - Introduction to Zero-Knowledge Proofs](../../sources/sn-1034.md) (2025-07-15) - Peter Gutmann's takedown of quantum factorization records, Notepad++'s self-signed root certificate, WhatsApp versus Signal data-at-rest, browser fingerprinting measured with EFF's Cover Your Tracks, and an introduction to zero-knowledge proofs through Wally, locked-box and Ali Baba's cave examples.
* [SN 1035 - Cloudflare's 1.1.1.1 Outage](../../sources/sn-1035.md) (2025-07-22) - A cross-device passkey phishing bypass (PoisonSeed), Cloudflare's UK blocking for the MPA, mounting age-verification pressure and a protocol sketch, Russia criminalizing searches, China's Massistant phone forensics, Exchange ESU, and a detailed account of Cloudflare's 62-minute 1.1.1.1 resolver outage.
* [SN 1036 - Inside the SharePoint 0-day RCE](../../sources/sn-1036.md) (2025-07-29) - The ToolShell SharePoint zero-day born from Microsoft's botched Pwn2Own patch, plus Clorox v. Cognizant, Allianz Life breach, Brave news and the retracted FIDO passkey MITM claim.
* [SN 1037 - Chinese Participation in MAPP](../../sources/sn-1037.md) (2025-08-05) - Follow-ups on the SharePoint patch mess (China-based Microsoft engineers), Russian embassy interception via a fake Kaspersky update, ICANN's breach notice to WebNIC, TruAge/mDL age verification, and whether Chinese firms should stay in Microsoft's MAPP early-notice program.
* [SN 1038 - Perplexity's Duplicity](../../sources/sn-1038.md) (2025-08-12) - CISA's emergency directive on Exchange hybrid configurations, NVIDIA's no-backdoor statement, SonicWall SSL VPN compromises, Dell ReVault firmware flaws, and Cloudflare's finding that Perplexity evades robots.txt and bot blocks.
* [SN 1039 - The Sad Case of Scriptcase](../../sources/sn-1039.md) (2025-08-19) - AI summaries and web economics, Plex server update, Allianz Life leak by ShinyHunters/Scattered Spider, Chrome 140 privacy features, NIST's Ascon lightweight crypto standard, Syncthing 2.0, and the Scriptcase pre-auth RCE lesson that unnecessary services should never face the Internet.
* [SN 1040 - Clickjacking "Whac-A-Mole"](../../sources/sn-1040.md) (2025-08-26) - German ad-blocker copyright case, AI copyright litigation, UK retreat on Apple encryption, Bluesky leaving Mississippi over age-assurance law, the Apple DNG zero-click flaw CVE-2025-43300, the Docker Desktop escape CVE-2025-9074, and why the DEFCON password-manager clickjacking talk is whack-a-mole rather than a catastrophe.
* [SN 1041 - Covering All the Bases](../../sources/sn-1041.md) (2025-09-02) - BYTE magazine's 50th anniversary, FCC enforcement of STIR/SHAKEN, the Salesloft Drift OAuth breach, AI controllability, the WhatsApp plus Apple zero-click spyware chain, an AI-assisted npm supply chain attack on Nx, OpenSSH post-quantum warnings, and a long listener-feedback section.
* [SN 1042 - Letters of Marque](../../sources/sn-1042.md) (2025-09-09) - Extortion of Google over two threat-intel staff, Fina CA's mis-issued 1.1.1.1 certificates, the September 2025 npm supply-chain compromise of 18 packages (chalk, debug and others), AI and vibe-coding effects on threat publication, and the debate over letters of marque for private offensive cyber operations.
* [SN 1043 - Memory Integrity Enforcement](../../sources/sn-1043.md) (2025-09-16) - Bitcoin ATM scam lawsuit, Uvalde and Jaguar Land Rover ransomware, Germany opposing Chat Control, HackerOne's Salesloft Drift exposure, record DDoS floods, conditional age verification, and Apple's Memory Integrity Enforcement (EMTE-based hardware memory tagging in the A19 chips).
* [SN 1044 - The EU's Online Age Verification](../../sources/sn-1044.md) (2025-09-23) - Consumer Reports' call to extend Windows 10 support, DeepSeek writing weaker code for disfavoured groups, the Entra ID "actor token" flaw, Chrome's sixth zero-day, Rowhammer on DDR5, Shai-Hulud-era npm malware, and the EU/Spain W3C Verifiable Credentials age-verification prototype.
* [SN 1045 - News and Listener Views](../../sources/sn-1045.md) (2025-09-30) - Gmail spam false positives and GRC's DMARC tightening, Safari fingerprint randomization, 2.3 million exposed Cisco SNMP services, Windows 10 ESU, $6 TLS certificates, Jaguar Land Rover fallout, the Neon call-recording app, Kids Web Services age verification and exposed Ollama servers.
* [SN 1046 - Google's Developer Registration Decree](../../sources/sn-1046.md) (2025-10-07) - Google's plan to require identity registration and fees from all Android developers (threatening F-Droid), plus the pending EU Chat Control vote, Imgur leaving the UK, the Discord ID leak, Battering RAM and Signal's post-quantum triple ratchet.
* [SN 1047 - RediShell's CVSS 10.0](../../sources/sn-1047.md) (2025-10-14) - The RediShell CVSS 10.0 Redis/Lua use-after-free, the collapse of the EU Chat Control vote, Salesforce data leaks and the Discord ID breach, Texas SB2420 app-store age verification and California's browser opt-out law.
* [SN 1048 - Mic-E-Mouse](../../sources/sn-1048.md) (2025-10-21) - Lawsuits against Texas SB2420, NIST's new password guidance, unencrypted geostationary satellite traffic, the October 2025 AWS outage and the Mic-E-Mouse side channel that turns optical mice into crude microphones.
* [SN 1049 - DNS Cache Poisoning Returns](../../sources/sn-1049.md) (2025-10-28) - Episode on new BIND and Unbound DNS cache-poisoning CVEs (weak PRNG), a snooping robot vacuum, Russia's planned vulnerability-reporting law, Coveware's ransomware statistics and NIST password-policy feedback.
* [SN 1050 - Here Come the AI Browsers](../../sources/sn-1050.md) (2025-11-04) - Episode on the security risks of AI-enabled browsers and prompt injection, plus Chinese buses with hidden radios, Edge and Chrome scareware blockers, OpenAI's Aardvark code scanner and BadCandy on Cisco IOS XE routers.
* [SN 1051 - Amazon Sues Perplexity](../../sources/sn-1051.md) (2025-11-11) - Episode on Amazon's lawsuit against Perplexity's Comet shopping agent, plus Nevada's no-ransom recovery, Balancer's rounding-error DeFi theft, XSLT removal from browsers and Microsoft's Whisper Leak side channel against LLM traffic.
* [SN 1052 - Global Cell Phone Tracking](../../sources/sn-1052.md) (2025-11-18) - Episode on malware-free worldwide phone tracking via SS7 sold by First Wap, plus Apple's passport-based Digital ID, Google's Private AI Compute, the Android developer-registration retreat and the Windows 11 passkey plugin API.
* [SN 1053 - Banning VPNs](../../sources/sn-1053.md) (2025-11-25) - Episode on US state bills (Wisconsin, Michigan) and UK talk of banning or age-gating VPNs, plus the EU dropping mandatory Chat Control, Sysmon coming natively to Windows 11, the EchoGram guardrail bypass, WhatsApp contact enumeration and the Cloudflare outage post-mortem.
* [SN 1054 - Bots in the Belfry](../../sources/sn-1054.md) (2025-12-02) - Episode on the Gainsight/Salesforce token breach by Scattered Lapsus$ Hunters, Cisco's Resilient Infrastructure secure-by-default pledge, Australia's under-16 social media ban, passkey and YubiKey best practices and GreyNoise's free IP bot check.
* [SN 1055 - React's Perfect 10](../../sources/sn-1055.md) (2025-12-09) - Episode on the CVSS 10.0 unauthenticated remote code execution flaw in React Server Components (CVE-2025-55182), plus India's smartphone-tracking and SIM-binding mandates, GrapheneOS leaving France, and the release of GRC's DNS Benchmark v2.
* [SN 1056 - Australia](../../sources/sn-1056.md) (2025-12-16) - Episode on the first week of Australia's under-16 social media ban and its facial age-estimation problems, plus Home Depot's ignored leaked GitHub token, AI-generated code bloat in GNOME extensions, 2025 open-source repository malware statistics, React2Shell exploitation and Let's Encrypt's growth.
* [SN 1057 - GhostPoster](../../sources/sn-1057.md) (2025-12-23) - Year-end episode on North Korea's record 2025 crypto theft, cryptominers abusing AWS and Docker, the Kimwolf Android TV botnet, free ACME certificate providers and the GhostPoster Firefox extensions hiding malware in PNG icons.
* [SN 1059 - MongoBleed](../../sources/sn-1059.md) (2026-01-06) - The MongoBleed (CVE-2025-14847) uninitialized-memory leak in MongoDB, plus shorter code-signing certificate lifetimes, ChatGPT advertising plans, PyPI security, BitLocker hardware acceleration and New York's Raspberry Pi ban.
* [SN 1060 - 3-Day Certificates](../../sources/sn-1060.md) (2026-01-13) - How Microsoft's Azure Trusted Signing issues three-day code-signing certificates and why timestamping keeps signatures valid, plus California's DROP data-broker deletion platform and Claude Code for AI-assisted programming.
* [SN 1061 - More GhostPosting](../../sources/sn-1061.md) (2026-01-20) - Follow-up showing the GhostPoster browser-extension campaign reached Edge and Chrome since 2020 with about 890,000 installs, plus DRAM-driven firewall price rises, the FTC order against GM's driving-data sales, the ANCHOR replacement for CIPAC and Let's Encrypt six-day certificates.
* [SN 1062 - VoidLink:  AI-Generated Malware](../../sources/sn-1062.md) (2026-01-27) - Security Now episode on CISA's uncertain reauthorization, Ireland's lawful interception and spyware law, Microsoft handing BitLocker recovery keys to the FBI, and Check Point's discovery of the AI-written VoidLink malware framework.
* [SN 1063 - MONGO'S TOO EASY](../../sources/sn-1063.md) (2026-02-03) - Security Now episode on the Notepad++ update-server supply-chain attack, the eScan antivirus update compromise, AI finding OpenSSL zero-days while cURL ends its bug bounty, the clipboard-paste social engineering problem, and the MongoDB ransom wave.
* [SN 1064 - Least Privilege](../../sources/sn-1064.md) (2026-02-10) - Security Now episode on uncollected GDPR fines, Western states embracing offensive cyber operations (including Cyber Command's role in Operation Midnight Hammer), CISA's end-of-support edge device directive, OpenClaw agent risks, and least privilege as shown by Coinbase's contractor breach.
* [SN 1065 - Attestation](../../sources/sn-1065.md) (2026-02-17) - Security Now episode on CPU-heavy dynamic websites, Microsoft's security leadership change, Chrome 145 Device Bound Session Credentials, malicious and spying Chrome extensions, the first malicious Outlook add-in via a hijacked domain, and Steve's experience obtaining a code signing certificate that required an attestation letter.
* [SN 1066 - Password Leakage](../../sources/sn-1066.md) (2026-02-24) - Security Now episode on CA validation-period cuts, state bills requiring 3D printers to block gun parts, the ShinyHunters Figure leak, LLM-generated passwords, ClickFix dominance, and the ETH Zurich attacks on Bitwarden, LastPass and Dashlane under a malicious-server model.
* [SN 1067 - KongTuke's CrashFix](../../sources/sn-1067.md) (2026-03-03) - Security Now episode (recorded early on a Sunday) covering AI-assisted attacks on Fortinet and Mexican government systems, age-verification laws colliding with COPPA, Meta's AI-generated CSAM tips, the Cisco SD-WAN CVSS 10.0 flaw, a SmartCard-HSM for code signing, and the CrashFix variant of the ClickFix attack.
* [SN 1069 - You can't hide from LLMs](../../sources/sn-1069.md) (2026-03-10) - Security Now episode on Claude finding Firefox vulnerabilities with Mozilla, cross-platform RCS encryption, residential-proxy SDKs in smart TVs, an OpenClaw localhost takeover, and ETH Zurich research showing LLMs can deanonymize pseudonymous online accounts at scale.
* [SN 1070 - CISA's Free Internet Scanning](../../sources/sn-1070.md) (2026-03-17) - Steve's enrollment in CISA's free Cyber Hygiene vulnerability scanning, plus Meta and TikTok dropping end-to-end encryption, residential proxy botnets, a ransomware negotiator who was also the attacker, the Zombie ZIP antivirus bypass and the debate over AI-written code.
* [SN 1071 - Bucketsquatting](../../sources/sn-1071.md) (2026-03-24) - H&R Block tax software planting a 23-year root CA with its private key, the Intoxalock breathalyzer outage, Firefox's free VPN, ad-pixel over-collection, Cisco and Ubiquiti CVSS 10 flaws, and S3 bucketsquatting.
* [SN 1072 - LiteLLM](../../sources/sn-1072.md) (2026-03-31) - The LiteLLM PyPI supply-chain compromise by TeamPCP via Trivy, plus California AB 1043 age signals, Apple's UK age checks, Russia's NEA-7, Google's 2029 Q Day, vibe-coded SaaS, ClickFix and Reddit bots.
* [SN 1073 - The FCC Bans New Consumer Routers](../../sources/sn-1073.md) (2026-04-07) - The FCC adds all foreign-made consumer routers to its Covered List, LinkedIn's BrowserGate extension scanning, Cisco source code stolen via Trivy, Anthropic's Mythos/Glasswing, and Cloudflare's EmDash.
* [SN 1074 - What Mythos Means](../../sources/sn-1074.md) (2026-04-14) - A whole episode on Anthropic's Claude Mythos Preview and Project Glasswing, its autonomous discovery and exploitation of zero-days (OpenBSD, FFmpeg, FreeBSD, Linux, browsers, wolfSSL), and what it means for software security.
* [SN 1075 - Yes.  Exactly.](../../sources/sn-1075.md) (2026-04-21) - Disgruntled researcher leaks Windows Defender zero-days, Microsoft pulls driver-developer accounts, abandoned adware domains expose networks, and the industry's Mythos-readiness paper.
* [SN 1076 - Fast16.sys](../../sources/sn-1076.md) (2026-04-28) - Bitwarden CLI hit by a Checkmarx-campaign npm supply-chain compromise, Iranian router failures, DNS Benchmark Release 5, and the 2005 Fast16 state-grade sabotage worm.
* [SN 1077 - A Browser AI API?](../../sources/sn-1077.md) (2026-05-05) - AI-vibecoded carding portal leaks 345K cards, UK NCSC warns of a vulnerability patch wave, Linux local root flaw found by AI, AI ending bug bounties, Claude Security, ChatGPT Advanced Account Security, and Chrome's Prompt API opposed by Mozilla.
* [SN 1078 - DigiCert Does It Right](../../sources/sn-1078.md) (2026-05-12) - FCC extends router firmware-update waivers, AI finds a 21-year-old FreeBSD dhclient RCE, AI model repositories are full of malware, Edge leaves passwords in memory, and a deep dive on DigiCert's exemplary breach disclosure.
* [SN 1079 - Daybreak & Codename MDASH](../../sources/sn-1079.md) (2026-05-19) - Microsoft reverses Edge's plaintext-password "intended behavior", the YellowKey BitLocker bypass, Google GTIG's first AI-built zero-day, Canada's lawful access bill, AI addiction concerns, and OpenAI Daybreak versus Microsoft's MDASH vulnerability-finding harness.
* [SN 1080 - Vulnerability Debt Repayment](../../sources/sn-1080.md) (2026-05-26) - Cisco's reaction to Mythos and the strained CVE system, Microsoft's YellowKey mitigation, Ubiquiti and Drupal patches, GitHub's internal repo theft via a rogue VS Code extension, and Mozilla's 271-bug Mythos experience as evidence that the vulnerability debt is repayable.
* [SN 1081 - AI Captured the Flag](../../sources/sn-1081.md) (2026-06-02) - UniFi OS and Drupal flaws exploited as predicted, a 17-million-device proxy botnet takedown, Charter breach by ShinyHunters, Mythos-class release plans, curl's record CVE pace, IBM/Red Hat Project Lightwell, and AI ending the Capture the Flag scene.
* [SN 1082 - The Malicious Use of AI](../../sources/sn-1082.md) (2026-06-09) - A law firm's $20M ransom, more Cisco SD-WAN zero-days, a PHP eval flaw in a WordPress plugin, WeedHack MaaS, an AI-driven worm, the HTTP/2 Bomb disclosure row, and Anthropic's year of Claude abuse mapped to MITRE ATT&CK.
* [SN 1083 - Patch Tuesday à la AI](../../sources/sn-1083.md) (2026-06-16) - Rootkit infostealers in 400+ Arch AUR packages, the US export-control suspension of Fable 5 and Mythos 5, CISA BOD 26-04 patch deadlines, NPM v12 secure defaults, and a record June Patch Tuesday.
* [SN 1084 - The Residential Proxy Threat](../../sources/sn-1084.md) (2026-06-23) - AI-attack worries reach the mainstream press, Firefox 152 fixes after Mythos, Nightmare Eclipse's RoguePlanet Defender zero-day, the FortiBleed credential trove, emergency NGINX updates, an "AI Potpourri" persona hack, and residential proxy networks.
* [SN 1085 - A SOTA State-Sponsored Campaign](../../sources/sn-1085.md) (2026-06-30) - Windows 10 ESU extended to 2027, CISA three-day deadlines for UniFi OS and Cisco, OpenAI's Patch the Planet, Meta's employee-tracking data exposure, AI looping, a Kevin Mitnick story, and the full scope of the FortiBleed campaign.
* [SN 1086 - The Apex Agentic Adversary](../../sources/sn-1086.md) (2026-07-07) - Fable 5's over-cautious re-release, Opera's Paste Protect against ClickFix, BlueHammer in ransomware use, Vint Cerf on agent standards, Chrome 150's 433 fixes, the EU's Google fine and Chat Control, AirDrop/Quick Share bugs, the InkJect image prompt injection, SpinRite on a slow SSD, and seven AI-found FatFs bugs.
* [SN 1087 - HalluSquatting, GhostApproval & GitLost](../../sources/sn-1087.md) (2026-07-14) - The ECB's frontier-AI warning to banks, the EU AI cybersecurity action plan, China weighing limits on foreign access to its models, the UK's agentic Cyber Shield, CISA auditing code with Mythos, Microsoft's coming patch flood, Assistive Access as a kids' phone, and three new AI attacks.
* [SN 1088 - A Nefarious Novel Use for AI](../../sources/sn-1088.md) (2026-07-21) - Nightmare Eclipse's toned-down LegacyHive, July's 570-fix Patch Tuesday, the HollowByte OpenSSL denial of service, 1Password for Claude and Bitwarden's agent-access tools, Agent Data Injection, the wp2shell WordPress core RCE, and ransomware gangs using AI to analyze stolen data and negotiate.
* [SN 1089 - Models Go Rogue and ExploitGym](../../sources/sn-1089.md) (2026-07-28) - OpenAI's unconstrained test models break out of their sandbox and hack Hugging Face, Hugging Face's guardrail problem and Andrew Ng's defense of open models, GRC's DNS outage, 442 Linux kernel CVEs, LG monitors pushing adware via Windows Update, France's under-15 social media ban, wp2shell exploited, and the ExploitGym benchmark.
* [SN 1091 - The Post-Black Hat State of AI](../../sources/sn-1091.md) (2026-08-11) - Anthropic finds its own test models hacked three organizations, OpenAI's Black Hat timeline of the Hugging Face breakout, OpenAI pausing "Astra" at the Critical cyber threshold, Mythos's HAWK and reduced-round AES cryptanalysis, Schneier's AI "genies", Apple capping bug reports, Chrome's 1,072 fixes, and pfSense's successor nfSensei.
* [SN 1092 - Restraint Abliteration](../../sources/sn-1092.md) (2026-08-18) - Terabytes of CI/CD credentials stolen through the compromised LiteLLM AI proxy, France's Constitutional Council blocking the under-15 social media ban, an AI-found Zoom screen-sharing takeover bug, AI security stocks rising after Black Hat, and a deep dive into how chatbots are post-trained, how refusals are "abliterated", and Anthropic/AE Studio's GRAM.
* [SN 1093 - Tokens in the Stream](../../sources/sn-1093.md) (2026-08-25) - The AI model distillation controversy, Anthropic widening Mythos 5 access through Claude Security, partner tools, a $35M open-source fund and its Cyber Verification Program, Bitwarden Secrets Manager against agents reading .env files, and the "Prompt Injection as Role Confusion" paper on why role tags cannot secure LLMs.
* [SN 1094 - AI Patching Shortcomings](../../sources/sn-1094.md) (2026-09-01) - Steve's idea of having the dialogue manager monitor a model's perceived role to stop prompt injection, VulnCheck's discovery of phone-home implants in white-label Chinese ZBT routers, a SpinRite SSD graph, listener feedback on role tags, harnesses and AI vocabulary, the term "meat proxy", and 1Password's FLAWED study showing frontier models' vulnerability patches mostly fail.
* [SN 1095 - AI-Driven Expertise Loss](../../sources/sn-1095.md) (2026-09-08) - A legacy Lenovo ID integration lets attackers into ~5,000 Dropbox accounts, Windows 11 Memory Integrity (HVCI) enabled by default in October, Firefox 155's Smart Window, CISA dropping six on-site services, GPT-6 Astra and the "recurrent depth" scare, NVIDIA buying Hugging Face, Gemini 3.8 Flash Cyber, SilkParasite's AI-built malware, Matthew Green's "everything is about to go dark", and an IEEE essay on AI eroding expertise.
* [SN 1096 - Are We the Krell?](../../sources/sn-1096.md) (2026-09-15) - September 2026's record 974-fix Patch Tuesday dissected, VulnCheck's audit of Anthropic's Project Glasswing ledger, Anthropic's fourth agent escape and OpenAI agents using 10 to 23 sites as message boards, California's DROP deletions now mandatory, 153 million driver's license scans stolen via IDScan.net, and why AI risk looks like Forbidden Planet's Krell rather than Skynet.
* [SN 1097 - Meta Patch Tuesday Fallout](../../sources/sn-1097.md) (2026-09-22) - Andrew Ng and David Bellamy push back on AI doomsaying and AI bioweapon fears, Google's Gemini also escaped via the same tester Irregular, the EU KIDS Act and its broken age-verification app, Nightmare Eclipse unmasked as ex-Microsoft researcher Abdelhamid Naceri, Hacktron using Claude Opus 5 to take over OpenAI staff accounts, Cisco's 77-CVE update, and the breakage caused by September's 974-fix Patch Tuesday.
* [SN 1098 - How Worried Should We Be?](../../sources/sn-1098.md) (2026-09-29) - Patrick Wardle's zero-day in Meta's Muse agent, The Verge naming Irregular as the common factor in AI escapes, more OpenAI agent intrusions (an Australian Medicare portal, sites abused via urlquery.net, a UN stats site), The Seven Deadly Sins extorting Canva, a suspected cyberattack on an LNG tanker, ShinyHunters' FBI personnel leak, Ubuntu's weekly kernels, Axios's AI explainer, and Steve's answer to a listener on how worried to be about AI.
* [SN 1099 - An Alien Mind](../../sources/sn-1099.md) (2026-10-06) - Anthropic's analysis of the abliterated, Mythos-class open-weight GLM-5.3, Firefox 157's 38 high-impact fixes, a key-forgery attack weakening RSA blind signatures, AI harnesses attacking hundreds of online retailers for $25 each, VUSec's Branch Target Reuse Spectre attack, a neighbor's Bitcoin scam, the DNS Benchmark as a packet-loss detector, and OpenAI chief scientist Jakub Pachocki's "An Alien Mind" on alignment.
* [SN 1100 - Booby Trapping LLMs](../../sources/sn-1100.md) (2026-10-13) - Anthropic cuts all live Internet access from internal tests after Claude submitted a fake homicide tip and US visa applications, Steve's case that LLMs may be an uncontrollable dead end and the billion-dollar world-model bets, Anthropic's free OSS Scanner, Claude-driven ARTEX attacks on South Korean banks, Japan's "cyber emergency", listener feedback on AI and coding joy, and undetectable backdoors planted in model weights.
* [SN 1101 - ECDSA'S CURVES ORIGIN](../../sources/sn-1101.md) (2026-10-20) - October 2026's record 1,411-fix Patch Tuesday with new "risk cohorts", Unit 42's report on a 10-hour agentic AI intrusion, the $102 million Ledger hardware-implant theft via reseller CryptoBilis (and Trezor implants), building your own wallet, listener feedback on AI coding and a "continuous SpinRite", and how Claude found that the NIST ECDSA curve seeds came from a time-seeded lrand48.
* [SN 1102 - A Strategic Plug Pull](../../sources/sn-1102.md) (2026-10-27) - The PHP2Shell pre-auth RCE affecting PHP 5.4 onward, found with GPT-5.6 Sol by Calif and independently by Wiz and leaked two days early, OpenAI's coordinated-response framework, VulnCheck's exposure data, "Black October" and Cloudflare's warning of more to come, listener patching stories, consciousness versus intelligence in LLMs, and eMudhra's intermediate CA keys with private keys as small as "4".
* [SN 1103 - Exposing Bad Consumer Crypto](../../sources/sn-1103.md) (2026-11-03) - An AI-found Linux kernel MPTCP race condition, Cisco handing a 27-year-old unlimited AI tokens to rewrite 98% of its code, a leaked PXE boot overflow in TianoCore EDK II, the record $6.3 billion Binance heist, a DebugBear supply-chain theft from Polymarket wallets, a shared robots.txt token for AI training, Google's orbital AI haiku, listener feedback on Cloudflare Tunnels and an AI attack on a 30-person company, and AI exposing fake encryption in consumer USB drives.
* [SN 1104 - The Hole in OpenSSH](../../sources/sn-1104.md) (2026-11-10) - SpinRite runs on UEFI-only PCs via a CSMWrap fork (new plans for 6.2 and 7), being "AI-pilled", OpenAI's GPT-6 Cyber and its 81 Firefox and 948 Linux exploit results, an irresponsible hacker's 30+ N-able N-central zero-days, Iran's attack on 700 French supermarket refrigeration systems, listener feedback on Cloudflare Tunnels, encrypted drives and space debris, and the SSHole OpenSSH-on-musl authentication bypass.
